# More adjectives: T–U

> Adjectives T–U beyond the grammar groups — the everyday adjectives that a triage of WordNet’s adjective lemmas showed a learner needs.

[← The grammar of adjectives](../README.md) &middot; [All groups](README.md)

Most English adjectives behave: they go before a noun or after *be*, and they compare with *-er* or *more*. These are those — but each still says how it compares, where it can stand, and what it takes. WordNet lists about twenty thousand adjectives; most of the rest are technical, archaic or British-only, and a triage pass left them out on purpose.

---
### taboo

**Pronunciation:** /təˈbuː/ &middot; *tuh-BOO*
**Русский:** запретный, табуированный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — *a taboo subject*, *Money talk is taboo here*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *breaking a taboo*. The stress is on the second syllable: tuh-BOO.

Not allowed to be talked about or done, by custom.

1. Money is still a *taboo* subject in many families.
2. In his house, politics at dinner was *taboo*.
3. The play deals with *taboo* topics.

---

### tacit

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæsɪt/ &middot; *TAS-it*
**Русский:** молчаливый, негласный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *tacit approval*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from Latin *tacere*, to be silent
**Opposite:** *explicit*, *stated*
**Contrast:** *Tacit* means understood without being said. It has nothing to do with *tact* — that word gives *tactful* and *tactless*.

Understood or agreed to without being said out loud.

1. The plan had the boss's *tacit* approval.
2. There was a *tacit* understanding that nobody would mention it.
3. Silence here counts as *tacit* consent.

---

### taciturn

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæsɪtɜːrn/ &middot; *TAS-i-turn*
**Русский:** молчаливый, неразговорчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a taciturn man*, *He gets taciturn*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more taciturn*, *most taciturn*
**Opposite:** *talkative*

Saying very little by nature.

1. Her father was a *taciturn* man who answered in one word.
2. He gets *taciturn* when he's working something out.
3. The two *taciturn* brothers ran the farm together.

---

### tacky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæki/ &middot; *TAK-ee*
**Русский:** безвкусный, пошлый; липкий
**Type:** evaluative; also quality
**Position:** both — *a tacky souvenir*, *The paint is still tacky*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tackier*, *tackiest*

Tacky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cheap-looking and in poor taste.** (безвкусный, пошлый)

1. The lobby is full of *tacky* gold furniture.
2. It felt *tacky* to bring up money at the funeral.
3. Every year their holiday sweaters get *tackier*.

**2. Slightly sticky, like paint that isn't dry yet.** (липкий, ещё не высохший)

4. The varnish is still *tacky*.
5. The floor felt *tacky* under our shoes.

---

### tactful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæktfəl/ &middot; *TAKT-fuhl*
**Русский:** тактичный, деликатный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both — *a tactful answer*, *She was tactful about it*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tactful*, *most tactful*
**Takes:** *about* + noun (the touchy subject)
**Form:** -ful from *tact*
**Opposite:** *tactless*

Careful not to say the thing that would hurt or embarrass someone.

1. She found a *tactful* way to say no.
2. He's honest but never *tactful*.
3. It wasn't *tactful* to bring up his ex.

---

### tactical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæktɪkəl/ &middot; *TAK-ti-kuhl*
**Русский:** тактический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a tactical error*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *tactic*
**Contrast:** *Tactical* is about the short-term moves; *strategic* is about the long-term plan: *a tactical retreat* serves *a strategic goal*.

Tactical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with the moves you make to reach a goal.** (тактический)

1. Pulling out of the deal was a *tactical* mistake.
2. The coach made a *tactical* decision to rest him.

**2. Used on the battlefield, at short range — of weapons and units.** (тактический (о вооружении))

3. The unit trains for *tactical* operations in cities.
4. *Tactical* nuclear weapons have a short range.

---

### tactile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæktəl/ &middot; *TAK-tuhl*
**Русский:** осязательный, тактильный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *tactile feedback*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also said TAK-tyl. American *-ile* words vary: *fragile* is FRAJ-uhl, *mobile* is MOH-buhl.

Tactile has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with the sense of touch.** (осязательный, тактильный)

1. Babies learn through *tactile* exploration.
2. A glass screen gives you no *tactile* feedback.

**2. Pleasant or interesting to touch.** (приятный на ощупь)

3. The book has a lovely *tactile* cover.
4. She likes *tactile* fabrics like velvet and raw wool.

---

### tactless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæktləs/ &middot; *TAKT-luhs*
**Русский:** бестактный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a tactless remark*, *That was tactless*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tactless*, *most tactless*
**Form:** -less from *tact*
**Opposite:** *tactful*

Saying the wrong thing without thinking how it will land.

1. That was a *tactless* thing to say to a new widow.
2. He isn't cruel, just *tactless*.
3. Her *tactless* question silenced the table.

---

### Tahitian

**Pronunciation:** /təˈhiːʃən/ &middot; *tuh-HEE-shuhn*
**Русский:** таитянский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *Tahitian pearls*, *Her mother is Tahitian*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Tahiti*

Of or from Tahiti, its people or its language.

1. We stayed in a *Tahitian* village for a week.
2. She collects *Tahitian* pearls.
3. The hotel serves a *Tahitian* breakfast of fruit and coconut.

---

### tailor-made

**Pronunciation:** /ˌteɪlərˈmeɪd/ &middot; *tay-lur-MAYD*
**Русский:** сшитый на заказ; идеально подходящий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — *a tailor-made suit*, *The job is tailor-made for her*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *for* + noun or person (who it suits)
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle
**Opposite:** *ready-made*, *off-the-rack*

Tailor-made has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made to fit one particular person, of clothes.** (сшитый на заказ)

1. He wore a *tailor-made* suit to the hearing.
2. Her winter coat is *tailor-made*.

**2. Perfectly suited to a person or a purpose.** (будто созданный, идеально подходящий)

3. The job is *tailor-made* for her.
4. It was a *tailor-made* excuse to leave early.

---

### tailored

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪlərd/ &middot; *TAY-lurd*
**Русский:** приталенный; подогнанный под нужды
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a tailored jacket*, *The course is tailored to beginners*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun (whose needs it fits)
**Form:** -ed from *tailor*

Tailored has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cut to fit closely and neatly, of clothes.** (приталенный, хорошо сидящий)

1. She wore a *tailored* gray jacket.
2. The pants are *tailored* and a little too tight.

**2. Made or changed to fit a particular need.** (подогнанный, адаптированный)

3. The lessons are *tailored* to each student.
4. We offer plans *tailored* to small businesses.

---

### tainted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪntɪd/ &middot; *TAYN-tid*
**Русский:** заражённый, испорченный; запятнанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *tainted meat*, *The evidence was tainted*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *taint*

Tainted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Spoiled or made unsafe by something harmful in it.** (заражённый, испорченный)

1. Dozens of people got sick from *tainted* lettuce.
2. The well water was *tainted* with gasoline.

**2. Damaged in its reputation or fairness.** (запятнанный, скомпрометированный)

3. The verdict was *tainted* by the leaked photos.
4. He left office with a *tainted* record.

---

### Taiwanese

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtaɪwɑːˈniːz/ &middot; *ty-wah-NEEZ*
**Русский:** тайваньский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *Taiwanese food*, *The company is Taiwanese*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ese from *Taiwan*
**Contrast:** Like all *-ese* words it adds no *-s* for people: *three Taiwanese engineers*. For one person say *a Taiwanese woman*, not *a Taiwanese*.

Of or from Taiwan, its people or its culture.

1. We had *Taiwanese* beef noodle soup.
2. The chip company is *Taiwanese*.
3. *Taiwanese* students make up a big part of the program.

---

### take-home

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪkˌhoʊm/ &middot; *TAYK-hohm*
**Русский:** чистый (о зарплате); на дом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *take-home pay*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — verb + adverb

Take-home has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Left over after taxes are taken out, of pay.** (чистый, на руки)

1. My *take-home* pay is about two-thirds of that number.
2. The raise barely changed her *take-home* amount.

**2. Meant to be taken away and used or done at home.** (на дом, с собой)

3. The final is a *take-home* exam.
4. The *take-home* message is simple: start early.

---

### taken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪkən/ &middot; *TAY-kuhn*
**Русский:** занятый; увлечённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb: *That seat is taken*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *very taken with* is common
**Takes:** *with* or *by* + noun (what charmed you)
**Form:** past participle of *take*
**Contrast:** Of a person, *He's taken* means he is already in a relationship.

Taken has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Already used, occupied or claimed.** (занятый)

1. Sorry, this seat is *taken*.
2. Every decent username was already *taken*.

**2. Charmed or impressed by someone or something.** (очарованный, увлечённый)

3. She was quite *taken* with the little house.
4. I'm not *taken* with the new design.

---

### takeout

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪkˌaʊt/ &middot; *TAYK-owt*
**Русский:** на вынос
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *takeout food*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *Let's get takeout tonight*. The British say *takeaway*. Written as one word for the adjective, two for the verb: *take out the trash*.

Sold to be carried away and eaten somewhere else.

1. The fridge is full of *takeout* containers.
2. We ordered *takeout* pizza and watched a movie.
3. The place has a *takeout* window on the side.

---

### talented

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæləntɪd/ &middot; *TAL-uhn-tid*
**Русский:** талантливый
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both — *a talented singer*, *The cast is talented*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more talented*, *most talented*
**Takes:** *at* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ed from *talent*
**Opposite:** *talentless*

Having a natural ability to do something well.

1. She's a *talented* cellist.
2. He's the most *talented* kid in the program.
3. The cast is young and *talented*.

---

### talentless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæləntləs/ &middot; *TAL-uhnt-luhs*
**Русский:** бездарный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a talentless hack*, *The band is talentless*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more talentless*, *most talentless*
**Form:** -less from *talent*
**Opposite:** *talented*

Having no skill or ability at all.

1. The critics called the band *talentless*.
2. He isn't *talentless* — he just never practices.
3. It's a *talentless* remake of a good movie.

---

### talkative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɔːkətɪv/ &middot; *TAW-kuh-tiv*
**Русский:** разговорчивый, болтливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a talkative kid*, *He gets talkative*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more talkative*, *most talkative*
**Form:** -ative from *talk*
**Opposite:** *quiet*, *taciturn*
**Contrast:** *Talkative* is neutral, *chatty* is warm, *long-winded* is the unkind one.

Talking a lot, or liking to talk.

1. He gets *talkative* after one beer.
2. She's a *talkative* kid who narrates everything she does.
3. The driver wasn't *talkative*, which was fine with me.

---

### tall

**Pronunciation:** /tɔːl/ &middot; *TAWL*
**Русский:** высокий
**Type:** size
**Position:** both — *a tall building*, *He's tall*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *taller*, *tallest*
**Opposite:** *short*
**Contrast:** *Tall* is for people, trees, buildings and other narrow things; mountains and flying planes are *high*. With a measurement the adjective comes last: *six feet tall*, never *tall six feet*.

Tall has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of more than average height.** (высокий, рослый)

1. Her brother is very *tall*.
2. They planted *tall* grass along the fence.
3. He's the *tallest* player on the team.

**2. Hard to believe or hard to do — in fixed phrases.** (невероятный; непосильный)

4. Getting there by six is a *tall* order.
5. Grandpa told us another *tall* tale about the war.

---

### tame

**Pronunciation:** /teɪm/ &middot; *TAYM*
**Русский:** ручной, прирученный; пресный, вялый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a tame rabbit*, *The movie is pretty tame*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tamer*, *tamest*
**Opposite:** *wild*
**Contrast:** *Tame* is also a verb: *They tamed the horse in a week*.

Tame has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not wild — used to people and not dangerous.** (ручной, прирученный)

1. The deer in the park are almost *tame*.
2. She kept a *tame* crow that rode on her shoulder.

**2. Milder or duller than you expected.** (пресный, безобидный)

3. The movie is pretty *tame* for an R rating.
4. The party was *tamer* than last year.

---

### Tamil

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæməl/ &middot; *TAM-uhl*
**Русский:** тамильский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *a Tamil family*, *Her husband is Tamil*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized, and the same word names the people and the language: *Tamil food*, *She speaks Tamil*.

Of the Tamil people of southern India and Sri Lanka, or their language.

1. He grew up in a *Tamil* neighborhood in Chennai.
2. The restaurant serves *Tamil* food.
3. *Tamil* movies are big business.

---

### tan

**Pronunciation:** /tæn/ &middot; *TAN*
**Русский:** жёлто-коричневый; загорелый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both — *tan pants*, *She's really tan*
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *tanner*, *tannest*, or *more tan*, *most tan*
**Contrast:** Double the *n*: *tanner*, *tannest*. *Tan* is also a verb (*I burn, I don't tan*) and a noun (*She came back with a great tan*).

Tan has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a light yellowish-brown color.** (светло-коричневый, песочный)

1. He wore *tan* khakis and a blue shirt.
2. They picked a *tan* carpet for the hallway.

**2. Having skin darkened by the sun.** (загорелый)

3. She came back from Florida *tan* and rested.
4. He's *tanner* now than he was in May.

---

### tangential

**Pronunciation:** /tænˈdʒɛnʃəl/ &middot; *tan-JEN-shuhl*
**Русский:** побочный, не относящийся к делу
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb, with *to*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tangential*, *most tangential*
**Takes:** *to* + noun (the main subject)
**Form:** -al from *tangent*

Only slightly connected to the main subject.

1. That point is *tangential* to what we're discussing.
2. He kept going off on *tangential* stories about his cousin.
3. Her role in the project was *tangential* at best.

---

### tangible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtændʒəbəl/ &middot; *TAN-juh-buhl*
**Русский:** осязаемый, ощутимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *tangible results*, *The benefit is tangible*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tangible*, *most tangible*
**Form:** -ible from Latin *tangere*, to touch
**Opposite:** *intangible*

Tangible has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Real and clear enough to be seen, measured or pointed to.** (ощутимый, реальный)

1. We need *tangible* results by June.
2. There's no *tangible* evidence of a leak.

**2. Able to be touched.** (осязаемый)

3. The tension in the room was almost *tangible*.
4. Cash in your hand is *tangible* in a way a number on a screen isn't.

---

### tangled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæŋɡəld/ &middot; *TANG-guhld*
**Русский:** запутанный, спутанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *tangled hair*, *The cords got tangled*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tangled*, *most tangled*
**Form:** -ed from *tangle*

Tangled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Twisted together into a knot or a mess.** (спутанный, запутавшийся)

1. Her hair was *tangled* from the wind.
2. The headphones came out of my bag *tangled* again.

**2. Confusingly complicated.** (запутанный, сложный)

3. It's a *tangled* story with too many people in it.
4. The company sits behind a *tangled* web of subsidiaries.

---

### tangy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæŋi/ &middot; *TANG-ee*
**Русский:** терпкий, с кислинкой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a tangy sauce*, *The dressing is tangy*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tangier*, *tangiest*
**Form:** -y from *tang*

Having a strong, pleasantly sharp or sour taste or smell.

1. The sauce is sweet and *tangy*.
2. I like a *tangy* goat cheese on that salad.
3. This lemonade is *tangier* than the last batch.

---

### tanned

**Pronunciation:** /tænd/ &middot; *TAND*
**Русский:** загорелый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *tanned arms*, *He came back tanned*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tanned*, *most tanned*
**Form:** -ed from *tan*
**Contrast:** *Tanned* and *tan* both describe sun-darkened skin, and Americans often just say *tan*: *She's very tan*. Leather is *tanned* too — that is the older meaning.

Having skin made browner by the sun.

1. He came back from Greece *tanned* and relaxed.
2. Her *tanned* arms stood out against the white shirt.
3. Everyone on the boat was *tanned* by the second day.

---

### tantalizing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæntəlaɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *TAN-tuh-ly-zing*
**Русский:** дразнящий, соблазнительный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both — *a tantalizing hint*, *The smell was tantalizing*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tantalizing*, *most tantalizing*
**Form:** -ing from *tantalize*, after King Tantalus
**Contrast:** American spelling is *-izing*; *tantalising* is British.

Attractive in a way that makes you want something you can't quite have.

1. The smell from the kitchen was *tantalizing*.
2. She dropped a *tantalizing* hint about the ending.
3. The offer was *tantalizing*, but the timing was wrong.

---

### tantamount

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæntəmaʊnt/ &middot; *TAN-tuh-mownt*
**Русский:** равносильный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb, always with *to*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun or *-ing*
**Preposition patterns:** [tantamount to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#tantamount-to)

Equal in effect to something bad, even if it isn't called that.

1. Saying nothing here is *tantamount* to agreeing.
2. The memo was *tantamount* to an admission of guilt.
3. Refusing the test is *tantamount* to a confession.

---

### Tanzanian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtænzəˈniːən/ &middot; *tan-zuh-NEE-uhn*
**Русский:** танзанийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *the Tanzanian coast*, *Our guide is Tanzanian*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Tanzania*

Of or from Tanzania or its people.

1. The *Tanzanian* coast is beautiful in February.
2. Our guide was *Tanzanian*.
3. She works with a *Tanzanian* coffee cooperative.

---

### Taoist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdaʊɪst/ &middot; *DOW-ist*
**Русский:** даосский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *a Taoist temple*, *His teacher is Taoist*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ist from *Tao*
**Contrast:** Usually said with a *d*: DOW-ist, rhyming with *now*; TOW-ist is also heard. The newer spelling is *Daoist*, and the same word names a follower.

Of Taoism, the Chinese philosophy and religion, or its followers.

1. We visited a *Taoist* temple outside the city.
2. He studies *Taoist* philosophy.
3. The book explains *Taoist* ideas about nature.

---

### tapered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪpərd/ &middot; *TAY-purd*
**Русский:** сужающийся, конусообразный
**Type:** participial; also shape
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *tapered jeans*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *taper*

Getting narrower toward one end.

1. He wears *tapered* jeans now.
2. The candle has a *tapered* top.
3. The legs of the table are gently *tapered*.

---

### tardy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɑːrdi/ &middot; *TAR-dee*
**Русский:** опоздавший; запоздалый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a tardy student*, *He was tardy again*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tardier*, *tardiest*
**Opposite:** *prompt*, *punctual*
**Contrast:** In American schools it is the official word for late, and it is even a noun there: *three tardies and you get detention*.

Tardy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Arriving later than you should.** (опоздавший)

1. He was *tardy* twice this week.
2. *Tardy* students have to check in at the office.

**2. Coming later than it should have.** (запоздалый)

3. The city's response was *tardy* and half-hearted.
4. Her thank-you notes get *tardier* every year.

---

### tart

**Pronunciation:** /tɑːrt/ &middot; *TART*
**Русский:** кислый, терпкий; язвительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a tart apple*, *These cherries are tart*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tarter*, *tartest*
**Contrast:** *Tart* is also a noun — a small open fruit pie.

Tart has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Pleasantly sour in taste.** (кислый, с кислинкой)

1. These cherries are *tart*.
2. A *tart* apple works better in a pie.
3. The green ones are *tarter* than the red.

**2. Sharp and a little unkind, of a remark or a tone.** (язвительный, колкий)

4. She gave a *tart* reply and hung up.
5. His reviews are famous for their *tart* humor.

---

### Tasmanian

**Pronunciation:** /tæzˈmeɪniən/ &middot; *taz-MAY-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** тасманийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *the Tasmanian coast*, *Her mother is Tasmanian*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Tasmania*
**Contrast:** The *Tasmanian devil* is a real animal about the size of a small dog, not only a cartoon.

Of or from Tasmania, the island state of Australia.

1. The *Tasmanian* devil hunts at night.
2. We hiked along the *Tasmanian* coast.
3. Her mother is *Tasmanian*.

---

### tasseled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæsəld/ &middot; *TAS-uhld*
**Русский:** с кисточками
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *tasseled loafers*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *tassel*
**Contrast:** American spelling keeps one *l*: *tasseled*; British doubles it, *tasselled*.

Decorated with tassels.

1. He wore *tasseled* loafers with no socks.
2. A *tasseled* lamp shade sat on the piano.
3. The graduates threw their *tasseled* caps in the air.

---

### tasteful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪstfəl/ &middot; *TAYST-fuhl*
**Русский:** со вкусом сделанный, изысканный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a tasteful room*, *The tribute was tasteful*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tasteful*, *most tasteful*
**Form:** -ful from *taste*
**Opposite:** *tasteless*, *tacky*
**Contrast:** *Tasteful* is about good taste in style, never about flavor — food that tastes good is *tasty*.

Showing good judgment about style or about what is appropriate.

1. The apartment is small but *tasteful*.
2. They sent a *tasteful* arrangement of white flowers.
3. He made a *tasteful* joke about it, which is harder than it sounds.

---

### tasteless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪstləs/ &middot; *TAYST-luhs*
**Русский:** пошлый, бестактный; безвкусный (о еде)
**Type:** evaluative; also quality
**Position:** both — *a tasteless joke*, *The soup was tasteless*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tasteless*, *most tasteless*
**Form:** -less from *taste*
**Opposite:** *tasteful*; *tasty* (of food)

Tasteless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Offensive or in bad taste.** (пошлый, бестактный)

1. He told a *tasteless* joke about the accident.
2. The ad was widely called *tasteless*.

**2. Having no flavor at all.** (безвкусный, пресный)

3. The soup was warm and *tasteless*.
4. Hospital food is famously *tasteless*.

---

### tasty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈteɪsti/ &middot; *TAY-stee*
**Русский:** вкусный
**Type:** evaluative; also quality
**Position:** both — *a tasty sandwich*, *That was tasty*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tastier*, *tastiest*
**Form:** -y from *taste*
**Opposite:** *tasteless*, *bland*
**Contrast:** *Tasty* is about flavor; *tasteful* is about style. For Russian *вкусный* Americans most often just say *really good*: *This soup is really good*.

Having a good flavor.

1. That was a *tasty* sandwich.
2. She makes a *tasty* black bean soup.
3. The second batch was *tastier* than the first.

---

### tattered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtætərd/ &middot; *TAT-urd*
**Русский:** потрёпанный, изорванный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a tattered flag*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tattered*, *most tattered*
**Form:** -ed from *tatter*

Tattered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Old and torn.** (изорванный, потрёпанный)

1. He carried a *tattered* paperback everywhere.
2. The flag over the post office is *tattered*.

**2. Badly damaged — of a reputation or a relationship.** (подорванный, изрядно потрёпанный)

3. The deal left his reputation *tattered*.
4. They're trying to repair a *tattered* friendship.

---

### taupe

**Pronunciation:** /toʊp/ &middot; *TOHP*
**Русский:** серо-коричневый, тауп
**Type:** color
**Position:** both — *a taupe rug*, *The walls are taupe*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** One syllable, like *tope*: TOHP. It names the gray-brown between beige and gray, and it is also a noun: *a soft taupe*.

Of a grayish-brown color.

1. The walls in the hallway are *taupe*.
2. She picked a *taupe* rug for the bedroom.
3. *Taupe* curtains warm up a gray room.

---

### taut

**Pronunciation:** /tɔːt/ &middot; *TAWT*
**Русский:** натянутый, тугой; напряжённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a taut rope*, *Pull the line taut*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tauter*, *tautest*
**Opposite:** *slack*, *loose*
**Contrast:** *Taut* sounds exactly like *taught*, the past of *teach*: keep the rope *taut*; the class was *taught* by a substitute.

Taut has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Stretched tight.** (натянутый, тугой)

1. Pull the line *taut* and tie it off.
2. The tent ropes were *taut* in the wind.
3. Get the wire *tauter* or the fence will sag.

**2. Tense and tightly controlled — of a face, a mood or a story.** (напряжённый)

4. His face was *taut* with worry.
5. It's a *taut* ninety-minute thriller.

---

### tautological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɔːtəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *taw-tuh-LAH-ji-kuhl*
**Русский:** тавтологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a tautological definition*, *The argument is tautological*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *tautology*

Saying the same thing twice in different words, so that it adds nothing.

1. 'Free gift' is *tautological* — a gift is already free.
2. His definition is *tautological*: it uses the word it defines.
3. The whole argument turns out to be *tautological*.

---

### tawdry

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɔːdri/ &middot; *TAW-dree*
**Русский:** безвкусный, кричащий; неприглядный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *tawdry souvenirs*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tawdrier*, *tawdriest*

Tawdry has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cheap and showy.** (дешёвый и кричащий)

1. The casino is all *tawdry* gold and neon.
2. The shop sells *tawdry* souvenirs to tourists.

**2. Sordid — morally ugly and a little shabby.** (неприглядный, грязный)

3. The paper printed every *tawdry* detail.
4. The story got *tawdrier* with every new witness.

---

### tawny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɔːni/ &middot; *TAW-nee*
**Русский:** рыжевато-коричневый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both — *a tawny lion*, *The hills turn tawny*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tawnier*, *tawniest*

Of a warm brownish-orange color, like a lion's coat.

1. A *tawny* lion lay in the shade.
2. The hills turn *tawny* by August.
3. Her hair looked *tawnier* in the summer light.

---

### tax-exempt

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtæksɪɡˈzɛmpt/ &middot; *taks-ig-ZEMPT*
**Русский:** освобождённый от налогов
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both — *a tax-exempt charity*, *The foundation is tax-exempt*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *exempt*
**Contrast:** *Tax-exempt* describes the organization or income that the law excuses from tax; *tax-free* describes money or goods you pay no tax on: *a tax-exempt charity*, *tax-free shopping*.

Not required to pay tax, or not taxed.

1. The foundation is *tax-exempt*.
2. Interest on those bonds is *tax-exempt*.
3. Churches hold *tax-exempt* status.

---

### tax-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtæksˈfriː/ &middot; *taks-FREE*
**Русский:** не облагаемый налогом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — *a tax-free weekend*, *The withdrawal is tax-free*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-free*, meaning without
**Contrast:** In compounds *-free* means without, not at no cost: *tax-free* is без налога, not бесплатный. It is also an adverb: *You can take the money out tax-free*.

With no tax to pay on it.

1. The state has a *tax-free* weekend in August.
2. Withdrawals after age 59 are *tax-free*.
3. They advertise *tax-free* shopping for tourists.

---

### taxable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæksəbəl/ &middot; *TAK-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** облагаемый налогом
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *taxable income*, *The prize is taxable*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *tax*
**Opposite:** *tax-free*, *tax-exempt*

That tax must be paid on.

1. Your *taxable* income is what's left after deductions.
2. Tips are *taxable*.
3. The prize money is fully *taxable*.

---

### taxing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæksɪŋ/ &middot; *TAK-sing*
**Русский:** утомительный, изматывающий
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both — *a taxing schedule*, *The trip was taxing*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more taxing*, *most taxing*
**Form:** -ing from *tax*, in its older sense of to strain
**Contrast:** Nothing to do with taxes: *a taxing week* is a hard, tiring one.

Demanding a lot of effort or energy.

1. It was a *taxing* week for everyone.
2. The hike isn't *taxing*, just long.
3. She has a *taxing* job and two small kids.

---

### taxpaying

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtæksˌpeɪɪŋ/ &middot; *TAKS-pay-ing*
**Русский:** платящий налоги
**Type:** participial; also compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *taxpaying citizens*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing* participle
**Contrast:** Also written *tax-paying*. The person is a *taxpayer*, one word.

Paying taxes.

1. He says he speaks for ordinary *taxpaying* citizens.
2. *Taxpaying* businesses want a say in the plan.
3. They're hard-working, *taxpaying* people.

---

### teachable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtiːtʃəbəl/ &middot; *TEE-chuh-buhl*
**Русский:** обучаемый; поддающийся обучению
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a teachable player*, *The kid is teachable*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more teachable*, *most teachable*
**Form:** -able from *teach*

Teachable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Willing and able to learn.** (обучаемый, восприимчивый к учёбе)

1. He's raw but *teachable*.
2. Coaches look for *teachable* players.

**2. Able to be taught — of a skill, or of a moment that teaches something.** (которому можно научить)

3. Empathy is *teachable*, up to a point.
4. She turned the argument into a *teachable* moment.

---

### teal

**Pronunciation:** /tiːl/ &middot; *TEEL*
**Русский:** бирюзовый, сине-зелёный
**Type:** color
**Position:** both — *a teal sweater*; *the walls are teal*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from the name of the duck, which has a band of this color on its head

Of a dark blue-green color.

1. She painted the kitchen cabinets *teal*.
2. He showed up in a *teal* tie and gray pants.
3. The team's new uniforms are *teal* and black.

---

### tearful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪrfəl/ &middot; *TEER-fuhl*
**Русский:** заплаканный, слёзный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — *a tearful goodbye*; *she was tearful all morning*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tearful*, *most tearful*
**Form:** -ful from *tear*

Crying, or close to crying.

1. They said a *tearful* goodbye at the gate.
2. She got *tearful* when she talked about her father.
3. His speech was more *tearful* than anyone expected.

---

### teary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪri/ &middot; *TEER-ee*
**Русский:** со слезами на глазах, заплаканный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — *teary eyes*; *I always get teary at weddings*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more teary*, *most teary*
**Form:** -y from *tear*

Having tears in the eyes.

1. The ending of that movie makes me *teary* every time.
2. She looked up with *teary* eyes.
3. Dad got a little *teary* during the toast.

---

### teary-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɪriˈaɪd/ &middot; *TEER-ee-YD*
**Русский:** со слезами на глазах
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *she got teary-eyed*; before a noun: *a teary-eyed crowd*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *teary* + *eyed*, like *wide-eyed*, *blue-eyed*

With tears in the eyes, usually from strong feeling.

1. Half the room was *teary-eyed* by the end of the ceremony.
2. He gets *teary-eyed* whenever that song comes on.
3. A *teary-eyed* fan handed her a bouquet.

---

### teasing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtiːzɪŋ/ &middot; *TEE-zing*
**Русский:** поддразнивающий, шутливый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a teasing smile*; *his tone was teasing*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more teasing*, *most teasing*
**Form:** -ing from *tease*

Making fun of someone in a friendly, playful way.

1. She said it in a *teasing* voice, so nobody took it badly.
2. He gave his sister a *teasing* look.
3. Their whole conversation was light and *teasing*.

---

### technical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛknɪkəl/ &middot; *TEK-nih-kuhl*
**Русский:** технический; узкоспециальный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — *a technical problem*; *the report gets technical*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more technical*, *most technical*

Technical has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the machines, methods and skills of a particular field.** (технический)

1. The site is down because of a *technical* problem.
2. She has the *technical* skills for the job.
3. *Technical* support finally called me back.

**2. Needing special knowledge to follow.** (узкоспециальный, сложный для неспециалиста)

4. The paper gets very *technical* after page ten.
5. Can you explain it in less *technical* language?

**3. True only by a strict reading of the rules.** (формальный, по формальному признаку)

6. It was a *technical* violation, but the referee let it go.
7. He won the fight by *technical* knockout.

---

### technological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɛknəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *tek-nuh-LAH-jih-kuhl*
**Русский:** технологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *technological change*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *technology*

Relating to technology and the changes it brings.

1. *Technological* change has hit that industry hard.
2. The company made a big *technological* leap in two years.
3. We teach students to keep up with *technological* tools.

---

### tectonic

**Pronunciation:** /tɛkˈtɑːnɪk/ &middot; *tek-TAH-nik*
**Русский:** тектонический; коренной (о переменах)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *tectonic plates*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Tectonic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the huge plates that make up the earth's crust.** (тектонический)

1. The earthquake happened where two *tectonic* plates meet.
2. *Tectonic* activity built these mountains.
3. Geologists study *tectonic* movement along the fault.

**2. So large that it changes everything, like the movement of the earth.** (коренной, глубинный)

4. The election marked a *tectonic* shift in the country's politics.
5. Streaming caused a *tectonic* change in how music is sold.

---

### tedious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtiːdiəs/ &middot; *TEE-dee-uhs*
**Русский:** нудный, утомительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a tedious meeting*; *the drive was tedious*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tedious*, *most tedious*
**Opposite:** *interesting*

Boring because it is long and repetitive.

1. Copying the numbers by hand is *tedious* work.
2. The meeting was even more *tedious* than the last one.
3. It's a *tedious* drive, but the road is easy.

---

### teeming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtiːmɪŋ/ &middot; *TEE-ming*
**Русский:** кишащий, изобилующий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *teeming streets*; *the pond is teeming with frogs*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ing from *teem*, a verb almost never met without *with*

Full of living things that move around.

1. The pond is *teeming* with tadpoles in May.
2. We walked through the *teeming* streets of the old city.
3. The reef was *teeming* with fish.

---

### teen

**Pronunciation:** /tiːn/ &middot; *TEEN*
**Русский:** подростковый
**Type:** age
**Position:** attributive only — *teen movies*; after a verb say *a teenager* or *teenage*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** shortened from *teenage*
**Contrast:** *a teen* is also a noun, a person from 13 to 19; *her teens* are those years: *She learned to drive in her teens*.

Made for or done by people aged 13 to 19.

1. He writes *teen* novels that sell by the millions.
2. The magazine covers *teen* fashion.
3. *Teen* drivers pay more for insurance.

---

### teenage

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtiːneɪdʒ/ &middot; *TEEN-ayj*
**Русский:** подростковый
**Type:** age
**Position:** attributive only — *a teenage son*; after a verb say *is a teenager*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *teen* + *age*

Aged between 13 and 19, or belonging to that age.

1. They have two *teenage* sons.
2. *Teenage* boys eat an unbelievable amount of food.
3. She remembers her *teenage* years with mixed feelings.

---

### teenaged

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtiːneɪdʒd/ &middot; *TEEN-ayjd*
**Русский:** подростковый
**Type:** age
**Position:** attributive only — *their teenaged daughter*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed added to *teenage*
**Contrast:** *teenage* and *teenaged* mean exactly the same thing; *teenage* is much more common in American writing.

Aged between 13 and 19.

1. Their *teenaged* daughter watched the younger kids.
2. A group of *teenaged* volunteers cleaned up the park.
3. He was a *teenaged* runner with Olympic hopes.

---

### teensy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtiːnzi/ &middot; *TEEN-zee*
**Русский:** крошечный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both — *a teensy apartment*; *the portions are teensy*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *teensier*, *teensiest*
**Form:** a playful form of *teeny*, often doubled: *teensy-weensy*
**Contrast:** Very informal and often childlike; in writing use *tiny*.

Extremely small.

1. The soup needs a *teensy* bit more salt.
2. Her studio is even *teensier* than mine.
3. They served the cake in *teensy* squares.

---

### teeny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtiːni/ &middot; *TEE-nee*
**Русский:** крошечный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both — *a teeny bug*; *the print is teeny*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *teenier*, *teeniest*
**Form:** a playful form of *tiny*
**Opposite:** *huge*

Very small.

1. There's a *teeny* hole in this sock.
2. The print on the label is the *teeniest* I've ever seen.
3. She keeps a *teeny* notebook in her pocket.

---

### telepathic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɛləˈpæθɪk/ &middot; *tel-uh-PATH-ik*
**Русский:** телепатический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *telepathic powers*; *those twins are practically telepathic*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *telepathy*

Able to send or receive thoughts without speaking.

1. In the book the aliens are *telepathic*.
2. She claims she has *telepathic* powers.
3. Those two are so close they seem *telepathic*.

---

### telling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛlɪŋ/ &middot; *TEL-ing*
**Русский:** показательный, красноречивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a telling detail*; *his silence was telling*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more telling*, *most telling*
**Form:** -ing from *tell*, in the sense 'reveal'

Showing the truth about something without meaning to.

1. The most *telling* detail was the empty parking lot.
2. Her long pause was *telling*.
3. It's *telling* that nobody from management showed up.

---

### telltale

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛlteɪl/ &middot; *TEL-tayl*
**Русский:** выдающий, явный (о признаке)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the telltale signs*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *tell* + *tale*

Showing clearly that something is there, especially something hidden.

1. The *telltale* smell of smoke was coming from the kitchen.
2. Doctors look for the *telltale* signs of the disease.
3. There were *telltale* scratches around the lock.

---

### temperamental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɛmprəˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *tem-pruh-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** капризный, вспыльчивый; ненадёжный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a temperamental chef*; *the printer is temperamental*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more temperamental*, *most temperamental*
**Form:** -al from *temperament*
**Contrast:** Not the Russian 'темпераментный' in the sense of lively or passionate; in English it means moody and hard to deal with.

Temperamental has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Changing mood suddenly and often.** (капризный, неуравновешенный)

1. The chef is brilliant but *temperamental*.
2. He got more *temperamental* as the deadline got closer.
3. She's not *temperamental*; she just says what she thinks.

**2. Working well one day and badly the next.** (капризный, ненадёжный (о технике))

4. The old printer is *temperamental* in humid weather.
5. Our car is *temperamental* on cold mornings.

---

### temperate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛmpərət/ &middot; *TEM-pur-uht*
**Русский:** умеренный (о климате); сдержанный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — *a temperate climate*; *the winters here are temperate*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more temperate*, *most temperate*
**Contrast:** Don't confuse it with *temporary*, which is about time, or with *temperamental*.

Temperate has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Never very hot and never very cold.** (умеренный (о климате))

1. Oregon has a *temperate* climate near the coast.
2. These plants grow best in *temperate* regions.
3. Winters in the valley are more *temperate* than in the mountains.

**2. Calm and controlled in what you say or do.** (сдержанный, умеренный)

4. Her response to the criticism was *temperate* and careful.
5. He is *temperate* in everything, from food to spending.

---

### tempered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛmpərd/ &middot; *TEM-purd*
**Русский:** закалённый; смягчённый, умеренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *tempered glass*; *the glass is tempered*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *temper*
**Contrast:** Most often met inside compounds: *even-tempered*, *bad-tempered*, *hot-tempered*, where it means 'having that kind of temper'.

Tempered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made harder and stronger by heating and then cooling.** (закалённый (о стекле, стали))

1. The shower door is made of *tempered* glass.
2. A *tempered* steel blade holds its edge.
3. Car windows are *tempered* so they break into small pieces.

**2. Made less extreme by something else.** (смягчённый, умеренный)

4. He gave a *tempered* response instead of firing back.
5. The report shows a *tempered* optimism about next year.

---

### tempestuous

**Pronunciation:** /tɛmˈpɛstʃuəs/ &middot; *tem-PES-choo-uhs*
**Русский:** бурный, неистовый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a tempestuous marriage*; *their friendship was tempestuous*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tempestuous*, *most tempestuous*
**Form:** -ous from *tempest*, an old word for a violent storm

Full of strong feeling, arguments and sudden changes.

1. They had a *tempestuous* marriage that lasted forty years.
2. The band's *tempestuous* early years are all in the documentary.
3. Her relationship with the director was more *tempestuous* than anyone knew.

---

### temporal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛmpərəl/ &middot; *TEM-pur-uhl*
**Русский:** временной; мирской, светский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the temporal order of events*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *temporal* means 'having to do with time'; *temporary* means 'lasting only a short while'. A *temporal* arrangement is one in time, not a short-term one.

Temporal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to time or to the order of events in time.** (временной, относящийся ко времени)

1. The novel plays with the *temporal* order of events.
2. We looked for a *temporal* link between the two accidents.
3. Physicists talk about spatial and *temporal* dimensions.

**2. Belonging to this world rather than to religion.** (мирской, светский)

4. The church lost most of its *temporal* power.
5. He drew a line between *temporal* and spiritual authority.

---

### temporary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛmpəˌrɛri/ &middot; *TEM-puh-rer-ee*
**Русский:** временный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a temporary job*; *the fix is temporary*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *permanent*
**Contrast:** Not the same as *temporal*, which means 'relating to time'.

Lasting for a limited time only.

1. She took a *temporary* job over the summer.
2. This is a *temporary* fix until the part arrives.
3. The road closure is *temporary*.

---

### tempting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛmptɪŋ/ &middot; *TEMP-ting*
**Русский:** заманчивый, соблазнительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a tempting offer*; *that sounds tempting*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tempting*, *most tempting*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive — *it's tempting to quit*
**Form:** -ing from *tempt* — what makes you want something

Making you want to have or do something.

1. They made him a *tempting* offer.
2. It's *tempting* to skip the meeting and go home.
3. A quiet weekend sounds more *tempting* every year.

---

### tenable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛnəbəl/ &middot; *TEN-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** состоятельный, обоснованный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *that position is no longer tenable*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tenable*, *most tenable*
**Form:** -able from Latin *tenere*, 'to hold'
**Opposite:** *untenable*
**Contrast:** The negative *untenable* is far more common: *an untenable position*.

Able to be defended against criticism or attack.

1. After the report came out, his story was barely *tenable*.
2. Is that reading of the law still *tenable*?
3. They looked for a more *tenable* compromise.

---

### tenacious

**Pronunciation:** /təˈneɪʃəs/ &middot; *tuh-NAY-shuhs*
**Русский:** упорный, цепкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a tenacious reporter*; *she is tenacious*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tenacious*, *most tenacious*

Tenacious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not giving up, even when something is hard.** (упорный, настойчивый)

1. She's the most *tenacious* reporter on the staff.
2. It took a *tenacious* defense to win that game.
3. He was *tenacious* about getting the records released.

**2. Holding on tightly and hard to get rid of.** (цепкий, стойкий)

4. The weed has a *tenacious* root system.
5. That cough is *tenacious* — it's been three weeks.

---

### tendentious

**Pronunciation:** /tɛnˈdɛnʃəs/ &middot; *ten-DEN-shuhs*
**Русский:** тенденциозный, предвзятый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a tendentious article*; *the summary is tendentious*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tendentious*, *most tendentious*
**Form:** -ous from *tendency*

Written or said to push one opinion, not to be fair.

1. The article is well written but deeply *tendentious*.
2. He gave a *tendentious* summary of what the study found.
3. Her second book is even more *tendentious* than the first.

---

### tender

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛndər/ &middot; *TEN-dur*
**Русский:** нежный; мягкий (о мясе); болезненный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a tender look*; *the meat is tender*
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *tenderer*, *tenderest*; *more tender*, *most tender*
**Opposite:** *tough* (meat); *harsh* (manner)
**Contrast:** *tender* is also a verb (*tender your resignation*) and a noun (*legal tender*). The phrase *at a tender age* means 'very young'.

Tender has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Gentle, kind and loving.** (нежный, ласковый)

1. He gave her a *tender* look across the table.
2. There's a *tender* moment near the end of the film.
3. She spoke to the child in a *tender* voice.

**2. Soft and easy to cut or chew.** (мягкий, нежный (о еде))

4. Cook the beans until they're *tender*.
5. This roast came out *more tender* than last time.

**3. Sore and painful when touched.** (болезненный, чувствительный)

6. My ankle is still *tender* where I twisted it.
7. The doctor asked if the area was *tender*.

---

### tenderized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛndəraɪzd/ &middot; *TEN-duh-ryzd*
**Русский:** отбитый, размягчённый (о мясе)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *tenderized steak*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *tenderize*, which comes from *tender*

Made softer by pounding, cutting or soaking in a marinade.

1. The recipe calls for *tenderized* cutlets.
2. He fried up a piece of *tenderized* steak.
3. The meat is already *tenderized*, so it cooks fast.

---

### tenfold

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛnfoʊld/ &middot; *TEN-fohld*
**Русский:** десятикратный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — *a tenfold increase*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *ten* + *-fold*, like *twofold*, *threefold*
**Contrast:** After a verb the same word is an adverb: *Sales rose tenfold* — not *were tenfold*.

Ten times as big or as many.

1. The city saw a *tenfold* increase in applications.
2. A *tenfold* jump in cases forced the schools to close.
3. They promised a *tenfold* return on the investment.

---

### tense

**Pronunciation:** /tɛns/ &middot; *TENS*
**Русский:** напряжённый
**Type:** emotion; also quality
**Position:** both — *a tense meeting*; *everyone was tense*
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *tenser*, *tensest*; *more tense*, *most tense*
**Opposite:** *relaxed*
**Contrast:** *tense* is also a noun in grammar: *the past tense*.

Tense has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Nervous and unable to relax.** (напряжённый, взвинченный)

1. She gets *tense* before every flight.
2. He looked *tense* all through dinner.
3. I was *more tense* about the interview than about the test.

**2. Full of worry, so that nobody feels comfortable.** (напряжённый (о ситуации))

4. It was a *tense* meeting, and it ran two hours.
5. The room went *tense* when he walked in.

**3. Stretched tight and stiff.** (напряжённый (о мышцах))

6. My shoulders are *tense* from driving all day.
7. Her jaw was *tense*.

---

### tentative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛntətɪv/ &middot; *TEN-tuh-tiv*
**Русский:** предварительный; неуверенный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — *a tentative date*; *the plan is tentative*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tentative*, *most tentative*
**Opposite:** *final* (plans); *confident* (manner)

Tentative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not final, and likely to change.** (предварительный, ориентировочный)

1. We have a *tentative* date for the wedding.
2. The two sides reached a *tentative* agreement.
3. The schedule is *tentative* until the permits come through.

**2. Done without confidence, as if you might stop at any moment.** (неуверенный, осторожный)

4. He took a few *tentative* steps on the bad knee.
5. Her answer was *tentative*, almost a question.

---

### tenuous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛnjuəs/ &middot; *TEN-yoo-uhs*
**Русский:** слабый, шаткий, призрачный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a tenuous connection*; *the link is tenuous*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tenuous*, *most tenuous*

So weak or thin that it could easily fail or be wrong.

1. The connection between the two cases is *tenuous* at best.
2. He has a *tenuous* hold on his job.
3. Their evidence looks more *tenuous* every week.

---

### tenured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛnjərd/ &middot; *TEN-yurd*
**Русский:** штатный, с постоянной должностью (в вузе)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a tenured professor*; *she is tenured*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the noun *tenure*, a permanent university post

Holding a permanent teaching position that cannot easily be taken away.

1. She became a *tenured* professor at thirty-six.
2. Only *tenured* faculty vote on that committee.
3. He's *tenured* now, so he can take the risky project.

---

### tepid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛpɪd/ &middot; *TEP-id*
**Русский:** тепловатый; прохладный (о реакции)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *tepid coffee*; *the water is tepid*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tepid*, *most tepid*

Tepid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Only slightly warm, when it should be hot or cold.** (тепловатый, чуть тёплый)

1. The coffee had gone *tepid*.
2. Wash the sweater in *tepid* water.
3. He drank the *tepid* soup without complaining.

**2. Showing very little interest or enthusiasm.** (прохладный, вялый)

4. The new album got a *tepid* review.
5. Her response to the idea was *tepid*.

---

### terminal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɜːrmɪnəl/ &middot; *TUR-muh-nuhl*
**Русский:** неизлечимый, смертельный; конечный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *terminal cancer*; *his illness is terminal*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *a terminal* is also a noun: an airport building or a computer screen.

Terminal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Certain to cause death, and in its last stage.** (неизлечимый, в последней стадии)

1. He was diagnosed with *terminal* cancer in March.
2. The hospice cares for *terminal* patients.
3. Her illness is *terminal*, but she is still working.

**2. At the end of a line, route or process.** (конечный, крайний)

4. The *terminal* station is two stops past downtown.
5. Cut the stem above the *terminal* bud.

---

### terrestrial

**Pronunciation:** /təˈrɛstriəl/ &middot; *tuh-RES-tree-uhl*
**Русский:** земной; наземный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *terrestrial animals*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from Latin *terra*, 'earth'
**Opposite:** *extraterrestrial*; *aquatic*

Terrestrial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Living on land rather than in water or in trees.** (наземный, сухопутный)

1. Tortoises are *terrestrial* animals.
2. The island has very few *terrestrial* predators.
3. These orchids are *terrestrial*, not the kind that grow on branches.

**2. Belonging to the earth rather than to space.** (земной)

4. Mars and Venus are *terrestrial* planets.
5. The signal turned out to have a *terrestrial* source.

---

### terrific

**Pronunciation:** /təˈrɪfɪk/ &middot; *tuh-RIF-ik*
**Русский:** отличный, потрясающий; огромный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a terrific dinner*; *the show was terrific*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more terrific*, *most terrific*
**Contrast:** A trap: although it comes from the same root as *terror*, *terrific* is a compliment. The frightening word is *terrifying*.

Terrific has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very good; excellent.** (отличный, замечательный)

1. She did a *terrific* job on the presentation.
2. We had a *terrific* time in Chicago.
3. That's a *terrific* idea.

**2. Very great in size, force or degree.** (огромный, страшной силы)

4. There was a *terrific* crash from the garage.
5. The engine puts out a *terrific* amount of heat.

---

### territorial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɛrəˈtɔːriəl/ &middot; *ter-uh-TOR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** территориальный; ревностно охраняющий своё
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — *a territorial dispute*; *cats are territorial*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more territorial*, *most territorial*
**Takes:** *about* + noun — *territorial about her desk*

Territorial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the land a country or state controls.** (территориальный)

1. The two countries have a long *territorial* dispute.
2. Fishing boats stayed outside *territorial* waters.
3. The treaty settled their *territorial* claims.

**2. Ready to defend your own space or area from others.** (ревностно оберегающий своё)

4. Male robins are very *territorial* in the spring.
5. He's *territorial* about the kitchen when he cooks.

---

### terror-stricken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛrərˌstrɪkən/ &middot; *TER-ur-strik-uhn*
**Русский:** объятый ужасом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — *terror-stricken passengers*; *the children were terror-stricken*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle, like *panic-stricken*, *grief-stricken*

Filled with sudden, overwhelming fear.

1. *Terror-stricken* passengers ran for the exits.
2. She stood *terror-stricken* in the doorway.
3. The dog was *terror-stricken* by the fireworks.

---

### terse

**Pronunciation:** /tɜːrs/ &middot; *TURS*
**Русский:** лаконичный, сухой, резкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a terse reply*; *his answer was terse*
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *terser*, *tersest*; *more terse*, *most terse*
**Contrast:** *terse* is short in a way that sounds unfriendly; *concise* is short in a way that is praised.

Using very few words, in a way that sounds cold or annoyed.

1. He sent a *terse* two-line reply.
2. Her second email was even *terser* than the first.
3. The coach was *terse* with reporters after the loss.

---

### tertiary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɜːrʃiˌɛri/ &middot; *TUR-shee-er-ee*
**Русский:** третичный, третьего уровня
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a tertiary source*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from Latin *tertius*, 'third'; the series is *primary*, *secondary*, *tertiary*

Third in order or importance, after the first two.

1. An encyclopedia is a *tertiary* source.
2. Patients are sent to a *tertiary* care center for surgery.
3. Tourism is a *tertiary* concern for the town budget.

---

### testicular

**Pronunciation:** /tɛˈstɪkjələr/ &middot; *tes-TIK-yuh-lur*
**Русский:** яичковый, тестикулярный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *testicular cancer*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ar from *testicle*

Relating to the testicles.

1. *Testicular* cancer is very treatable when it's caught early.
2. The clinic teaches men how to do a *testicular* self-exam.
3. He was treated for a *testicular* injury.

---

### testy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛsti/ &middot; *TES-tee*
**Русский:** раздражительный, вспыльчивый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — *a testy exchange*; *he gets testy when he's hungry*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *testier*, *testiest*

Easily annoyed and quick to answer sharply.

1. He gets *testy* when he hasn't eaten.
2. There was a *testy* exchange between the two senators.
3. She was *testier* than usual after the long flight.

---

### Teutonic

**Pronunciation:** /tuːˈtɑːnɪk/ &middot; *too-TAH-nik*
**Русский:** тевтонский, германский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *Teutonic efficiency*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *very Teutonic* when it means 'typical of Germans'
**Contrast:** Outside history books it is usually half-joking, about German thoroughness or seriousness.

Relating to the ancient Germanic peoples, or to Germans and what is seen as their character.

1. The trains run with *Teutonic* precision.
2. He studies the *Teutonic* tribes of the Roman period.
3. There's something very *Teutonic* about the way the office is organized.

---

### Texan

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛksən/ &middot; *TEK-suhn*
**Русский:** техасский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *Texan hospitality*; *his accent is unmistakably Texan*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *very Texan* when it means 'typical of Texas'
**Contrast:** *a Texan* is also a noun, a person from Texas. Before many nouns Americans use *Texas* itself: *Texas barbecue*, *the Texas heat*.

From the state of Texas, or typical of it.

1. We were met with *Texan* hospitality.
2. His accent is unmistakably *Texan*.
3. The menu is *Texan* down to the last brisket.

---

### textbook

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛkstbʊk/ &middot; *TEKST-buuk*
**Русский:** классический, образцовый (пример)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *a textbook example*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The noun *a textbook* is a book for a course. As an adjective the word always means 'exactly like the standard example', and it goes only before a noun.

Matching the standard example of something perfectly.

1. That was a *textbook* landing.
2. The case is a *textbook* example of how not to run a company.
3. She made a *textbook* tackle.

---

### textual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛkstʃuəl/ &middot; *TEKS-choo-uhl*
**Русский:** текстовый, текстуальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *textual evidence*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ual from *text*

Relating to a written text and its exact words.

1. Back up your claim with *textual* evidence.
2. The editor made a few small *textual* changes.
3. Scholars compared the *textual* differences between the two manuscripts.

---

### textured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɛkstʃərd/ &middot; *TEKS-churd*
**Русский:** рельефный, фактурный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a textured wall*; *the paper is textured*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more textured*, *most textured*
**Form:** -ed from the noun *texture*
**Opposite:** *smooth*

Textured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a surface you can feel, not flat and smooth.** (рельефный, с фактурой)

1. The invitations are printed on *textured* paper.
2. We put in a *textured* floor so it wouldn't be slippery.
3. The ceiling is *textured*, which makes it hard to paint.

**2. Made of many layers or details that are worth noticing.** (многослойный, богатый оттенками)

4. Her new album has a richer, more *textured* sound.
5. The novel gives us a *textured* picture of small-town life.

---

### Thai

**Pronunciation:** /taɪ/ &middot; *TY*
**Русский:** тайский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *Thai food*; *the recipe is Thai*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *very Thai* when it means 'typical of Thailand'
**Contrast:** *Thai* is also a noun: the language, and a person from Thailand.

From Thailand, or relating to Thailand and its people.

1. We ordered *Thai* food for dinner.
2. She's taking *Thai* cooking classes.
3. The dish is sweet, sour and hot, which is very *Thai*.

---

### thankless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθæŋkləs/ &middot; *THANGK-luhs*
**Русский:** неблагодарный (о деле, работе)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a thankless job*; *the work is thankless*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more thankless*, *most thankless*
**Form:** -less from *thank*
**Contrast:** It describes the work, not the person. A person who gives no thanks is *ungrateful*.

Hard to do and never appreciated by anyone.

1. Being the referee is a *thankless* job.
2. She took on the *thankless* task of sorting the files.
3. Cleaning up after a party is *thankless* work.

---

### theatrical

**Pronunciation:** /θiˈætrɪkəl/ &middot; *thee-AT-rih-kuhl*
**Русский:** театральный; наигранный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both — *a theatrical production*; *his reaction was theatrical*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more theatrical*, *most theatrical*

Theatrical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the theater and to acting.** (театральный)

1. The film got a *theatrical* release in June.
2. She works in *theatrical* lighting.
3. The school has a strong *theatrical* tradition.

**2. Exaggerated and done to be noticed.** (наигранный, показной)

4. He gave a *theatrical* sigh and left the room.
5. Her apology was *theatrical* and nobody believed it.

---

### thematic

**Pronunciation:** /θɪˈmætɪk/ &middot; *thih-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** тематический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *thematic units*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *theme*

Organized around a subject or theme, or relating to one.

1. The course is broken into *thematic* units.
2. There's a clear *thematic* link between the two novels.
3. The museum arranged the show in *thematic* rooms.

---

### theocratic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθiːəˈkrætɪk/ &middot; *thee-uh-KRAT-ik*
**Русский:** теократический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a theocratic state*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *theocracy*, rule by religious leaders

Governed by religious leaders or by religious law.

1. The country became a *theocratic* state after the revolution.
2. He compared the group's rules to a *theocratic* system.
3. *Theocratic* governments answer to clerics, not to voters.

---

### theological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθiːəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *thee-uh-LAH-jih-kuhl*
**Русский:** богословский, теологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a theological argument*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *theology*

Relating to the study of religion and of God.

1. He teaches at a *theological* seminary.
2. The split began as a *theological* argument about baptism.
3. She wrote her thesis on a *theological* question from the Middle Ages.

---

### theoretical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθiːəˈrɛtɪkəl/ &middot; *thee-uh-RET-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** теоретический; чисто умозрительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *theoretical physics*; *the risk is purely theoretical*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *practical*; *empirical*

Theoretical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Based on ideas and theory rather than on practice.** (теоретический)

1. She works in *theoretical* physics.
2. The course is *theoretical*, with no lab work.
3. There's a big gap between the *theoretical* model and the shop floor.

**2. Possible in principle but not happening in reality.** (умозрительный, гипотетический)

4. For most people the danger is purely *theoretical*.
5. He raised a *theoretical* objection that nobody could test.

---

### therapeutic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθɛrəˈpjuːtɪk/ &middot; *ther-uh-PYOO-tik*
**Русский:** лечебный; успокаивающий
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — *therapeutic exercises*; *I find gardening therapeutic*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more therapeutic*, *most therapeutic*
**Form:** -ic from *therapy*

Therapeutic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Used to treat an illness or injury.** (лечебный, терапевтический)

1. The drug has a narrow *therapeutic* range.
2. He was given *therapeutic* exercises for his back.
3. The state allows *therapeutic* use of the plant.

**2. Making you feel calmer and better, even if it isn't medicine.** (успокаивающий, целебный (для души))

4. I find weeding the garden oddly *therapeutic*.
5. A long walk is more *therapeutic* than an hour online.

---

### thermal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθɜːrməl/ &middot; *THUR-muhl*
**Русский:** тепловой, термический; тёплый (об одежде)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *thermal energy*, *thermal socks*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *thermals* is also a noun: warm underwear worn under your clothes.

Thermal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to heat.** (тепловой, термический)

1. The camera picks up the *thermal* signature of the engine.
2. The plant turns *thermal* energy into electricity.
3. We checked the windows for *thermal* leaks.

**2. Made to keep body heat in.** (тёплый, термо- (об одежде))

4. Pack *thermal* socks for the hike.
5. He sleeps in a *thermal* shirt all winter.

---

### thermonuclear

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθɜːrmoʊˈnuːkliər/ &middot; *thur-moh-NOO-klee-ur*
**Русский:** термоядерный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a thermonuclear weapon*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *thermo-* 'heat' + *nuclear*

Using the huge energy released when light atoms join at very high temperature.

1. The country tested its first *thermonuclear* device in 1952.
2. A *thermonuclear* weapon is far more powerful than an atomic bomb.
3. The sun runs on *thermonuclear* fusion.

---

### thick

**Pronunciation:** /θɪk/ &middot; *THIK*
**Русский:** толстый; густой; сильный (об акценте)
**Type:** size; also quality
**Position:** both — *a thick book*; *the walls are thick*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *thicker*, *thickest*
**Opposite:** *thin*
**Contrast:** For people and animals use *fat*, not *thick*: a *thick* person sounds like an insult meaning 'slow to understand'.

Thick has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Having a large distance between one surface and the opposite one.** (толстый)

1. He handed me a *thick* envelope.
2. The walls are two feet *thick*.
3. Use the *thicker* board for the shelf.

**2. Not flowing easily, like a heavy liquid.** (густой)

4. The gravy is too *thick* — add a little water.
5. She likes her milkshakes *thick*.

**3. Made of many things growing or packed close together.** (густой, плотный)

6. A *thick* fog rolled in off the bay.
7. He has *thick* dark hair.
8. We cut a path through the *thick* brush.

**4. Strong and easy to hear, of an accent.** (сильный, заметный (об акценте))

9. She speaks with a *thick* Boston accent.
10. His English is good, but the accent is *thick*.

---

### thick-skinned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθɪkˈskɪnd/ &middot; *thik-SKIND*
**Русский:** толстокожий, невосприимчивый к критике
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *you have to be thick-skinned*; also *a thick-skinned politician*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more thick-skinned*, *most thick-skinned*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *thin-skinned*

Not easily hurt by criticism or insults.

1. You have to be *thick-skinned* to work in this business.
2. He's *thick-skinned* enough to laugh off the comments.
3. Reporters need a *thick-skinned* editor.

---

### thieving

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθiːvɪŋ/ &middot; *THEE-ving*
**Русский:** вороватый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *those thieving raccoons*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *thieve*, an old verb from *thief*
**Contrast:** Informal and usually half-joking, most often about animals or about someone taking small things.

In the habit of stealing.

1. Those *thieving* raccoons got into the trash again.
2. He called his brother a *thieving* little rat.
3. *Thieving* gulls circled over the picnic tables.

---

### thin-skinned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθɪnˈskɪnd/ &middot; *thin-SKIND*
**Русский:** обидчивый, болезненно воспринимающий критику
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — *a thin-skinned boss*; *he's too thin-skinned for this job*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more thin-skinned*, *most thin-skinned*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *thick-skinned*

Easily hurt or offended by criticism.

1. He's too *thin-skinned* to read his own reviews.
2. A *thin-skinned* manager makes everyone careful.
3. She gets *thin-skinned* when she's tired.

---

### third-rate

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθɜːrdˈreɪt/ &middot; *thurd-RAYT*
**Русский:** третьесортный, никудышный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a third-rate hotel*; *the food was third-rate*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — the series is *first-rate*, *second-rate*, *third-rate*
**Opposite:** *first-rate*

Of very poor quality.

1. We stayed in a *third-rate* hotel near the airport.
2. The script is *third-rate*, but the acting saves it.
3. He gave a *third-rate* imitation of the original.

---

### third-year

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθɜːrdˈjɪr/ &middot; *thurd-YEER*
**Русский:** третьего курса, третьего года
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a third-year student*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — ordinal + noun, like *first-year*, *second-year*

In the third year of a course, a job or a program.

1. She's a *third-year* law student.
2. The hospital hired two *third-year* residents.
3. He's in his *third-year* slump as a starter.

---

### thirsty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθɜːrsti/ &middot; *THUR-stee*
**Русский:** испытывающий жажду; жаждущий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *I'm thirsty*; before a noun: *thirsty hikers*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *thirstier*, *thirstiest*
**Takes:** *for* + noun — figurative: *thirsty for power*
**Contrast:** English says *I'm thirsty*, never *I have thirst* — the state is an adjective, as with *hungry* and *tired*.

Thirsty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Needing or wanting something to drink.** (испытывающий жажду)

1. I'm *thirsty* — is there any water left?
2. The salty popcorn made everyone *thirstier*.
3. They handed out lemonade to the *thirsty* runners.

**2. Wanting something very much.** (жаждущий чего-либо)

4. The team was *thirsty* for a win after four losses.
5. He's *thirsty* for any news from home.

---

### thorny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθɔːrni/ &middot; *THOR-nee*
**Русский:** колючий; каверзный, щекотливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a thorny issue*; *the bush is thorny*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *thornier*, *thorniest*
**Form:** -y from *thorn*

Thorny has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Difficult and full of problems.** (каверзный, трудный)

1. Pensions are the *thorniest* issue in the negotiation.
2. They avoided the *thorny* question of who pays.
3. It's a *thorny* problem with no clean answer.

**2. Covered with sharp points that grow on a plant.** (колючий)

4. Wear gloves — the branches are *thorny*.
5. A *thorny* hedge runs along the fence.

---

### thorough

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθɜːroʊ/ &middot; *THUR-oh*
**Русский:** тщательный, основательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a thorough search*; *she is thorough*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more thorough*, *most thorough*
**Contrast:** Don't mix it up with *through* /θruː/, which is a preposition, or with *though*. *Thorough* has two syllables: THUR-oh.

Done carefully and completely, with nothing left out.

1. The police did a *thorough* search of the building.
2. She's the most *thorough* editor I've worked with.
3. Give the pan a *thorough* cleaning before you use it.

---

### thought-provoking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθɔːtprəˌvoʊkɪŋ/ &middot; *THAWT-pruh-voh-king*
**Русский:** заставляющий задуматься
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — *a thought-provoking film*; *the talk was thought-provoking*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more thought-provoking*, *most thought-provoking*
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing* participle

Making you think seriously about something.

1. It's a *thought-provoking* documentary about food waste.
2. She asked a *thought-provoking* question at the end.
3. The talk was short but *thought-provoking*.

---

### thoughtful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθɔːtfəl/ &middot; *THAWT-fuhl*
**Русский:** внимательный, заботливый; вдумчивый; задумчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a thoughtful gift*; *that was thoughtful of you*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more thoughtful*, *most thoughtful*
**Form:** -ful from *thought*
**Opposite:** *thoughtless*

Thoughtful has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Kind, and careful about what other people need.** (внимательный, заботливый)

1. That was a *thoughtful* gift.
2. It was *thoughtful* of you to call.
3. He's the most *thoughtful* person in the office.

**2. Careful and serious in thinking about something.** (вдумчивый, обстоятельный)

4. She gave a *thoughtful* answer to a hard question.
5. The report is *thoughtful* and well argued.

**3. Looking as if you are deep in thought.** (задумчивый)

6. He went quiet and *thoughtful* after the news.
7. There was a *thoughtful* look on her face.

---

### thoughtless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθɔːtləs/ &middot; *THAWT-luhs*
**Русский:** бездумный, невнимательный к другим
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — *a thoughtless remark*; *it was thoughtless of him*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more thoughtless*, *most thoughtless*
**Form:** -less from *thought*
**Opposite:** *thoughtful*

Not thinking about how your words or actions affect other people.

1. It was a *thoughtless* remark, but he didn't mean any harm.
2. Leaving the gate open was *thoughtless*.
3. She apologized for being *thoughtless* about the schedule.

---

### threadbare

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθrɛdbɛr/ &middot; *THRED-bair*
**Русский:** потёртый, изношенный; избитый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a threadbare rug*; *his coat was threadbare*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more threadbare*, *most threadbare*
**Form:** compound — *thread* + *bare*: worn until the threads show

Threadbare has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Worn so thin that the cloth is almost see-through.** (потёртый, вытертый)

1. He still wears that *threadbare* corduroy jacket.
2. The rug by the door is *threadbare*.
3. The hotel towels were *threadbare*.

**2. Used so often that it no longer convinces anyone.** (избитый, затасканный)

4. That excuse is getting *threadbare*.
5. He repeated the same *threadbare* argument.

---

### threatened

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθrɛtənd/ &middot; *THRET-uhnd*
**Русский:** находящийся под угрозой; чувствующий угрозу
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a threatened species*; *he felt threatened*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more threatened*, *most threatened*
**Takes:** *by* + noun — *threatened by the new rules*
**Contrast:** *threatened* is how the person or animal feels; *threatening* is what causes the fear.

Threatened has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In danger of being harmed or of dying out.** (находящийся под угрозой исчезновения)

1. The turtle is a *threatened* species in this state.
2. Small farms are *threatened* by rising costs.
3. Coastal towns are the most *threatened* by the storms.

**2. Feeling that someone may hurt you or take what is yours.** (чувствующий угрозу)

4. He gets *threatened* by anyone younger than he is.
5. She said she felt *threatened* during the call.

---

### threatening

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθrɛtənɪŋ/ &middot; *THRET-uh-ning*
**Русский:** угрожающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a threatening letter*; *his tone was threatening*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more threatening*, *most threatening*
**Form:** -ing from *threaten* — what causes the fear
**Contrast:** Compare *threatened*: *a threatening email* frightens people, *a threatened worker* is the one who is frightened.

Threatening has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Suggesting that harm is coming.** (угрожающий, грозящий)

1. She got a *threatening* letter and called the police.
2. His tone turned *threatening*.
3. The dog took a *threatening* step toward the fence.

**2. Looking as if a storm is about to start.** (грозовой, предвещающий непогоду)

4. The sky looked *threatening* all afternoon.
5. *Threatening* clouds moved in over the lake.

---

### three-dimensional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθriːdɪˈmɛnʃənəl/ &middot; *three-dih-MEN-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** трёхмерный, объёмный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a three-dimensional model*; *the image is three-dimensional*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *three* + *dimensional*; often shortened to *3-D*

Three-dimensional has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having height, width and depth, so it looks solid.** (трёхмерный, объёмный)
