1. The printer makes *three-dimensional* models out of plastic.
2. A globe is *three-dimensional*; a map is flat.
3. They built a *three-dimensional* map of the cave.

**2. Believable and fully developed, of a character in a story.** (живой, многогранный (о персонаже))

4. The villain is the only *three-dimensional* character in the book.
5. She writes *three-dimensional* people, not types.

---

### three-legged

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθriːˈlɛɡɪd/ &middot; *three-LEG-id*
**Русский:** трёхногий, на трёх ножках
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a three-legged stool*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — number + noun + *-ed*
**Contrast:** The *-ed* here is a full syllable: *LEG-id*, not *legd*, like *ragged* and *naked*.

Having three legs.

1. He milked the cow on a *three-legged* stool.
2. Their *three-legged* dog gets around just fine.
3. The kids won the *three-legged* race at the picnic.

---

### three-piece

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθriːˈpiːs/ &middot; *three-PEES*
**Русский:** состоящий из трёх предметов (о костюме, наборе)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a three-piece suit*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — number + noun

Made up of three matching parts.

1. He showed up in a *three-piece* suit.
2. They bought a *three-piece* luggage set.
3. The band is a *three-piece* outfit from Detroit.

---

### three-pronged

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθriːˈprɔːŋd/ &middot; *three-PRAWNGD*
**Русский:** трёхсторонний, идущий по трём направлениям
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a three-pronged approach*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — number + noun + *-ed*, from the *prongs* of a fork

Working in three separate directions at the same time.

1. The city announced a *three-pronged* approach to homelessness.
2. Their *three-pronged* strategy covers price, service and speed.
3. He led a *three-pronged* attack on the proposal.

---

### three-way

**Pronunciation:** /ˌθriːˈweɪ/ &middot; *three-WAY*
**Русский:** трёхсторонний, на три стороны
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a three-way race*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — number + noun

Involving three people, groups or directions.

1. It turned into a *three-way* race for mayor.
2. We set up a *three-way* call with the lawyer.
3. There's a *three-way* stop at the end of our street.

---

### threefold

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθriːfoʊld/ &middot; *THREE-fohld*
**Русский:** трёхкратный, тройной
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — *a threefold increase*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *three* + *-fold*, like *twofold*, *tenfold*
**Contrast:** After a verb the same word is an adverb: *Enrollment grew threefold* — not *was threefold*.

Three times as great, or made of three parts.

1. The study found a *threefold* increase in risk.
2. Her job has a *threefold* purpose: teach, advise and recruit.
3. They expect a *threefold* rise in demand by spring.

---

### thrifty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθrɪfti/ &middot; *THRIF-tee*
**Русский:** бережливый, экономный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a thrifty shopper*; *my grandmother was thrifty*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *thriftier*, *thriftiest*
**Opposite:** *wasteful*
**Contrast:** *thrifty* is a compliment; *cheap* and *stingy* are insults for the same behavior.

Careful with money and good at not wasting it.

1. My grandmother was *thrifty* — she saved every jar.
2. *Thrifty* shoppers wait for the end-of-season sales.
3. He's *thriftier* than anyone I know, and he still travels.

---

### thriving

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθraɪvɪŋ/ &middot; *THRY-ving*
**Русский:** процветающий, преуспевающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a thriving business*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *thrive*
**Contrast:** After a verb it is the verb, not the adjective: *Her business is thriving* is the present progressive.

Growing well and doing very successfully.

1. She runs a *thriving* bakery downtown.
2. The town has a *thriving* arts scene.
3. *Thriving* coral reefs are getting rare.

---

### throaty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθroʊti/ &middot; *THROH-tee*
**Русский:** хриплый, гортанный, низкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a throaty laugh*; *her voice is throaty*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *throatier*, *throatiest*
**Form:** -y from *throat*

Low and rough, made deep in the throat.

1. She has a *throaty* laugh you can hear across the room.
2. The singer's voice gets *throatier* in the low notes.
3. The old truck has a *throaty* engine.

---

### throbbing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθrɑːbɪŋ/ &middot; *THRAH-bing*
**Русский:** пульсирующий, ноющий (о боли)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a throbbing headache*; *the pain was dull and throbbing*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *throb*

Throbbing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hurting in a steady beat, like a pulse.** (пульсирующий, ноющий)

1. I woke up with a *throbbing* headache.
2. There's a *throbbing* pain in my thumb where I hit it.
3. The ache was dull and *throbbing*, not sharp.

**2. Beating or sounding with a strong steady rhythm.** (пульсирующий, ритмично бьющий)

4. *Throbbing* bass came through the wall all night.
5. We could hear the *throbbing* engines of the ferry.

---

### throwaway

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθroʊəˌweɪ/ &middot; *THROH-uh-way*
**Русский:** одноразовый; брошенный вскользь
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a throwaway line*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound from the phrasal verb *throw away*

Throwaway has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Meant to be thrown out after one use.** (одноразовый)

1. The picnic came with *throwaway* plates and forks.
2. We live in a *throwaway* culture.
3. Cheap *throwaway* razors fill the shelf.

**2. Said quickly and without much thought, though people may still notice it.** (брошенный вскользь, случайный)

4. It was a *throwaway* line, but it made the whole room laugh.
5. His *throwaway* remark about the budget started a fight.

---

### thunderous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθʌndərəs/ &middot; *THUN-dur-uhs*
**Русский:** громовой, оглушительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *thunderous applause*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more thunderous*, *most thunderous*
**Form:** -ous from *thunder*

Extremely loud, like thunder.

1. She left the stage to *thunderous* applause.
2. There was a *thunderous* crash as the shelf came down.
3. The crowd's roar was *thunderous*.

---

### thunderstruck

**Pronunciation:** /ˈθʌndərstrʌk/ &middot; *THUN-dur-struk*
**Русский:** ошеломлённый, как громом поражённый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** predicative only — *we were thunderstruck*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *thunder* + *struck*

So surprised that you cannot speak.

1. We were *thunderstruck* when he said he was quitting.
2. She sat there *thunderstruck*, holding the letter.
3. The whole room looked *thunderstruck*.

---

### Tibetan

**Pronunciation:** /tɪˈbɛtən/ &middot; *tih-BET-uhn*
**Русский:** тибетский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *Tibetan monks*; *the script is Tibetan*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *very Tibetan* when it means 'typical of Tibet'
**Contrast:** *Tibetan* is also a noun: a person from Tibet, and the language.

From Tibet, or relating to Tibet and its people.

1. The museum has a room of *Tibetan* art.
2. She studies *Tibetan* Buddhism.
3. The writing on the scroll is *Tibetan*.

---

### ticklish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪklɪʃ/ &middot; *TIK-lish*
**Русский:** боящийся щекотки; щекотливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *a ticklish situation*; *she's ticklish*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ticklish*, *most ticklish*
**Form:** -ish from *tickle*

Ticklish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Laughing and twitching when someone touches you lightly.** (боящийся щекотки)

1. Don't touch his feet — he's *ticklish*.
2. I'm *ticklish* on my sides.
3. The baby is *ticklish* under her chin.

**2. Needing careful handling because it could easily go wrong.** (щекотливый, деликатный)

4. Asking him about the money is a *ticklish* business.
5. The negotiation reached a *ticklish* stage.

---

### tidal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaɪdəl/ &middot; *TY-duhl*
**Русский:** приливный, приливно-отливный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a tidal pool*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *tide*

Relating to the daily rise and fall of the sea.

1. The kids looked for crabs in the *tidal* pools.
2. A *tidal* creek runs behind the houses.
3. Scotland is testing *tidal* power turbines.

---

### tied

**Pronunciation:** /taɪd/ &middot; *TYD*
**Русский:** равный по счёту; завязанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a tied game*; *the score is tied*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *at* + score, *with* + team — *tied at 3-3*, *tied with Houston*

Tied has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Level with the other side, with neither one ahead.** (с равным счётом, вничью)

1. The score is *tied* at two.
2. It was a *tied* game going into the ninth.
3. They're *tied* with Houston for first place.

**2. Fastened with a knot.** (завязанный, привязанный)

4. His shoes weren't *tied*, and he tripped.
5. The bundle came in *tied* with string.

---

### tied up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtaɪd ˈʌp/ &middot; *tyd UP*
**Русский:** занятый; связанный (о деньгах)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *I'm tied up until four*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* / *with* + noun — *tied up in meetings*, *tied up with a client*
**Contrast:** Very common in American offices as a polite 'busy': *He's tied up right now* means he can't come to the phone.

Tied up has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too busy with something else to be available.** (занятый, не могущий освободиться)

1. I'm *tied up* until four — can we talk after that?
2. She's *tied up* in meetings all morning.
3. Sorry, he's *tied up* with a client right now.

**2. Held in something so that it cannot be used or moved.** (вложенный, связанный (о средствах))

4. Most of their money is *tied up* in the house.
5. Traffic was *tied up* for two miles.

---

### tiered

**Pronunciation:** /tɪrd/ &middot; *TEERD*
**Русский:** многоярусный, многоуровневый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a tiered cake*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the noun *tier*, a level or row
**Contrast:** *tiered* rhymes with *weird*, not with *tired* — one syllable.

Tiered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Built in levels, one above the other.** (многоярусный, ступенчатый)

1. They ordered a three-layer *tiered* cake.
2. The hall has *tiered* seating, so everyone can see.
3. We planted the slope in *tiered* beds.

**2. Organized in levels that give different amounts or prices.** (многоуровневый (о ценах, системе))

4. The company moved to *tiered* pricing.
5. It's a *tiered* system: basic, plus and premium.

---

### tight

**Pronunciation:** /taɪt/ &middot; *TYT*
**Русский:** тесный, тугой; плотный; напряжённый (о графике)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *tight jeans*; *the lid is too tight*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tighter*, *tightest*
**Opposite:** *loose*
**Contrast:** *tight* is also an adverb after a verb (*Hold on tight*, *Sleep tight*), but before a participle use *tightly*: *tightly packed*.

Tight has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Fitting very closely to the body, often too closely.** (тесный, облегающий)

1. These jeans are too *tight*.
2. The shoes felt *tighter* after the hike.
3. He wore a *tight* black T-shirt.

**2. Firmly fixed and hard to move or undo.** (тугой, туго затянутый)

4. The lid is on too *tight*.
5. Make sure the knot is *tight*.

**3. Leaving very little room, time or money.** (плотный, напряжённый; ограниченный)

6. We're working on a *tight* deadline.
7. Money is *tight* this month.
8. It's a *tight* schedule, but it can be done.

**4. Very close, with almost nothing between the sides.** (напряжённый, с минимальным отрывом)

9. It was a *tight* game until the last minute.
10. The polls show a *tight* race.

---

### tight-fitting

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtaɪtˈfɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *tyt-FIT-ing*
**Русский:** облегающий, плотно прилегающий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a tight-fitting lid*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + *-ing* participle
**Opposite:** *loose-fitting*

Made to fit very closely, with no gap.

1. Store the soup in a jar with a *tight-fitting* lid.
2. She wore a *tight-fitting* dress.
3. The mask needs a *tight-fitting* seal to work.

---

### tight-knit

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtaɪtˈnɪt/ &middot; *tyt-NIT*
**Русский:** сплочённый, дружный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a tight-knit family*; *the team is tight-knit*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tight-knit*, *most tight-knit*
**Form:** compound — *tight* + *knit*, from knitting close stitches

Made of people who know each other well and help each other.

1. They grew up in a *tight-knit* Italian neighborhood.
2. It's a *tight-knit* family — everyone shows up for everything.
3. The crew is *tight-knit* after three years together.

---

### tightfisted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtaɪtˈfɪstɪd/ &middot; *tyt-FIS-tid*
**Русский:** скупой, прижимистый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — *a tightfisted landlord*; *he's tightfisted*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tightfisted*, *most tightfisted*
**Form:** compound — *tight* + *fist* + *-ed*: holding the money in a closed hand
**Opposite:** *generous*
**Contrast:** Always an insult, unlike *thrifty*, which is praise.

Unwilling to spend or give money.

1. Our landlord is *tightfisted* about repairs.
2. He's *tightfisted* with everyone but his grandkids.
3. The company has a *tightfisted* travel policy.

---

### time-honored

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaɪmˌɑːnərd/ &middot; *TYM-ah-nurd*
**Русский:** освящённый временем, традиционный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a time-honored tradition*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + past participle
**Contrast:** American spelling is *time-honored*; British writing has *time-honoured*.

Respected because people have done it this way for a very long time.

1. Opening day is a *time-honored* tradition here.
2. They settled it in the *time-honored* way, over coffee.
3. The recipe follows a *time-honored* method.

---

### time-release

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaɪmrɪˌliːs/ &middot; *TYM-rih-lees*
**Русский:** с замедленным высвобождением (о лекарстве)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a time-release capsule*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + noun used as one modifier
**Contrast:** Labels also say *timed-release* and *extended-release* for the same thing.

Made to let the medicine out slowly over several hours.

1. The doctor switched him to a *time-release* capsule.
2. *Time-release* pain medication lasts through the night.
3. Some fertilizers use the same *time-release* idea.

---

### time-tested

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaɪm ˌtɛstɪd/ &middot; *TYM-tes-tid*
**Русский:** проверенный временем
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ed* participle

Proved to work over a long period of use.

1. She uses a *time-tested* recipe for the holidays.
2. The company stuck with its *time-tested* approach.
3. His advice is simple and *time-tested*.

---

### timed

**Pronunciation:** /taɪmd/ &middot; *TYMD*
**Русский:** на время; идущий по расписанию
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a timed test*; after a verb it is the passive verb, not the adjective
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *time*

Done under a set time limit, or set to happen at a fixed time.

1. The final is a *timed* test — fifty minutes, no extensions.
2. The sprinklers run on a *timed* cycle.
3. We did a *timed* run to see how long the drive takes.

---

### timeless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaɪmləs/ &middot; *TYM-luhs*
**Русский:** вечный, неподвластный времени
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more timeless*, *most timeless*
**Form:** -less from *time*

Not affected by the passing of time; always in style.

1. A plain black dress is *timeless*.
2. The movie has a *timeless* quality.
3. Nothing looks *more timeless* than a white shirt and jeans.

---

### timid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪmɪd/ &middot; *TIM-id*
**Русский:** робкий, застенчивый
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more timid*, *most timid*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Opposite:** *bold*

Easily frightened and not confident.

1. He was too *timid* to raise his hand in class.
2. A *timid* little dog hid under the couch.
3. She's *more timid* about calling strangers than her sister is.

---

### timorous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪmərəs/ &middot; *TIM-ur-uhs*
**Русский:** боязливый, робкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more timorous*, *most timorous*
**Contrast:** Formal and literary. In speech Americans say *timid* or *nervous*.

Nervous and easily frightened.

1. There was a *timorous* knock on the door.
2. Her voice was *timorous* at first.
3. The *timorous* new hire barely spoke in meetings.

---

### tingling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪŋɡlɪŋ/ &middot; *TING-gling*
**Русский:** покалывающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *tingle*

Feeling or causing a light prickling on the skin.

1. I had a *tingling* feeling in my fingers.
2. My foot fell asleep and now it's *tingling*.
3. The mint gives the balm a *tingling* coolness.

---

### tinny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪni/ &middot; *TIN-ee*
**Русский:** дребезжащий, жестяной (о звуке)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tinnier*, *tinniest*

Sounding thin and metallic, like a cheap speaker.

1. The phone's speaker sounds *tinny*.
2. *Tinny* music played from an old radio in the corner.
3. The laptop speakers are even *tinnier* than the phone.

---

### tipsy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪpsi/ &middot; *TIP-see*
**Русский:** подвыпивший, навеселе
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tipsier*, *tipsiest*
**Contrast:** *Tipsy* is mild and friendly — a glass or two. *Drunk* is much stronger.

Slightly drunk.

1. After two glasses of wine she was a little *tipsy*.
2. A few *tipsy* guests were singing in the yard.
3. He gets *tipsier* faster than anyone I know.

---

### tireless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaɪərləs/ &middot; *TY-ur-luhs*
**Русский:** неутомимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tireless*, *most tireless*
**Form:** -less from *tire*

Working on and on without getting tired or giving up.

1. She was a *tireless* advocate for the students.
2. His *tireless* work on the case finally paid off.
3. Nobody is *more tireless* than our volunteer coordinator.

---

### tiresome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaɪərsəm/ &middot; *TY-ur-suhm*
**Русский:** надоедливый, утомительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tiresome*, *most tiresome*
**Form:** -some from *tire*
**Contrast:** *Tiresome* means boring and annoying, not physically tiring — for that say *tiring*.

Boring and annoying.

1. His jokes get *tiresome* after a while.
2. We sat through a *tiresome* speech about parking.
3. Filling out the same form twice is *more tiresome* than it sounds.

---

### titanic

**Pronunciation:** /taɪˈtænɪk/ &middot; *ty-TAN-ik*
**Русский:** титанический, колоссальный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more titanic*, *most titanic*
**Contrast:** Lowercase for the adjective. Capitalized, *Titanic* is the ship.

Enormous in size, strength or effort.

1. It took a *titanic* effort to finish on time.
2. The two teams fought a *titanic* battle in the final.
3. They faced *titanic* problems in the first year.

---

### titillating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪtɪleɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *TIT-i-lay-ting*
**Русский:** щекочущий нервы, возбуждающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more titillating*, *most titillating*
**Form:** -ing from *titillate*

Mildly exciting, usually in a sexual or gossipy way.

1. The article promised *titillating* details about the actor.
2. There's nothing *titillating* about it — it's just gossip.
3. The show is *more titillating* than scary.

---

### titular

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɪtʃələr/ &middot; *TICH-uh-lur*
**Русский:** номинальный; заглавный (о персонаже)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the titular head*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Titular has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Holding a title but not the real power.** (номинальный, титульный)

1. The queen is the *titular* head of state.
2. He's the *titular* president; the board runs everything.

**2. Named in the title of a book, movie or show.** (заглавный, давший название)

3. The *titular* character doesn't appear until the last act.
4. The novel takes its name from its *titular* lighthouse.

---

### toasted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtoʊstɪd/ &middot; *TOHS-tid*
**Русский:** поджаренный, подрумяненный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *toast*

Browned by heat, the way bread is in a toaster.

1. I'll have a *toasted* bagel with cream cheese.
2. Sprinkle *toasted* almonds over the salad.
3. The bread was barely *toasted*.

---

### token

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtoʊkən/ &middot; *TOH-kuhn*
**Русский:** символический, формальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a token gesture*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** As a noun a *token* is a coin-like piece or a sign of feeling; as an adjective it always means done only for show.

Done only for show, and too small to matter.

1. The company made a *token* effort to fix the problem.
2. They offered a *token* discount of two dollars.
3. She was the *token* woman on the committee.

---

### tolerable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɑːlərəbəl/ &middot; *TAHL-ur-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** терпимый, сносный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tolerable*, *most tolerable*
**Form:** -able from *tolerate*
**Opposite:** *intolerable*

Bad, but not so bad that you cannot stand it.

1. The heat is *tolerable* in the shade.
2. The food was *tolerable*, nothing more.
3. Music makes the drive *more tolerable*.

---

### tolerant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɑːlərənt/ &middot; *TAHL-ur-uhnt*
**Русский:** терпимый, толерантный; устойчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tolerant*, *most tolerant*
**Takes:** *of* + noun or *-ing*
**Opposite:** *intolerant*

Tolerant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Willing to accept beliefs and behavior different from your own.** (терпимый, толерантный)

1. She's *tolerant* of people who disagree with her.
2. They raised their kids in a *tolerant* household.
3. He's *more tolerant* than he used to be.

**2. Able to stand a hard condition without being harmed.** (устойчивый (к чему-л.))

4. This variety is *tolerant* of cold weather.
5. We planted a drought-*tolerant* lawn.

---

### toll-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtoʊl ˈfriː/ &middot; *tohl-FREE*
**Русский:** бесплатный (о телефонном звонке)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *free*

Costing the caller nothing, said of a phone number.

1. Call our *toll-free* number for help.
2. The 800 number is *toll-free*.
3. Support is *toll-free* from anywhere in the country.

---

### tomboyish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɑːmbɔɪɪʃ/ &middot; *TAHM-boy-ish*
**Русский:** мальчишеский (о девочке), как у сорванца
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tomboyish*, *most tomboyish*
**Form:** -ish from *tomboy*

Of a girl, behaving or dressing in ways people think of as boyish.

1. She was a *tomboyish* kid who lived in jeans.
2. Her style is a little *tomboyish*.
3. The haircut looks *more tomboyish* than she wanted.

---

### tone-deaf

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtoʊn ˈdɛf/ &middot; *tohn-DEF*
**Русский:** лишённый музыкального слуха; не чувствующий настроения людей
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tone-deaf*, *most tone-deaf*
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective

Tone-deaf has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Unable to hear the difference between musical notes.** (лишённый музыкального слуха)

1. I love singing, but I'm completely *tone-deaf*.
2. He's *tone-deaf*, so the choir was never an option.

**2. Badly out of touch with how other people feel.** (бестактный, не чувствующий настроения)

3. The ad was *tone-deaf* about the layoffs.
4. Her apology came across as *tone-deaf*.
5. It was the *most tone-deaf* thing he could have said.

---

### toned

**Pronunciation:** /toʊnd/ &middot; *TOHND*
**Русский:** подтянутый (о мышцах)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more toned*, *most toned*
**Form:** -ed from *tone*

Of muscles or a body, firm from exercise.

1. Her arms are *toned* from swimming.
2. He has a lean, *toned* build.
3. She's *more toned* now than she was a year ago.

---

### tongue-in-cheek

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtʌŋ ɪn ˈtʃiːk/ &middot; *tuhng-in-CHEEK*
**Русский:** шутливый, ироничный (не всерьёз)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Said as a joke, even though it sounds serious.

1. The whole review was *tongue-in-cheek*.
2. He made a *tongue-in-cheek* suggestion that we all quit.
3. Her thank-you speech was *tongue-in-cheek* from start to finish.

---

### tongue-tied

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʌŋ taɪd/ &middot; *TUHNG-tyd*
**Русский:** потерявший дар речи (от смущения)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** predicative only — *He got tongue-tied*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Unable to speak because you are shy or nervous.

1. I get *tongue-tied* whenever she's in the room.
2. He was so *tongue-tied* he forgot his own name.
3. Don't be *tongue-tied* — just tell them what happened.

---

### toothless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtuːθləs/ &middot; *TOOTH-luhs*
**Русский:** беззубый; бессильный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more toothless*, *most toothless*
**Form:** -less from *tooth*

Toothless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having no teeth.** (беззубый)

1. The baby gave us a *toothless* grin.
2. An old, *toothless* dog slept by the fire.

**2. Having no real power to act.** (беззубый, бессильный)

3. The new rules are *toothless* without penalties.
4. Critics called the committee *toothless*.
5. The law is *more toothless* than the one it replaced.

---

### toothy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtuːθi/ &middot; *TOO-thee*
**Русский:** зубастый (об улыбке)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *a toothy grin*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *toothier*, *toothiest*

Showing a lot of teeth, said of a smile.

1. He flashed a *toothy* grin for the camera.
2. The mascot has a big *toothy* smile.
3. She gave the *toothiest* smile in the whole photo.

---

### top

**Pronunciation:** /tɑːp/ &middot; *TAHP*
**Русский:** верхний; лучший, главный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *the top shelf*; after a verb use *on top* or *the best*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** As a noun *a top* is the highest part or a piece of clothing; as a verb *to top* means to beat or to cover.

Top has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Highest in place.** (верхний)

1. The cereal is on the *top* shelf.
2. We stayed on the *top* floor.

**2. Best or most important.** (лучший, главный)

3. She's the *top* student in her class.
4. Safety is our *top* priority.
5. They hired a *top* lawyer from Chicago.

---

### top-down

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɑːp ˈdaʊn/ &middot; *tahp-DOWN*
**Русский:** нисходящий, идущий сверху (от руководства)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *bottom-up*

Decided by the people in charge and passed down to everyone else.

1. The change was *top-down*, with no input from the staff.
2. He runs the company in a *top-down* style.
3. They took a *top-down* approach to the budget.

---

### top-flight

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɑːp ˈflaɪt/ &middot; *tahp-FLYT*
**Русский:** первоклассный, высшего уровня
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *a top-flight team*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of the very best quality or rank.

1. They put together a *top-flight* research team.
2. She got *top-flight* medical care.
3. The school attracts *top-flight* coaches.

---

### top-heavy

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɑːp ˈhɛvi/ &middot; *tahp-HEV-ee*
**Русский:** перегруженный сверху; с раздутым руководством
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more top-heavy*, *most top-heavy*

Top-heavy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So heavy at the top that it tips over easily.** (неустойчивый, перегруженный сверху)

1. The bookcase is *top-heavy* — bolt it to the wall.
2. Don't load the cart that way; it gets *top-heavy*.

**2. Having too many managers for the number of workers.** (с раздутым управленческим аппаратом)

3. The company is *top-heavy* with vice presidents.
4. A *top-heavy* organization is slow to move.

---

### top-notch

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɑːp ˈnɑːtʃ/ &middot; *tahp-NAHCH*
**Русский:** первоклассный, отличный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Excellent; of the highest quality.

1. The service at that hotel is *top-notch*.
2. She's a *top-notch* editor.
3. We got *top-notch* seats for the game.

---

### top-secret

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɑːp ˈsiːkrɪt/ &middot; *tahp-SEE-krit*
**Русский:** совершенно секретный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Kept secret at the highest official level.

1. The files are *top-secret*.
2. He had *top-secret* clearance for years.
3. It's a *top-secret* project — nobody will say a word about it.

---

### topical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɑːpɪkəl/ &middot; *TAH-pi-kuhl*
**Русский:** злободневный, актуальный; наружный (о лекарстве)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more topical*, *most topical*

Topical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. About something people are talking about right now.** (злободневный, актуальный)

1. The show's humor is very *topical*.
2. She writes *topical* essays about the news.

**2. Put on the skin rather than swallowed.** (наружный, местный)

3. The doctor prescribed a *topical* cream.
4. *Topical* treatments work only where you rub them in.

---

### topless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɑːpləs/ &middot; *TAHP-luhs*
**Русский:** топлес, с обнажённой грудью
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *top*

Wearing nothing above the waist, or allowing that.

1. Some of the beaches there are *topless*.
2. *Topless* sunbathing is common on that coast.
3. The club advertised *topless* dancers.

---

### topsy-turvy

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtɑːpsi ˈtɜːrvi/ &middot; *tahp-see-TUR-vee*
**Русский:** перевёрнутый вверх дном, хаотичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Turned upside down; in complete disorder.

1. The move left the whole house *topsy-turvy*.
2. It's been a *topsy-turvy* year for the team.
3. Everything felt *topsy-turvy* after the news.

---

### tormented

**Pronunciation:** /tɔːrˈmɛntɪd/ &middot; *tor-MEN-tid*
**Русский:** измученный, истерзанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tormented*, *most tormented*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *torment*

Suffering deep mental pain.

1. He was *tormented* by guilt for years.
2. The novel follows a *tormented* young artist.
3. She looked *more tormented* every time we spoke.

---

### torrential

**Pronunciation:** /təˈrɛnʃəl/ &middot; *tuh-REN-shuhl*
**Русский:** проливной (о дожде)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *torrential rain*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of rain, falling very hard and in huge amounts.

1. *Torrential* rain closed the highway.
2. We drove through a *torrential* downpour.
3. *Torrential* storms flooded the valley.

---

### torrid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɔːrɪd/ &middot; *TOR-id*
**Русский:** знойный; страстный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more torrid*, *most torrid*

Torrid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very hot and dry.** (знойный, палящий)

1. They crossed the *torrid* desert at night.
2. August brought *torrid* heat to the city.

**2. Full of strong sexual feeling.** (страстный, пылкий)

3. The tabloids described a *torrid* romance.
4. It was a *torrid* affair that lasted one summer.

---

### tortuous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɔːrtʃuəs/ &middot; *TOR-choo-uhs*
**Русский:** извилистый; запутанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tortuous*, *most tortuous*
**Contrast:** *Tortuous* means twisting; *torturous* means full of pain. One letter apart, and Americans mix them up too.

Tortuous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of twists and turns.** (извилистый)

1. We took a *tortuous* mountain road.
2. The river follows a *tortuous* path to the sea.

**2. Long, complicated and hard to follow.** (запутанный, замысловатый)

3. After *tortuous* negotiations, they finally signed.
4. His explanation was *tortuous* and full of side stories.

---

### tortured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɔːrtʃərd/ &middot; *TOR-churd*
**Русский:** измученный; натянутый, надуманный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tortured*, *most tortured*
**Form:** -ed from *torture*

Tortured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Deeply unhappy; full of mental pain.** (измученный, истерзанный)

1. He gave a *tortured* look and said nothing.
2. The band's songs are full of *tortured* love.

**2. Twisted into something forced and unnatural.** (натянутый, притянутый за уши)

3. That's a *tortured* reading of the rules.
4. He offered a *tortured* explanation for the missing money.

---

### torturous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɔːrtʃərəs/ &middot; *TOR-chur-uhs*
**Русский:** мучительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more torturous*, *most torturous*
**Contrast:** Not *tortuous*, which means twisting. *Torturous* is about pain.

Causing great pain or suffering.

1. The last mile of the run was *torturous*.
2. They sat through a *torturous* three-hour meeting.
3. Waiting for the results was *more torturous* than the test itself.

---

### totaled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtoʊtəld/ &middot; *TOH-tuhld*
**Русский:** разбитый вдребезги (об автомобиле)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a totaled car*; *The car was totaled*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the American verb *total*, to wreck beyond repair
**Contrast:** American spelling with one *l*; the British write *totalled*. Used almost only about vehicles.

Of a vehicle, wrecked so badly that repair would cost more than it is worth.

1. Her car was *totaled* in the crash.
2. The insurance company declared the truck *totaled*.
3. He bought a *totaled* sedan for parts.

---

### totalitarian

**Pronunciation:** /toʊˌtæləˈtɛriən/ &middot; *toh-tal-uh-TAIR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** тоталитарный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of a government that controls every part of life and allows no opposition.

1. They grew up under a *totalitarian* regime.
2. The novel imagines a *totalitarian* future.
3. By the second year the state was openly *totalitarian*.

---

### touch-and-go

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtʌtʃ ən ˈɡoʊ/ &middot; *tuhch-uhn-GOH*
**Русский:** рискованный, с неясным исходом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** predicative only — *It was touch-and-go for a while*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Uncertain and risky, so that it could still go either way.

1. It was *touch-and-go* for a while, but she pulled through.
2. The landing was *touch-and-go* in that wind.
3. Funding for next year is still *touch-and-go*.

---

### touchy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʌtʃi/ &middot; *TUHCH-ee*
**Русский:** обидчивый; щекотливый
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *touchier*, *touchiest*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*

Touchy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Easily offended.** (обидчивый, мнительный)

1. He's *touchy* about his height.
2. Don't tease her — she gets *touchier* when she's tired.

**2. Of a subject or situation, needing careful handling.** (щекотливый, деликатный)

3. Money is a *touchy* subject in that family.
4. We're in a *touchy* spot with the landlord.

---

### touchy-feely

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtʌtʃi ˈfiːli/ &middot; *tuhch-ee-FEE-lee*
**Русский:** сентиментальный, с излишними обнимашками
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more touchy-feely*, *most touchy-feely*
**Contrast:** Usually disapproving — too much hugging or too much talk about feelings.

Involving a lot of touching or open talk about feelings, often more than you want.

1. The workshop was too *touchy-feely* for me.
2. He's not a *touchy-feely* kind of boss.
3. Her family is *more touchy-feely* than mine.

---

### toughened

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʌfənd/ &middot; *TUHF-uhnd*
**Русский:** закалённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *toughen*
**Contrast:** Glass treated this way is usually called *tempered* in American English, not *toughened*.

Made stronger or harder, by hard experience or by treatment.

1. He came back *toughened* by the experience.
2. A *toughened* veteran of two campaigns, she didn't flinch.
3. The steel is *toughened* to resist cracking.

---

### touristy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʊrɪsti/ &middot; *TUUR-i-stee*
**Русский:** туристический (в неодобрительном смысле)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more touristy*, *most touristy*
**Contrast:** Disapproving. The neutral word is *tourist* before a noun: *a tourist attraction*.

Full of tourists and tourist shops, and not very real.

1. That part of the city is too *touristy*.
2. We looked for a restaurant that wasn't *touristy*.
3. The beach gets *more touristy* every summer.

---

### tousled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaʊzəld/ &middot; *TOW-zuhld*
**Русский:** взъерошенный, растрёпанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *tousle*

Of hair, messy in a casual way.

1. He showed up with *tousled* hair and no jacket.
2. She has that *tousled* just-woke-up look.
3. His hair was *tousled* from the wind.

---

### towering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtaʊərɪŋ/ &middot; *TOW-ur-ing*
**Русский:** высоченный; выдающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a towering pine*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *tower*

Towering has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very tall and rising high above everything around.** (высоченный, возвышающийся)

1. *Towering* pines lined the road.
2. They live under a *towering* cliff.

**2. Far greater than others in importance.** (выдающийся, исполинский)

3. She was a *towering* figure in American poetry.
4. Building it in one year was a *towering* achievement.

---

### toxic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɑːksɪk/ &middot; *TAHK-sik*
**Русский:** ядовитый, токсичный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more toxic*, *most toxic*
**Opposite:** *nontoxic*

Toxic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Poisonous; able to harm or kill.** (ядовитый, токсичный)

1. The plant gives off *toxic* fumes.
2. These paints are *toxic* if swallowed.

**2. Making the people around it unhappy or harmed.** (токсичный (об отношениях, обстановке))

3. She finally left a *toxic* relationship.
4. The office culture had turned *toxic*.
5. Nothing is *more toxic* than a boss who lies to you.

---

### traceable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtreɪsəbəl/ &middot; *TRAY-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** прослеживаемый, отслеживаемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** -able from *trace*

Able to be followed back to a source.

1. The leak was *traceable* to one email account.
2. Every part they use is *traceable*.
3. Cash isn't *traceable* the way a card is.

---

### trackable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrækəbəl/ &middot; *TRAK-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** отслеживаемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *track*

Able to be followed while it moves or happens.

1. Ship it with a *trackable* number.
2. The package is *trackable* online.
3. Every click on the site is *trackable*.

---

### tractable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtræktəbəl/ &middot; *TRAK-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** покладистый; поддающийся решению
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tractable*, *most tractable*
**Opposite:** *intractable*
**Contrast:** Formal. The opposite, *intractable*, is far more common in everyday writing.

Tractable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Easy to control or manage.** (покладистый, послушный)

1. The pony is *tractable* enough for beginners.
2. He gets *more tractable* after lunch.

**2. Of a problem, simple enough to be worked out.** (разрешимый, поддающийся решению)

3. Break the job into *tractable* pieces.
4. The question is hard but *tractable*.

---

### trademarked

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtreɪdmɑːrkt/ &middot; *TRAYD-mahrkt*
**Русский:** зарегистрированный как товарный знак
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *trademark*

Registered as a company's legal name or symbol.

1. The name is *trademarked*, so you can't use it.
2. They sell it under a *trademarked* brand.
3. *Trademarked* logos can't be copied onto your own shirts.

---

### traditional

**Pronunciation:** /trəˈdɪʃənəl/ &middot; *truh-DISH-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** традиционный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more traditional*, *most traditional*
**Opposite:** *modern*

Following the customs and ways that have been handed down.

1. We had a *traditional* Thanksgiving dinner.
2. Her parents are *traditional* about weddings.
3. This neighborhood is *more traditional* than downtown.

---

### traditionalist

**Pronunciation:** /trəˈdɪʃənəlɪst/ &middot; *truh-DISH-uh-nuh-list*
**Русский:** традиционалистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** More often a noun (*He's a traditionalist*); as an adjective it describes people and views that hold to the old ways.

Holding to old ways and against change.

1. The change upset the *traditionalist* wing of the party.
2. He takes a *traditionalist* view of the ceremony.
3. *Traditionalist* parishes kept the older service.

---

### tragic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrædʒɪk/ &middot; *TRAJ-ik*
**Русский:** трагический, трагичный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tragic*, *most tragic*

Tragic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very sad, especially because of death or loss.** (трагичный, печальный)

1. It was a *tragic* accident.
2. The *most tragic* part is that it could have been prevented.

**2. Belonging to tragedy as a kind of drama.** (трагедийный)

3. He played the *tragic* hero.
4. The play has a *tragic* ending.

---

### trained

**Pronunciation:** /treɪnd/ &middot; *TRAYND*
**Русский:** обученный, подготовленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + noun; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** -ed from *train*

Taught the skills that a job or task needs.

1. All our staff are *trained* in first aid.
2. She's a *trained* nurse.
3. The dogs are *trained* to find survivors.

---

### tranquil

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtræŋkwɪl/ &middot; *TRANG-kwil*
**Русский:** спокойный, безмятежный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tranquil*, *most tranquil*
**Opposite:** *turbulent*

Calm, quiet and peaceful.

1. We found a *tranquil* spot by the lake.
2. The house is *tranquil* in the early morning.
3. Nothing is *more tranquil* than that little garden.

---

### transatlantic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtrænzətˈlæntɪk/ &middot; *tranz-uht-LAN-tik*
**Русский:** трансатлантический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a transatlantic flight*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** trans- + *Atlantic*

Crossing the Atlantic, or between North America and Europe.

1. We took a *transatlantic* flight to Lisbon.
2. The two labs have a *transatlantic* partnership.
3. *Transatlantic* trade talks resumed in May.

---

### transcendent

**Pronunciation:** /trænˈsɛndənt/ &middot; *tran-SEN-duhnt*
**Русский:** превосходящий обычное, запредельный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more transcendent*, *most transcendent*

Going far beyond ordinary limits; extraordinary.

1. Her performance was *transcendent*.
2. He talked about a *transcendent* moment on the mountain.
3. The last song was the *most transcendent* part of the night.

---

### transcontinental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtrænzkɑːntɪˈnɛntəl/ &middot; *tranz-kahn-ti-NEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** трансконтинентальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a transcontinental railroad*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** trans- + *continental*

Crossing a whole continent.

1. The *transcontinental* railroad changed the country.
2. They made a *transcontinental* drive in five days.
3. It's a *transcontinental* flight, so pack a snack.

---

### transferable

**Pronunciation:** /trænsˈfɜːrəbəl/ &middot; *trans-FUR-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** передаваемый, переносимый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** -able from *transfer*

Able to be passed from one person, place or job to another.

1. The ticket is not *transferable*.
2. She has *transferable* skills that any office would want.
3. The credits are *transferable* to a state school.

---

### transgender

**Pronunciation:** /trænzˈdʒɛndər/ &middot; *tranz-JEN-dur*
**Русский:** трансгендерный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** An adjective only — *a transgender student*, never a noun. Do not add *-ed*.

Having a gender identity different from the sex recorded at birth.

1. She is *transgender* and uses she and her.
2. The school has a policy for *transgender* students.
3. He came out as *transgender* last year.

---

### transient

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrænʃənt/ &middot; *TRAN-shuhnt*
**Русский:** мимолётный, временный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more transient*, *most transient*
**Opposite:** *permanent*
**Contrast:** As a noun *a transient* is a person with no fixed home.

Transient has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Lasting only a short time.** (мимолётный, кратковременный)

1. The pain was *transient* and passed by morning.
2. Fame like that is *transient*.

**2. Staying only briefly in a place.** (временно проживающий, приезжий)

3. The motel serves a *transient* population.
4. It's a *transient* neighborhood — people move every year.

---

### transitional

**Pronunciation:** /trænˈzɪʃənəl/ &middot; *tran-ZISH-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** переходный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Belonging to the stage between one situation and the next.

1. She's in a *transitional* period between jobs.
2. They set up a *transitional* government.
3. The housing is *transitional*, not permanent.

---

### transitive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrænsətɪv/ &middot; *TRAN-suh-tiv*
**Русский:** переходный (о глаголе)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *intransitive*

Of a verb, taking a direct object.

1. *Hit* is a *transitive* verb: you hit something.
2. Many verbs are both *transitive* and intransitive.
3. The dictionary marks every *transitive* use with a label.

---

### transitory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrænsətɔːri/ &middot; *TRAN-suh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** преходящий, недолговечный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more transitory*, *most transitory*
**Contrast:** Formal; in speech Americans say *temporary* or *short-lived*.

Lasting only a short time.

1. Economists called the price jump *transitory*.
2. It was a *transitory* phase in her career.
3. Happiness like that is *transitory*.

---

### translatable

**Pronunciation:** /trænsˈleɪtəbəl/ &middot; *trans-LAY-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** переводимый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *into* + noun
**Form:** -able from *translate*

Able to be put into another language or form.

1. That joke isn't *translatable* into English.
2. The poem is barely *translatable*.
3. His lab results are *translatable* to human medicine.

---

### translucent

**Pronunciation:** /trænsˈluːsənt/ &middot; *trans-LOO-suhnt*
**Русский:** полупрозрачный, просвечивающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more translucent*, *most translucent*
**Contrast:** *Translucent* lets light through but you can't see through it clearly; *transparent* you can see right through.

Letting light through without being clear.

1. The bathroom window is *translucent*.
2. She used *translucent* paper for the lampshade.
3. The petals are thin and *translucent*.

---

### transmissible

**Pronunciation:** /trænzˈmɪsəbəl/ &middot; *tranz-MIS-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** передающийся (о болезни)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more transmissible*, *most transmissible*
**Takes:** *to* + noun

Able to be passed from one person or animal to another.

1. The disease is *transmissible* through the air.
2. The new variant is *more transmissible* than the last one.
3. It's *transmissible* to dogs but not to cats.

---

### transnational

**Pronunciation:** /trænzˈnæʃənəl/ &middot; *tranz-NASH-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** транснациональный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a transnational company*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** trans- + *national*

Working across several countries rather than tied to one.

1. *Transnational* corporations moved in first.
2. It's a *transnational* problem that no one country can fix.
3. She studies *transnational* labor movements.

---

### transparent

**Pronunciation:** /trænsˈpɛrənt/ &middot; *trans-PAIR-uhnt*
**Русский:** прозрачный; открытый; явный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more transparent*, *most transparent*
**Opposite:** *opaque*

Transparent has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Clear enough to see through.** (прозрачный)

1. The container is *transparent*, so you can see what's inside.
2. *Transparent* tape doesn't show on the paper.

**2. Open about what is being done, hiding nothing.** (открытый, прозрачный)

3. The city promised a *more transparent* budget process.
4. We try to be *transparent* with customers about prices.

**3. So obvious that nobody is fooled.** (явный, шитый белыми нитками)

5. It was a *transparent* attempt to change the subject.
6. His excuse was *transparent*.

---

### transportable

**Pronunciation:** /trænsˈpɔːrtəbəl/ &middot; *trans-POR-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** транспортабельный, переносной
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *transport*

Able to be carried or moved from place to place.

1. The extra classroom is a *transportable* unit.
2. The patient wasn't *transportable* until the next day.
3. We need a *transportable* generator for the job site.

---

### trapped

**Pronunciation:** /træpt/ &middot; *TRAPT*
**Русский:** пойманный в ловушку; загнанный в угол
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb (*He felt trapped*)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* or *under* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *trap*

Trapped has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Caught somewhere with no way out.** (запертый, застрявший)

1. Two workers were *trapped* under the rubble.
2. We were *trapped* in the elevator for an hour.

**2. Unable to escape a life or situation you hate.** (загнанный в угол, как в ловушке)

3. She felt *trapped* in the marriage.
4. A *trapped* feeling came over him at his desk.

---

### trashy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtræʃi/ &middot; *TRASH-ee*
**Русский:** дрянной, низкопробный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *trashier*, *trashiest*

Cheap and of poor quality, especially in taste.

1. I read *trashy* novels at the beach.
2. The show is *trashy* but fun.
3. Her taste in movies is *trashier* than mine.

---

### traumatic

**Pronunciation:** /trəˈmætɪk/ &middot; *truh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** травмирующий, травматический
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more traumatic*, *most traumatic*

Causing lasting emotional shock, or caused by a physical injury.

1. The accident was *traumatic* for the whole family.
2. He suffered a *traumatic* brain injury.
3. Moving was *more traumatic* for the kids than for us.

---

### traveled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrævəld/ &middot; *TRAV-uhld*
**Русский:** много поездивший; оживлённый (о дороге)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly in compounds: *well-traveled*, *much-traveled*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more traveled*, *most traveled*
**Contrast:** American spelling with one *l*; the British write *travelled*.

Traveled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having traveled a lot and seen many places.** (много поездивший, повидавший мир)

1. She's a widely *traveled* journalist.
2. He's the *most traveled* person I know.

**2. Of a road, used by many people.** (оживлённый, наезженный)

3. Take the less *traveled* road along the river.
4. It's a heavily *traveled* stretch of highway.

---

### treacherous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɛtʃərəs/ &middot; *TRECH-ur-uhs*
**Русский:** опасный, коварный; вероломный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more treacherous*, *most treacherous*

Treacherous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dangerous, especially when the danger is hidden.** (опасный, коварный)

1. The roads are *treacherous* after an ice storm.
2. They climbed a *treacherous* slope in the dark.
3. The current gets *more treacherous* near the rocks.

**2. Betraying someone who trusts you.** (вероломный, предательский)

4. It was a *treacherous* thing to do to a friend.
5. His *treacherous* deputy went straight to the press.

---

### treasonous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtriːzənəs/ &middot; *TREE-zuh-nuhs*
**Русский:** изменнический, предательский
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Amounting to betrayal of your own country.

1. The letters were called *treasonous*.
2. He was charged with *treasonous* acts.
3. Selling those plans would be *treasonous*.

---

### treasured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɛʒərd/ &middot; *TREZH-urd*
**Русский:** заветный, дорогой сердцу
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a treasured photo*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more treasured*, *most treasured*
**Form:** -ed from *treasure*

Kept and loved because it means a great deal to you.

1. The ring is a *treasured* family heirloom.
2. Those letters are among her *most treasured* possessions.
3. He keeps a *treasured* photo of his grandfather on the desk.

---

### trembling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɛmblɪŋ/ &middot; *TREM-bling*
**Русский:** дрожащий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *tremble*

Shaking slightly, usually from cold, fear or strong feeling.

1. She signed the paper with a *trembling* hand.
2. His voice was *trembling*.
3. The puppy stood *trembling* in the rain.

---

### tremendous

**Pronunciation:** /trɪˈmɛndəs/ &middot; *tri-MEN-duhs*
**Русский:** огромный; потрясающий
**Type:** size; also evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tremendous*, *most tremendous*

Tremendous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very large in amount, degree or force.** (огромный, колоссальный)

1. It took a *tremendous* amount of work.
2. There was a *tremendous* crash from the kitchen.

**2. Excellent.** (потрясающий, великолепный)

3. You did a *tremendous* job on that report.
4. The band was *tremendous* live.

---

### trenchant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɛntʃənt/ &middot; *TREN-chuhnt*
**Русский:** острый, меткий (о критике)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *trenchant criticism*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more trenchant*, *most trenchant*

Of comment or criticism, sharp, clear and forceful.

1. She wrote a *trenchant* analysis of the ruling.
2. His *trenchant* criticism stung.
3. The review raised the *most trenchant* objection yet.

---

### trendsetting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɛndsɛtɪŋ/ &middot; *TREND-set-ing*
**Русский:** задающий моду
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a trendsetting designer*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing* participle

Starting a style that others then copy.

1. She's a *trendsetting* designer.
2. The phone had a *trendsetting* design.
3. *Trendsetting* restaurants opened all over the neighborhood.

---

### trendy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɛndi/ &middot; *TREN-dee*
**Русский:** модный, в тренде
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *trendier*, *trendiest*
**Contrast:** Slightly disapproving — fashionable now, maybe not for long. Plain praise is *stylish*.

Very fashionable at the moment.

1. They opened a *trendy* coffee shop downtown.
2. That neighborhood gets *trendier* every year.
3. The jacket is *trendy* but not cheap.

---

### Triassic

**Pronunciation:** /traɪˈæsɪk/ &middot; *try-AS-ik*
**Русский:** триасовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the Triassic period*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized, like the other geological periods (*Jurassic*, *Cretaceous*).

Of the geological period before the Jurassic, when the first dinosaurs appeared.

1. These fossils are from the *Triassic* period.
2. *Triassic* rock runs through the valley.
3. The first dinosaurs appeared in *Triassic* times.

---

### tricky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɪki/ &middot; *TRIK-ee*
**Русский:** непростой, каверзный; хитрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *trickier*, *trickiest*

Tricky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hard to do or handle; needing care.** (непростой, требующий сноровки)

1. Parallel parking on this street is *tricky*.
2. The wiring is the *trickiest* part of the job.

**2. Clever in a dishonest way.** (хитрый, ловкий)

3. He's a *tricky* negotiator.
4. Watch out — that's a *tricky* question.

---

### tried and true

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtraɪd ən ˈtruː/ &middot; *tryd-uhn-TROO*
**Русский:** испытанный, проверенный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun: *a tried-and-true method*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Tested many times and known to work.

1. Her method is *tried and true*.
2. We went back to the *tried-and-true* recipe.
3. There's a *tried-and-true* way to fix this.

---

### trigger-happy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɪɡər ˌhæpi/ &middot; *TRIG-ur-hap-ee*
**Русский:** готовый чуть что стрелять; скорый на расправу
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more trigger-happy*, *most trigger-happy*

Too quick to shoot, or too quick to act without thinking.

1. The movie's hero is *trigger-happy*.
2. A *trigger-happy* manager fires people over nothing.
3. He's *more trigger-happy* with the delete key than I am.

---

### trilateral

**Pronunciation:** /traɪˈlætərəl/ &middot; *try-LAT-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** трёхсторонний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a trilateral agreement*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** tri- + *lateral*, like *bilateral*

Involving three countries or three sides.

1. The three states signed a *trilateral* agreement.
2. *Trilateral* talks begin next week.
3. It's a *trilateral* deal, not a pair of separate ones.

---

### trilingual

**Pronunciation:** /traɪˈlɪŋɡwəl/ &middot; *try-LING-gwuhl*
**Русский:** трёхъязычный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** tri- + *lingual*, like *bilingual*

Speaking three languages, or written in three.

1. She's *trilingual* — English, Spanish and Korean.
2. The signs at the border are *trilingual*.
3. They run a *trilingual* preschool.

---

### trim

**Pronunciation:** /trɪm/ &middot; *TRIM*
**Русский:** подтянутый, стройный; опрятный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *trimmer*, *trimmest*
**Contrast:** Also a verb (*trim the hedge*) and a noun (*white trim around the windows*).

Trim has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Slim and in good shape.** (подтянутый, стройный)

1. He stays *trim* by running every morning.
2. She's *trimmer* now than she was in college.

**2. Neat and well kept.** (опрятный, ухоженный)

3. A row of *trim* little houses lined the street.
4. The yard was *trim* and green.

---

### tripartite

**Pronunciation:** /traɪˈpɑːrtaɪt/ &middot; *try-PAHR-tyt*
**Русский:** трёхчастный; трёхсторонний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a tripartite agreement*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** tri- + *partite* — in three parts

Having three parts, or involving three groups.

1. The essay has a *tripartite* structure.
2. They reached a *tripartite* agreement.
3. The board set up a *tripartite* review.

---

### triple

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɪpəl/ &middot; *TRIP-uhl*
**Русский:** тройной, утроенный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — *a triple murder*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a verb (*prices tripled*) and, in baseball, a noun (*he hit a triple*).

Having three parts, or three times as much.

1. She ordered a *triple* espresso.
2. The recipe makes a *triple* batch.
3. They charged him in a *triple* homicide.

---

### trite

**Pronunciation:** /traɪt/ &middot; *TRYT*
**Русский:** банальный, избитый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *triter* or *more trite*, *tritest* or *most trite*
**Opposite:** *original*

Used so often that it has lost all its force.

1. The ending felt *trite*.
2. That advice is *trite* but true.
3. Nothing sounds *more trite* than a speech about following your dreams.

---

### triumphant

**Pronunciation:** /traɪˈʌmfənt/ &middot; *try-UHM-fuhnt*
**Русский:** торжествующий, победоносный
**Type:** emotion; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more triumphant*, *most triumphant*
**Form:** -ant from *triumph*

Having won, and showing great joy about it.

1. The team made a *triumphant* return home.
2. She looked *triumphant* when the numbers came in.
3. He gave a *triumphant* shout from the other room.

---

### trivial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɪviəl/ &middot; *TRIV-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** незначительный, пустяковый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more trivial*, *most trivial*
**Contrast:** A false friend: *trivial* means unimportant, not banal or obvious. For those say *trite* or *obvious*.

So small or unimportant that it isn't worth attention.

1. They argue over *trivial* things.
2. The bug is *trivial* — it can wait until Monday.
3. Nothing about her health is *trivial* to him.

---

### tropical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrɑːpɪkəl/ &middot; *TRAH-pi-kuhl*
**Русский:** тропический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of, from, or like the hot wet regions near the equator.

1. We spent a week on a *tropical* island.
2. The plant needs *tropical* humidity.
3. A *tropical* storm is forming off the coast.

---

### trouble-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtrʌbəl ˈfriː/ &middot; *truhb-uhl-FREE*
**Русский:** беспроблемный, безотказный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *free*

Having no problems; working without trouble.

1. The car gave us ten *trouble-free* years.
2. The install was completely *trouble-free*.
3. Nothing is entirely *trouble-free*.

---

### troubled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌbəld/ &middot; *TRUH-buhld*
**Русский:** обеспокоенный, встревоженный; неблагополучный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more troubled*, *most troubled*
**Form:** -ed from *trouble*

Troubled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Worried and unhappy.** (обеспокоенный, встревоженный)

1. She had a *troubled* look on her face.
2. He was deeply *troubled* by what he'd heard.
3. I could tell something was wrong — he sounded *troubled*.

**2. Full of problems, conflict or trouble.** (неспокойный, неблагополучный)

4. The company has had a *troubled* history.
5. They grew up in a *troubled* neighborhood.
6. It was the most *troubled* year of her life.

---

### troublesome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌbəlsəm/ &middot; *TRUH-buhl-suhm*
**Русский:** беспокойный, хлопотный, доставляющий неприятности
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more troublesome*, *most troublesome*
**Form:** -some from *trouble*

Causing trouble, difficulty or annoyance.

1. A *troublesome* cough kept her up all night.
2. The most *troublesome* part of the job is the paperwork.
3. Those old pipes have always been *troublesome*.

---

### troubling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌbəlɪŋ/ &middot; *TRUH-buh-ling*
**Русский:** тревожный, вызывающий беспокойство
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more troubling*, *most troubling*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling
**Contrast:** *Troubling* news makes you worried; a *troubled* person already is worried.

Making people worried or uneasy.

1. The report raises some *troubling* questions.
2. What he said was deeply *troubling*.
3. The most *troubling* part is that nobody noticed.

---

### truculent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌkjələnt/ &middot; *TRUHK-yuh-luhnt*
**Русский:** агрессивный, задиристый, воинственный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more truculent*, *most truculent*

Quick to argue or fight; aggressively unfriendly.

1. He turned *truculent* as soon as the subject came up.
2. A *truculent* teenager slouched in the doorway.
3. Her tone grew more *truculent* with every question.

---

### true-blue

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtruːˈbluː/ &middot; *troo-BLOO*
**Русский:** верный, преданный; убеждённый
**Type:** compound; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *true* + *blue*

Completely loyal to a person, a party or a team.

1. He's a *true-blue* Yankees fan.
2. She stayed *true-blue* through the whole scandal.
3. They want a *true-blue* conservative on the ticket.

---

### true-false

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtruːˈfɔːls/ &middot; *troo-FAWLS*
**Русский:** с ответами «верно/неверно» (о тесте)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a true-false question*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *true* + *false*

Of a test question that you answer by marking it either true or false.

1. The quiz is twenty *true-false* questions.
2. I'd rather have a *true-false* test than an essay.
3. Each *true-false* item is worth one point.

---

### true-to-life

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtruː tə ˈlaɪf/ &middot; *troo-tuh-LYF*
**Русский:** правдоподобный, реалистичный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more true-to-life*, *most true-to-life*
**Contrast:** Hyphenated before a noun (*a true-to-life story*), usually written open after a verb (*the story is true to life*).

Showing people and events the way they really are.

1. It's a *true-to-life* portrait of a small town.
2. The dialogue is remarkably *true-to-life*.
3. Her novel is more *true-to-life* than the movie version.

---

### trumped-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtrʌmptˈʌp/ &middot; *truhmpt-UHP*
**Русский:** сфабрикованный, ложный (об обвинениях)
**Type:** compound; also participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *trumped-up charges*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the phrasal verb *trump up* 'invent'

Invented in order to accuse someone; not real.

1. He was jailed on *trumped-up* charges.
2. The whole case rests on *trumped-up* evidence.
3. They fired her for some *trumped-up* reason about expenses.

---

### truncated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌŋkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *TRUHNG-kay-tid*
**Русский:** усечённый, сокращённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *truncate* 'cut short'

Cut short; made shorter by cutting off the end.

1. We played a *truncated* version of the game.
2. The file names come out *truncated* on that screen.
3. Rain gave us a *truncated* season.

---

### trusted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌstɪd/ &middot; *TRUHS-tid*
**Русский:** доверенный, надёжный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more trusted*, *most trusted*
**Form:** -ed from *trust*
**Contrast:** *Trusted* means other people trust you; *trusting* means you trust other people easily.

That people rely on and believe.

1. She's his most *trusted* adviser.
2. Get your news from a *trusted* source.
3. Only a few *trusted* employees have the key.

---

### trusting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌstɪŋ/ &middot; *TRUHS-ting*
**Русский:** доверчивый
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more trusting*, *most trusting*
**Form:** -ing from *trust*
**Contrast:** A *trusting* child believes everyone; a *trusted* friend is one everyone believes.

Ready to believe that other people are honest and mean no harm.

1. He's too *trusting* for his own good.
2. She gave me a *trusting* look and handed over the money.
3. Kids are more *trusting* than adults.

---

### trustworthy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌstˌwɜːrði/ &middot; *TRUHST-wur-thee*
**Русский:** надёжный, заслуживающий доверия
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more trustworthy*, *most trustworthy*
**Form:** compound — *trust* + *worthy*
**Opposite:** *untrustworthy*

Honest and reliable, so that people can trust you.

1. She needed a *trustworthy* babysitter.
2. Is that website *trustworthy*?
3. He's the most *trustworthy* person I know.

---

### trusty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtrʌsti/ &middot; *TRUHS-tee*
**Русский:** верный, испытанный (о вещи)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *my trusty pocketknife*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -y from *trust*
**Contrast:** Half-joking, and used of old reliable objects; a person is *trustworthy*. Also a noun in prisons (*a trusty*).

Old and reliable — said of a thing you have depended on for years.

1. He fixed the fence with his *trusty* hammer.
2. I took notes in my *trusty* little notebook.
3. She drove out there in the *trusty* old pickup.

---

### truthful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtruːθfəl/ &middot; *TROOTH-fuhl*
**Русский:** правдивый, честный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more truthful*, *most truthful*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ful from *truth*
**Opposite:** *untruthful*
**Contrast:** A person is *truthful*; a statement is *true*.

Truthful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Telling the truth; not lying.** (правдивый, честный)

1. Just be *truthful* with me.
2. She wasn't *truthful* about where she'd been.

**2. Giving a true account of how something really is.** (соответствующий действительности)

3. It's a *truthful* description of what the job is like.
4. The movie is more *truthful* than the book.

---

### trying

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtraɪɪŋ/ &middot; *TRY-ing*
**Русский:** тяжёлый, трудный, утомительный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more trying*, *most trying*
**Form:** -ing from *try* 'test someone's patience'
**Contrast:** Nothing to do with *trying* to do something — the adjective means 'hard to put up with'.

Hard to bear; testing your patience.

1. It's been a *trying* week.
2. Those last few months were *trying* for everyone.
3. She stayed calm under the most *trying* conditions.

---

### tsarist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈzɑːrɪst/ &middot; *ZAHR-ist*
**Русский:** царский
**Type:** classifying; also origin
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *tsarist Russia*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ist from *tsar*
**Contrast:** Also spelled *czarist*, which is the commoner spelling in American books.

Of the emperors who ruled Russia before 1917.

1. The building dates from *tsarist* times.
2. *Tsarist* Russia had no parliament until 1906.
3. He collects *tsarist* coins and medals.

---

### tubby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʌbi/ &middot; *TUH-bee*
**Русский:** толстенький, пухлый
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *tubbier*, *tubbiest*
**Form:** -y from *tub*
**Contrast:** Informal and a little rude; *heavy* or *overweight* is the polite word.

Short and fat, in a mild, informal way.

1. A *tubby* little dog waddled past.
2. He's gotten *tubbier* since he stopped running.
3. I felt *tubby* in that sweater.

---

### Tudor

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtuːdər/ &middot; *TOO-dur*
**Русский:** тюдоровский
**Type:** origin; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a Tudor house*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also the family name of the English kings and queens from 1485 to 1603.

Of the Tudor kings and queens of England, or built in the dark-beamed style of that time.

1. They bought a *Tudor* house with dark beams across the front.
2. The class is reading a play from the *Tudor* period.
3. *Tudor* England was smaller than you'd think.

---

### tumultuous

**Pronunciation:** /tuːˈmʌltʃuəs/ &middot; *too-MUHL-choo-uhs*
**Русский:** бурный, беспокойный; шумный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tumultuous*, *most tumultuous*

Tumultuous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of noise, confusion and sudden change.** (бурный, беспокойный)

1. It was a *tumultuous* decade in American politics.
2. Their marriage was short and *tumultuous*.

**2. Very loud and excited — of a crowd or its noise.** (бурный (об аплодисментах, встрече))

3. She got a *tumultuous* welcome at the airport.
4. The speech ended to *tumultuous* applause.

---

### Tunisian

**Pronunciation:** /tuːˈniːʒən/ &middot; *too-NEE-zhuhn*
**Русский:** тунисский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for a person from Tunisia: *she's a Tunisian*, *the Tunisians*.

From Tunisia, or having to do with Tunisia.

1. We ate at a *Tunisian* place near the station.
2. The *Tunisian* coast is full of small white towns.
3. Her husband is *Tunisian*.

---

### turbulent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɜːrbjələnt/ &middot; *TUR-byuh-luhnt*
**Русский:** бурный, неспокойный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more turbulent*, *most turbulent*

Turbulent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of trouble, conflict and sudden change.** (бурный, неспокойный)

1. He grew up during a *turbulent* period in the country's history.
2. Their relationship has been *turbulent* from the start.

**2. Of air or water: moving violently and unevenly.** (бурный, неспокойный (о воздухе, воде))

3. The plane hit *turbulent* air over the mountains.
4. The river is *turbulent* for a few days after a storm.

---

### turgid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtɜːrdʒɪd/ &middot; *TUR-jid*
**Русский:** напыщенный (о стиле); вздутый, опухший
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more turgid*, *most turgid*

Turgid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of writing or speech: heavy, pompous and hard to get through.** (напыщенный, тяжеловесный)

1. The report is two hundred pages of *turgid* prose.
2. His speeches are *turgid* and far too long.

**2. Swollen with liquid.** (вздутый, опухший)

3. The plant cells were *turgid* with water.
4. The bite left the skin red and *turgid*.

---

### Tuscan

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʌskən/ &middot; *TUHS-kuhn*
**Русский:** тосканский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

From Tuscany in Italy, or done in the style of that region.

1. We rented a *Tuscan* farmhouse for a week.
2. The kitchen is done in a *Tuscan* style.
3. He ordered the *Tuscan* white bean soup.

---

### twin

**Pronunciation:** /twɪn/ &middot; *TWIN*
**Русский:** сдвоенный, парный; близнец (о брате, сестре)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *twin sisters*, *twin engines*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Much commoner as a noun (*she has a twin*). Before a noun it is the adjective.

Twin has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Born at the same time from the same mother.** (-близнец (брат, сестра))

1. Her *twin* brothers are in the same class.
2. The *twin* girls still dress alike.

**2. Being one of two matching things.** (сдвоенный, парный)

3. The room has two *twin* beds.
4. The plane has *twin* engines.
5. The *twin* towers of the cathedral are visible from the highway.

---

### twinkling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtwɪŋklɪŋ/ &middot; *TWING-kling*
**Русский:** мерцающий; блестящий (о глазах)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *twinkle*
**Contrast:** Also a noun in *in the twinkling of an eye* 'in an instant'.

Shining with small, quick changes in brightness.

1. *Twinkling* lights hung across the porch.
2. She looked up at him with *twinkling* eyes.
3. From up here the whole valley is *twinkling*.

---

### twisted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtwɪstɪd/ &middot; *TWIS-tid*
**Русский:** скрученный, погнутый; извращённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more twisted*, *most twisted*
**Form:** -ed from *twist*

Twisted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Bent or turned out of its proper shape.** (скрученный, погнутый)

1. The frame was *twisted* in the crash.
2. A *twisted* piece of wire held the gate shut.

**2. Strange and unpleasant in a cruel way.** (извращённый, нездоровый)

3. That's a *twisted* thing to say.
4. He has a *twisted* sense of humor.
5. The plot gets more *twisted* with every episode.

---

### twisty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtwɪsti/ &middot; *TWIS-tee*
**Русский:** извилистый; запутанный
**Type:** shape; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *twistier*, *twistiest*
**Form:** -y from *twist*

Twisty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having many bends.** (извилистый)

1. It's a narrow, *twisty* road up the mountain.
2. The trail gets *twistier* near the top.

**2. Of a story: full of unexpected turns.** (запутанный, с неожиданными поворотами)

3. It's a *twisty* thriller that keeps you guessing.
4. The plot is *twisty* but it holds together.

---

### two-dimensional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuː dɪˈmɛnʃənəl/ &middot; *too-di-MEN-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** двумерный, плоский; поверхностный (о персонаже)
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more two-dimensional*, *most two-dimensional*
**Form:** compound — *two* + *dimensional*

Two-dimensional has 2 distinct senses.
