**1. Flat — having length and width but no depth.** (двумерный, плоский)

1. A drawing is *two-dimensional*; a sculpture isn't.
2. The map gives you a *two-dimensional* version of a round earth.

**2. Of a character in a book or movie: shallow, with no real inner life.** (плоский, поверхностный)

3. The villain is completely *two-dimensional*.
4. Even the minor characters are less *two-dimensional* than in the first book.

---

### two-faced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuːˈfeɪst/ &middot; *too-FAYST*
**Русский:** двуличный, лицемерный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more two-faced*, *most two-faced*
**Form:** compound — *two* + *face* + *-ed*

Saying one thing to someone's face and another behind their back.

1. I didn't realize how *two-faced* he was.
2. She called her boss a *two-faced* liar.
3. That was a *two-faced* thing to do.

---

### two-lane

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuːˈleɪn/ &middot; *too-LAYN*
**Русский:** двухполосный
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a two-lane road*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *two* + *lane*

Of a road: having one lane of traffic in each direction.

1. It's a *two-lane* highway the whole way there.
2. They're finally widening the *two-lane* bridge.
3. We took the old *two-lane* road along the river.

---

### two-party

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuːˈpɑːrti/ &middot; *too-PAHR-tee*
**Русский:** двухпартийный
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *the two-party system*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *two* + *party*

Involving or controlled by two main political parties.

1. The United States has a *two-party* system.
2. Third parties rarely break the *two-party* hold on Congress.
3. He's arguing for an end to *two-party* politics.

---

### two-piece

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuːˈpiːs/ &middot; *too-PEES*
**Русский:** состоящий из двух предметов; раздельный (о купальнике)
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a two-piece suit*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *two* + *piece*
**Contrast:** Also a noun for a swimsuit: *she wore a two-piece*.

Made of two separate matching pieces.

1. He wore a gray *two-piece* suit.
2. She bought a *two-piece* swimsuit.
3. The desk comes as a *two-piece* set.

---

### two-pronged

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuːˈprɔːŋd/ &middot; *too-PRAWNGD*
**Русский:** состоящий из двух направлений (о плане, атаке)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a two-pronged approach*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *two* + *prong* + *-ed*

Having two separate parts that work at the same time.

1. They took a *two-pronged* approach to the problem.
2. It was a *two-pronged* attack, from the north and from the west.
3. The bill is a *two-pronged* attempt to cut costs and raise revenue.

---

### two-sided

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuːˈsaɪdɪd/ &middot; *too-SY-did*
**Русский:** двусторонний; имеющий две стороны
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *two* + *side* + *-ed*

Two-sided has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having two sides that are both used or printed on.** (двусторонний)

1. Use the *two-sided* setting to save paper.
2. The sign is *two-sided*, so you can read it from either direction.

**2. Having two points of view that both deserve a hearing.** (имеющий две стороны (о споре))

3. Every argument is *two-sided*.
4. It's a *two-sided* story, and we've heard only one half.

---

### two-timing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuːˈtaɪmɪŋ/ &middot; *too-TY-ming*
**Русский:** изменяющий, неверный
**Type:** compound; also participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a two-timing husband*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the verb *two-time* 'cheat on a partner'

Cheating on a wife, husband or partner by secretly seeing someone else.

1. She threw the *two-timing* jerk out of the house.
2. It's a country song about a *two-timing* boyfriend.
3. He got a reputation as a *two-timing* husband.

---

### two-way

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtuːˈweɪ/ &middot; *too-WAY*
**Русский:** двусторонний; обоюдный
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a two-way street*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *two* + *way*
**Contrast:** *A two-way street* is also an idiom for something both sides have to do: *respect is a two-way street*.

Two-way has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Working or moving in both directions.** (двусторонний)

1. It's a *two-way* street, so watch for oncoming cars.
2. The crew talked on *two-way* radios.

**2. Involving two people or sides equally.** (обоюдный)

3. Trust is a *two-way* deal.
4. It has to be a *two-way* conversation, not a lecture.

---

### twofold

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtuːfoʊld/ &middot; *TOO-fohld*
**Русский:** двойной, двоякий
**Type:** compound; also quantity
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *two* + *-fold*
**Contrast:** Also an adverb, and that use is commoner: *sales rose twofold*.

Having two parts, or twice as large.

1. The problem is *twofold*: no money and no time.
2. We saw a *twofold* increase in traffic.
3. Her purpose in coming was *twofold*.

---

### typographical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtaɪpəˈɡræfɪkəl/ &middot; *ty-puh-GRAF-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** типографский; связанный с набором текста
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *typography*
**Contrast:** Nearly always in *typographical error* — in everyday speech people just say *typo*.

Having to do with how text is set and printed.

1. There's a *typographical* error on the cover.
2. The only mistakes left are *typographical*.
3. He made a few *typographical* changes to the layout.

---

### tyrannical

**Pronunciation:** /tɪˈrænɪkəl/ &middot; *ti-RAN-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** тиранический, деспотичный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more tyrannical*, *most tyrannical*
**Form:** -ical from *tyrant*

Using power over other people in a cruel and unfair way.

1. They finally got rid of a *tyrannical* boss.
2. The regime grew more *tyrannical* every year.
3. His father was *tyrannical* about bedtimes.

---

### U-shaped

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuː ʃeɪpt/ &middot; *YOO-shaypt*
**Русский:** U-образный, подковообразный
**Type:** shape; also compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — letter + *shaped*

Curved like the letter U.

1. The desks were set up in a *U-shaped* arrangement.
2. A *U-shaped* valley was carved out by a glacier.
3. The driveway is *U-shaped*, so you never have to back up.

---

### ubiquitous

**Pronunciation:** /juːˈbɪkwɪtəs/ &middot; *yoo-BIK-wi-tuhs*
**Русский:** вездесущий, повсеместный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ubiquitous*, *most ubiquitous*

Found everywhere; seeming to be in every place at once.

1. Cell phones are *ubiquitous* now.
2. Those *ubiquitous* orange cones mean road work ahead.
3. Coffee shops are more *ubiquitous* here than anywhere else I've lived.

---

### Ugandan

**Pronunciation:** /juːˈɡændən/ &middot; *yoo-GAN-duhn*
**Русский:** угандийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for a person from Uganda: *he's a Ugandan*.

From Uganda, or having to do with Uganda.

1. He teaches at a *Ugandan* university.
2. The *Ugandan* team won the relay.
3. Her parents are *Ugandan*.

---

### ulterior

**Pronunciation:** /ʌlˈtɪriər/ &middot; *uhl-TIR-ee-ur*
**Русский:** скрытый, тайный (о мотивах)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *an ulterior motive*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Almost always in *ulterior motive* — a hidden reason behind a friendly-looking act.

Hidden, and different from the reason a person gives.

1. I think he has an *ulterior* motive for helping.
2. She offered with no *ulterior* purpose at all.
3. There's always some *ulterior* reason with that family.

---

### ultimate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌltɪmət/ &middot; *UHL-ti-muht*
**Русский:** окончательный, конечный; высший
**Type:** classifying; also emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *the ultimate goal*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun in *the ultimate in* + noun: *the ultimate in comfort*.

Ultimate has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Final; coming at the end of a process.** (окончательный, конечный)

1. The *ultimate* decision is hers.
2. Our *ultimate* goal is to get everyone housed.

**2. The best or greatest of its kind.** (высший, идеальный)

3. It's the *ultimate* road trip.
4. That's the *ultimate* compliment, coming from her.

---

### ultraconservative

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌltrəkənˈsɜːrvətɪv/ &middot; *uhl-truh-kuhn-SUR-vuh-tiv*
**Русский:** ультраконсервативный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ultraconservative*, *most ultraconservative*
**Form:** ultra- + *conservative*
**Contrast:** Also a noun for a person: *the ultraconservatives in the party*.

Far to the right politically, or strongly opposed to any change.

1. He writes for an *ultraconservative* magazine.
2. Her views on schooling are *ultraconservative*.
3. The district keeps electing *ultraconservative* candidates.

---

### ultraviolet

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌltrəˈvaɪələt/ &middot; *uhl-truh-VY-uh-luht*
**Русский:** ультрафиолетовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *ultraviolet light*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** ultra- + *violet*
**Contrast:** Shortened to *UV* in everyday talk: *UV rays*, *UV index*.

Of light waves just beyond violet, which the eye cannot see but which burn skin.

1. Sunscreen blocks *ultraviolet* rays.
2. They check the bills under an *ultraviolet* lamp.
3. *Ultraviolet* light kills bacteria on the surface.

---

### umpteen

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌmpˈtiːn/ &middot; *uhmp-TEEN*
**Русский:** бесчисленный, уйма
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *umpteen times*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Informal, and always a vague 'a lot' — you never say how many, and you cannot count them.

A large but uncounted number of.

1. I've told you *umpteen* times to close the door.
2. There are *umpteen* ways to do this.
3. She has *umpteen* cousins in Ohio.

---

### umpteenth

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌmpˈtiːnθ/ &middot; *uhmp-TEENTH*
**Русский:** энный, бесчисленный (по счёту)
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — after *the*: *the umpteenth time*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *umpteen* + *-th*, like *ten* and *tenth*
**Contrast:** Nearly always in *for the umpteenth time*, said in exasperation.

Coming after a large, uncounted number of others.

1. For the *umpteenth* time, no.
2. This is the *umpteenth* meeting about the same thing.
3. He was watching the *umpteenth* rerun of that show.

---

### un-American

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈmɛrɪkən/ &middot; *uhn-uh-MAIR-i-kuhn*
**Русский:** антиамериканский, чуждый американским ценностям
**Type:** evaluative; also origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more un-American*, *most un-American*
**Form:** un- + *American*
**Contrast:** A political insult, not a neutral word: it means 'against American values', not 'not from America'.

Against what the speaker takes to be American values.

1. He called the protest *un-American*.
2. Censorship is *un-American*, they argued.
3. Nothing is more *un-American* than telling people how to pray.

---

### unabashed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈbæʃt/ &middot; *uhn-uh-BASHT*
**Русский:** нисколько не смущённый, откровенный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unabashed*, *most unabashed*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** un- + *abashed* 'embarrassed'

Not at all embarrassed or ashamed, where most people would be.

1. She's an *unabashed* fan of bad movies.
2. He was completely *unabashed* about asking for more money.
3. There was *unabashed* joy on their faces.

---

### unabated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈbeɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-BAY-tid*
**Русский:** неослабевающий, не стихающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after a verb: *the rain continued unabated*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *abated*, from *abate* 'get weaker'

Going on at full strength, without getting any weaker.

1. The rain continued *unabated* all night.
2. Demand for tickets was *unabated*.
3. *Unabated* construction has changed the whole neighborhood.

---

### unabridged

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈbrɪdʒd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-BRIJD*
**Русский:** полный, несокращённый
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *abridged*, from *abridge* 'shorten'
**Opposite:** *abridged*

Printed or recorded complete, with nothing cut out.

1. I listened to the *unabridged* audiobook.
2. It's the *unabridged* edition, all nine hundred pages.
3. The text on this page is *unabridged*.

---

### unacceptable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəkˈsɛptəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-uhk-SEP-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неприемлемый, недопустимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unacceptable*, *most unacceptable*
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** un- + *acceptable*
**Opposite:** *acceptable*

So bad or wrong that you cannot allow it.

1. That kind of language is *unacceptable* here.
2. The delay is *unacceptable* to our customers.
3. They sent back an *unacceptable* offer.

---

### unaccompanied

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈkʌmpənid/ &middot; *uhn-uh-KUHM-puh-need*
**Русский:** без сопровождения; без аккомпанемента
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** un- + *accompanied*

Unaccompanied has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With nobody going along.** (без сопровождения, один)

1. *Unaccompanied* minors have to check in at the desk.
2. Children under twelve may not ride *unaccompanied*.

**2. Of music: sung or played with no instruments behind it.** (без аккомпанемента)

3. She sang the last verse *unaccompanied*.
4. It's a piece for *unaccompanied* cello.

---

### unaccountable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈkaʊntəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-KOWN-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** необъяснимый; неподотчётный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unaccountable*, *most unaccountable*
**Takes:** *to* + noun, in the second sense
**Form:** un- + *accountable*

Unaccountable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Impossible to explain.** (необъяснимый)

1. For some *unaccountable* reason, he turned around and left.
2. There was an *unaccountable* delay at the border.

**2. Not having to answer to anyone for what you do.** (неподотчётный)

3. The agency is *unaccountable* to voters.
4. Power that is *unaccountable* gets abused.

---

### unaccustomed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈkʌstəmd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-KUHS-tuhmd*
**Русский:** непривыкший; непривычный, несвойственный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** un- + *accustomed*

Unaccustomed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not used to something.** (непривыкший (к чему-л.))

1. He was *unaccustomed* to the heat.
2. She's *unaccustomed* to being told no.

**2. Not usual; not what that person normally does.** (непривычный, несвойственный)

3. He spoke with *unaccustomed* warmth.
4. There was an *unaccustomed* quiet in the house.

---

### unachievable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈtʃiːvəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-CHEE-vuh-buhl*
**Русский:** недостижимый, невыполнимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unachievable*, *most unachievable*
**Form:** un- + *achievable*
**Opposite:** *achievable*

Impossible to reach or carry out.

1. The deadline was *unachievable* from the start.
2. They set an *unachievable* target and then blamed the staff.
3. Nothing here is *unachievable* if we start now.

---

### unacknowledged

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəkˈnɑːlɪdʒd/ &middot; *uhn-uhk-NAH-lijd*
**Русский:** непризнанный; оставшийся без ответа
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *acknowledged*

Not admitted, not noticed, or not thanked for.

1. Her work went *unacknowledged* for years.
2. There's an *unacknowledged* rule about where people sit.
3. My email is still *unacknowledged*.

---

### unaddressed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈdrɛst/ &middot; *uhn-uh-DREST*
**Русский:** нерешённый, оставленный без внимания
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after a verb: *the problem went unaddressed*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *addressed*, from *address* 'deal with'

Of a problem or a question: not dealt with.

1. The safety issues went *unaddressed* for months.
2. Several *unaddressed* complaints are still in the file.
3. That part of the question is *unaddressed* in the report.

---

### unadorned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈdɔːrnd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-DORND*
**Русский:** неукрашенный, простой
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *adorned*, from *adorn* 'decorate'

Plain, with nothing added for decoration.

1. The room was white and *unadorned*.
2. He gave the *unadorned* facts and sat down.
3. It's a simple, *unadorned* wooden box.

---

### unadulterated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈdʌltəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-DUHL-tuh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** чистейший, абсолютный; неразбавленный
**Type:** participial; also emphasizing
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *adulterated*, from *adulterate* 'mix in something cheaper'

Unadulterated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Complete, with nothing mixed in — used to make the next word stronger.** (чистейший, абсолютный)

1. That is pure, *unadulterated* nonsense.
2. The look on his face was *unadulterated* joy.

**2. Of food or drink: pure, with nothing added to it.** (неразбавленный, без примесей)

3. They sell *unadulterated* honey straight from the hive.
4. The oil is *unadulterated* — nothing in it but olives.

---

### unadventurous

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnədˈvɛntʃərəs/ &middot; *uhn-uhd-VEN-chuh-ruhs*
**Русский:** не склонный к риску; пресный, скучный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unadventurous*, *most unadventurous*
**Form:** un- + *adventurous*
**Opposite:** *adventurous*

Not willing to try new or risky things, and dull as a result.

1. I'm pretty *unadventurous* when it comes to food.
2. It was a safe, *unadventurous* choice.
3. The menu is more *unadventurous* than I expected.

---

### unaffected

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈfɛktɪd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-FEK-tid*
**Русский:** незатронутый; естественный, без притворства
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** un- + *affected*
**Contrast:** In the second sense the opposite is *affected* 'putting on an act', not 'influenced'.

Unaffected has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not changed or harmed by something.** (незатронутый)

1. Most of the town was *unaffected* by the flood.
2. Prices for *unaffected* products stayed the same.

**2. Natural and sincere, with no show about it.** (естественный, без притворства)

3. She has an *unaffected* way of talking to everyone.
4. His manner is warm and *unaffected*.

---

### unaffiliated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈfɪlieɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-FIL-ee-ay-tid*
**Русский:** не связанный с организацией; беспартийный
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** un- + *affiliated*

Not belonging to or connected with a particular organization or party.

1. The clinic is *unaffiliated* with any hospital.
2. *Unaffiliated* voters can't vote in the primary.
3. He's registered as *unaffiliated*.

---

### unafraid

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈfreɪd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-FRAYD*
**Русский:** не боящийся, бесстрашный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb: *she seemed unafraid*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unafraid*, *most unafraid*
**Takes:** *of* + noun or *-ing*; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** un- + *afraid*

Not frightened.

1. She walked right up to the dog, completely *unafraid*.
2. He was *unafraid* of losing the job.
3. They're *unafraid* to say what they think.

---

### unaided

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈeɪdɪd/ &middot; *uhn-AY-did*
**Русский:** без посторонней помощи, самостоятельный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after a verb: *he walked unaided*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *aided*
**Contrast:** Also fixed in *the unaided eye* — without a telescope, microscope or glasses.

Without help from anyone or anything.

1. A week later she was walking *unaided*.
2. The comet is visible to the *unaided* eye.
3. He finished the puzzle *unaided*.

---

### unalienable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈeɪliənəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-AY-lee-uh-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неотъемлемый (о правах)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *unalienable rights*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *alienable*
**Contrast:** *Inalienable* is the ordinary modern word; *unalienable* survives mainly because the Declaration of Independence used it.

Of a right: that cannot be taken away or given up.

1. The Declaration speaks of certain *unalienable* rights.
2. They call free speech an *unalienable* right.
3. Those are *unalienable* liberties, the judge wrote.

---

### unaltered

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɔːltərd/ &middot; *uhn-AWL-turd*
**Русский:** неизменённый, прежний
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *altered*

Exactly as it was before; not changed.

1. The recipe has come down *unaltered* for a hundred years.
2. His opinion remains *unaltered*.
3. They published the *unaltered* transcript.

---

### unambiguous

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnæmˈbɪɡjuəs/ &middot; *uhn-am-BIG-yoo-uhs*
**Русский:** недвусмысленный, однозначный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *ambiguous*
**Opposite:** *ambiguous*

Having only one possible meaning; perfectly clear.

1. The instructions need to be *unambiguous*.
2. She gave an *unambiguous* no.
3. The evidence is clear and *unambiguous*.

---

### unanimous

**Pronunciation:** /juːˈnænɪməs/ &middot; *yoo-NAN-i-muhs*
**Русский:** единогласный, единодушный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Contrast:** The stress falls on the second syllable, and the word starts *yoo-*, not *oo-*.

Agreed on by everyone, with nobody against it.

1. The vote was *unanimous*.
2. The jury reached a *unanimous* verdict.
3. The committee was *unanimous* in recommending her.

---

### unannounced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈnaʊnst/ &middot; *uhn-uh-NOWNST*
**Русский:** без предупреждения, необъявленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after a verb: *he showed up unannounced*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *announced*

Happening with no warning that it was coming.

1. He showed up at the door *unannounced*.
2. There was an *unannounced* inspection on Tuesday.
3. She left *unannounced* sometime in the night.

---

### unanswerable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈænsərəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-AN-suh-ruh-buhl*
**Русский:** не имеющий ответа; неопровержимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *answerable*

Unanswerable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. That cannot be answered.** (не имеющий ответа)

1. Kids ask *unanswerable* questions.
2. Why some people recover and others don't is *unanswerable* right now.

**2. That cannot be argued against.** (неопровержимый)

3. The case against him is *unanswerable*.
4. She made an *unanswerable* argument for waiting.

---

### unanswered

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈænsərd/ &middot; *uhn-AN-surd*
**Русский:** оставшийся без ответа
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after a verb: *the letter went unanswered*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *answered*

That nobody has replied to or explained.

1. My letter went *unanswered*.
2. There are still a lot of *unanswered* questions.
3. The phone rang *unanswered* for a full minute.

---

### unanticipated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnænˈtɪsɪpeɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-an-TIS-i-pay-tid*
**Русский:** непредвиденный, неожиданный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *anticipated*

Not expected, and so not planned for.

1. The trip had a few *unanticipated* expenses.
2. An *unanticipated* snowstorm closed the airport.
3. The side effects were entirely *unanticipated*.

---

### unapologetic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəpɑːləˈdʒɛtɪk/ &middot; *uhn-uh-pah-luh-JET-ik*
**Русский:** не извиняющийся, не раскаивающийся
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unapologetic*, *most unapologetic*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** un- + *apologetic*

Not sorry, and not pretending to be.

1. He's completely *unapologetic* about the way he votes.
2. She wrote an *unapologetic* defense of the program.
3. The music is loud and *unapologetic*.

---

### unappealing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈpiːlɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-uh-PEE-ling*
**Русский:** непривлекательный, малопривлекательный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unappealing*, *most unappealing*
**Form:** un- + *appealing*
**Opposite:** *appealing*

Not attractive or pleasant; not something anyone would want.

1. Both options are *unappealing*.
2. Cold eggs make an *unappealing* breakfast.
3. The idea of another meeting is deeply *unappealing*.

---

### unappetizing

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈæpɪtaɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-AP-i-ty-zing*
**Русский:** неаппетитный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unappetizing*, *most unappetizing*
**Form:** un- + *appetizing*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *-izing*; British books write *unappetising*.

Looking or smelling as though it would not taste good.

1. The soup looked gray and *unappetizing*.
2. It was an *unappetizing* pile of something on the plate.
3. Cafeteria food is famously *unappetizing*.

---

### unappreciated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈpriːʃieɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-PREE-shee-ay-tid*
**Русский:** недооценённый, не получающий признания
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after a verb: *she felt unappreciated*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *appreciated*

Not thanked for or valued as much as deserved.

1. She quit because she felt *unappreciated*.
2. His years of work went largely *unappreciated*.
3. It's an *unappreciated* little museum on a side street.

---

### unapproachable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈproʊtʃəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-PROH-chuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неприступный, недоступный (о человеке)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unapproachable*, *most unapproachable*
**Form:** un- + *approachable*
**Opposite:** *approachable*

Seeming cold or distant, so that people are afraid to start a conversation.

1. The new manager comes across as *unapproachable*.
2. He has an *unapproachable* air about him.
3. She's friendlier than she looks — not *unapproachable* at all.

---

### unashamed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈʃeɪmd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-SHAYMD*
**Русский:** не стыдящийся, откровенный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unashamed*, *most unashamed*
**Takes:** *of* or *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** un- + *ashamed*

Feeling no shame about something other people might think you should.

1. He's *unashamed* of where he came from.
2. It was an *unashamed* attempt to get attention.
3. She's *unashamed* about loving bad TV.

---

### unassailable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈseɪləbəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-SAY-luh-buhl*
**Русский:** неоспоримый, неуязвимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *assailable*, from *assail* 'attack'

So strong that it cannot be attacked or argued against.

1. By halftime they had an *unassailable* lead.
2. Her reputation was *unassailable*.
3. The logic of the argument is *unassailable*.

---

### unassuming

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈsuːmɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-uh-SOO-ming*
**Русский:** скромный, непритязательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unassuming*, *most unassuming*
**Form:** un- + *assuming*
**Contrast:** Praise, not criticism: it means modest, not weak.

Modest and quiet; not trying to seem important.

1. He's a quiet, *unassuming* man who happens to run the company.
2. The restaurant is small and *unassuming*.
3. She was the most *unassuming* person in the room.

---

### unattached

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈtætʃt/ &middot; *uhn-uh-TACHT*
**Русский:** свободный, не состоящий в отношениях; неприкреплённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun, in the second sense
**Form:** un- + *attached*

Unattached has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not married and not in a relationship.** (свободный, одинокий)

1. Most of her friends are still *unattached*.
2. He came to the wedding *unattached*.

**2. Not joined or connected to anything.** (неприкреплённый, отдельный)

3. One wire was left *unattached*.
4. The garage is *unattached* to the house.

---

### unattainable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈteɪnəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-TAY-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** недостижимый, недосягаемый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *attainable*
**Opposite:** *attainable*

Impossible to reach or get, however hard you try.

1. The magazines sell an *unattainable* standard of beauty.
2. For most families that price is simply *unattainable*.
3. He set himself an *unattainable* goal and then gave up.

---

### unattended

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈtɛndɪd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-TEN-did*
**Русский:** оставленный без присмотра
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after a verb: *don't leave bags unattended*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *attended*

Left with nobody watching or looking after it.

1. Do not leave your bags *unattended*.
2. An *unattended* campfire started the whole thing.
3. The register was *unattended* for ten minutes.

---

### unattractive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈtræktɪv/ &middot; *uhn-uh-TRAK-tiv*
**Русский:** непривлекательный, некрасивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unattractive*, *most unattractive*
**Form:** un- + *attractive*
**Opposite:** *attractive*

Unattractive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not good-looking.** (некрасивый)

1. He's not *unattractive*, just not my type.
2. It's an *unattractive* building on a beautiful street.

**2. Not appealing as a choice or an offer.** (непривлекательный (о варианте))

3. The terms of the loan are *unattractive*.
4. Both options look *unattractive* from here.

---

### unauthorized

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɔːθəraɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-AW-thuh-ryzd*
**Русский:** несанкционированный, неразрешённый
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *authorized*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *-ized*; British books write *unauthorised*.

Done or made without official permission.

1. *Unauthorized* vehicles will be towed.
2. Someone made an *unauthorized* charge on my card.
3. The biography is *unauthorized*.

---

### unavailable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈveɪləbəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-VAY-luh-buhl*
**Русский:** недоступный, отсутствующий; занятый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun; *for* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** un- + *available*
**Opposite:** *available*

Unavailable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not able to be had, bought or seen.** (недоступный, отсутствующий)

1. That size is *unavailable* right now.
2. The records are *unavailable* to the public.

**2. Of a person: not free to talk to or meet.** (занятый, недоступный)

3. She was *unavailable* for comment.
4. He's *unavailable* until Thursday.

---

### unavoidable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈvɔɪdəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-VOY-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** неизбежный, неминуемый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *avoidable*
**Opposite:** *avoidable*

Impossible to prevent or get out of.

1. A short delay is *unavoidable*.
2. There were *unavoidable* costs nobody had budgeted for.
3. The conclusion is *unavoidable*: we need more people.

---

### unbalanced

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbælənst/ &middot; *uhn-BAL-uhnst*
**Русский:** несбалансированный, однобокий; неуравновешенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unbalanced*, *most unbalanced*
**Form:** un- + *balanced*
**Contrast:** The 'mentally ill' sense is old-fashioned and unkind; careful writers avoid it.

Unbalanced has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not giving equal weight to each side; uneven.** (несбалансированный, однобокий)

1. The coverage was *unbalanced* — one side got all the airtime.
2. An *unbalanced* diet will catch up with you.

**2. Mentally disturbed.** (неуравновешенный, психически нездоровый)

3. The letters came from someone clearly *unbalanced*.
4. His lawyer argued that he was mentally *unbalanced*.

---

### unbearable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbɛrəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-BAIR-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** невыносимый, нестерпимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unbearable*, *most unbearable*
**Form:** un- + *bearable*
**Opposite:** *bearable*

Too painful, unpleasant or annoying to put up with.

1. The heat was *unbearable* by noon.
2. He's *unbearable* when he loses.
3. The waiting was the most *unbearable* part.

---

### unbeatable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbiːtəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-BEE-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** непобедимый; непревзойдённый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *beatable*

Unbeatable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Impossible to defeat.** (непобедимый)

1. At home, that team is *unbeatable*.
2. She had an *unbeatable* record that season.

**2. Better than anything else — of a price, an offer or a spot.** (непревзойдённый, вне конкуренции)

3. They advertise *unbeatable* prices on tires.
4. The location is *unbeatable*.

---

### unbeaten

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbiːtən/ &middot; *uhn-BEE-tuhn*
**Русский:** непобеждённый, не знающий поражений
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *beaten*
**Contrast:** *Unbeaten* is a fact — nobody has beaten them yet; *unbeatable* is a judgment — nobody can.

Having lost no games or races so far.

1. They're *unbeaten* going into the playoffs.
2. It's the school's first *unbeaten* season in thirty years.
3. His record in that weight class is still *unbeaten*.

---

### unbecoming

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnbɪˈkʌmɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-bi-KUH-ming*
**Русский:** неподобающий, недостойный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unbecoming*, *most unbecoming*
**Takes:** *in* or *to* + noun
**Form:** un- + *becoming*
**Contrast:** In the military phrase *conduct unbecoming an officer* it follows the noun and takes no preposition. The older sense 'not flattering' (*an unbecoming hat*) now sounds dated.

Not suitable for a person in that position; slightly shameful.

1. That kind of sniping is *unbecoming* in a senator.
2. He was fired for conduct *unbecoming* a teacher.
3. There was an *unbecoming* eagerness in the way he agreed.

---

### unbeknownst

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnbɪˈnoʊnst/ &middot; *uhn-bi-NOHNST*
**Русский:** без ведома (кого-л.), тайно от (кого-л.)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — almost always in the fixed phrase *unbeknownst to* + person
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** un- + *beknownst*, an old form of *known*
**Contrast:** Only ever used in *unbeknownst to someone* — you cannot say *an unbeknownst fact*.

Without a particular person knowing about it.

1. *Unbeknownst* to his parents, he'd dropped two classes.
2. The meeting had been moved, *unbeknownst* to half the staff.
3. She had been paying the bill for years, *unbeknownst* to him.

---

### unbelievable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnbɪˈliːvəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-bi-LEE-vuh-buhl*
**Русский:** невероятный, потрясающий; неправдоподобный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unbelievable*, *most unbelievable*
**Form:** un- + *believable*

Unbelievable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So surprising, good or bad that it is hard to take in.** (невероятный, потрясающий)

1. The view from up there is *unbelievable*.
2. It's *unbelievable* how fast kids grow.
3. That was the most *unbelievable* finish I've ever seen.

**2. Too unlikely to be true.** (неправдоподобный)

4. His excuse was frankly *unbelievable*.
5. The whole story is *unbelievable*.

---

### unbiased

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbaɪəst/ &middot; *uhn-BY-uhst*
**Русский:** беспристрастный, непредвзятый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unbiased*, *most unbiased*
**Form:** un- + *biased*
**Opposite:** *biased*
**Contrast:** American spelling has one *s*: *unbiased*, not *unbiassed*.

Fair, with no preference for one side over another.

1. We need an *unbiased* account of what happened.
2. No news source is completely *unbiased*.
3. The new study is more *unbiased* than the first one.

---

### unblemished

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈblɛmɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-BLEM-isht*
**Русский:** незапятнанный, безупречный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *blemished*, from *blemish* 'mark, flaw'

With no mark, fault or stain on it.

1. He retired with an *unblemished* record.
2. Her reputation is *unblemished*.
3. They only sell *unblemished* fruit.

---

### unblinking

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈblɪŋkɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-BLING-king*
**Русский:** немигающий; прямой, беспощадно честный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *blinking*

Unblinking has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Staring steadily, without blinking.** (немигающий, пристальный)

1. The cat watched him with an *unblinking* stare.
2. Her eyes were wide and *unblinking*.

**2. Looking straight at something hard or ugly without flinching.** (прямой, беспощадно честный)

3. It's an *unblinking* look at life in the camps.
4. He gave an *unblinking* account of his own mistakes.

---

### unborn

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbɔːrn/ &middot; *uhn-BORN*
**Русский:** нерождённый, ещё не родившийся
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *her unborn child*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *born*

Not yet born; still inside the mother.

1. She worried about her *unborn* baby.
2. The drug can harm an *unborn* child.
3. They argue about the rights of *unborn* children.

---

### unbreakable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbreɪkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-BRAY-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** небьющийся; нерушимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *breakable*, from *break*
**Opposite:** *breakable*, *fragile*

Unbreakable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Impossible to break.** (небьющийся, прочный)

1. These plates are *unbreakable*, which is why we take them camping.
2. The new phone screen is nearly *unbreakable*.
3. Kids' cups come in *unbreakable* plastic.

**2. Impossible to end, break or beat.** (нерушимый; непревзойдённый)

4. The two sisters have an *unbreakable* bond.
5. For thirty years his home run record looked *unbreakable*.
6. She made an *unbreakable* promise to her mother.

---

### unbridled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbraɪdəld/ &middot; *uhn-BRY-duhld*
**Русский:** необузданный, безудержный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *unbridled joy*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *bridled*, from *bridle*, the straps on a horse's head

Not held back or controlled at all.

1. The crowd greeted the team with *unbridled* joy.
2. *Unbridled* spending got the city into trouble.
3. He watched the news with *unbridled* anger.

---

### unbroken

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbroʊkən/ &middot; *uhn-BROH-kuhn*
**Русский:** непрерывный; целый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *broken*, from *break*

Unbroken has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Going on without a stop or a gap.** (непрерывный, сплошной)

1. We drove through miles of *unbroken* desert.
2. The team has an *unbroken* streak of eleven wins.
3. There was an *unbroken* silence in the room.

**2. Not broken or damaged.** (целый, неповреждённый)

4. The seal on the box was still *unbroken*.
5. Only one window was *unbroken* after the storm.

---

### unbuttoned

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈbʌtənd/ &middot; *uhn-BUHT-uhnd*
**Русский:** расстёгнутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *his collar was unbuttoned*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *unbutton*
**Opposite:** *buttoned*

With the buttons undone.

1. His collar was *unbuttoned* and his tie was gone.
2. She came in wearing an *unbuttoned* coat.
3. He sat on the porch with his shirt *unbuttoned*.

---

### uncalled-for

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkɔːld fɔːr/ &middot; *uhn-KAWLD for*
**Русский:** неуместный, незаслуженный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *that was uncalled-for*; also before a noun: *an uncalled-for remark*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — un- + *called for*
**Contrast:** After a verb many Americans write it as two words, *uncalled for*; the hyphen is normal before a noun.

Rude or unfair, and not deserved by anything the other person did.

1. That remark was completely *uncalled-for*.
2. Firing her over one late report was *uncalled-for*.
3. He apologized for his *uncalled-for* comments.

---

### uncanny

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkæni/ &middot; *uhn-KAN-ee*
**Русский:** жутковатый; поразительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncanny*, *most uncanny*

Uncanny has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Strange in a way that is hard to explain and a little unsettling.** (странный, жутковатый)

1. There was an *uncanny* stillness before the storm.
2. I had the *uncanny* feeling that we had met before.

**2. So accurate or so close that it seems impossible.** (поразительный, сверхъестественный)

3. She has an *uncanny* ability to guess what people are thinking.
4. The resemblance between the twins is *uncanny*.
5. His feel for the market is even more *uncanny* than his boss's.

---

### uncaring

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkɛrɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-KAIR-ing*
**Русский:** безразличный, чёрствый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncaring*, *most uncaring*
**Form:** un- + *caring*
**Opposite:** *caring*

Not interested in other people's feelings or troubles.

1. The nurse came across as cold and *uncaring*.
2. He isn't *uncaring* — he just doesn't know what to say.
3. They describe the company as an *uncaring* employer.

---

### uncategorized

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkætəɡəraɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-KAT-uh-guh-ryzd*
**Русский:** неклассифицированный, без категории
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *categorize*; American spelling with *-ize*

Not sorted into any group or category yet.

1. Everything I don't file ends up in an *uncategorized* folder.
2. Half of last month's expenses are still *uncategorized*.
3. The app puts *uncategorized* charges at the bottom of the list.

---

### unceasing

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsiːsɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-SEE-sing*
**Русский:** непрекращающийся, беспрестанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *ceasing*, from *cease*

Going on and on without stopping.

1. The *unceasing* noise from the highway kept me up.
2. She showed *unceasing* devotion to her students.
3. The rain was *unceasing* all weekend.

---

### uncensored

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsɛnsərd/ &middot; *uhn-SEN-surd*
**Русский:** нецензурированный, без купюр
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *censor*
**Opposite:** *censored*

Printed, shown or said with nothing cut out.

1. The streaming service has the *uncensored* version of the movie.
2. His posts are raw and *uncensored*.
3. The late show ran the whole interview *uncensored*.

---

### uncertain

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsɜːrtən/ &middot; *uhn-SUR-tuhn*
**Русский:** неуверенный; неопределённый
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncertain*, *most uncertain*
**Takes:** *about* or *of* + noun or *-ing*; *whether*- or *wh*-clause
**Form:** un- + *certain*
**Opposite:** *certain*, *sure*

Uncertain has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not sure about something.** (не уверенный, сомневающийся)

1. I'm *uncertain* about taking the job.
2. She was *uncertain* whether to call him back.
3. Doctors are still *uncertain* of the cause.

**2. Not known or not settled, and likely to change.** (неопределённый, шаткий)

4. The company faces an *uncertain* future.
5. Funding for the program is *uncertain*.
6. In *uncertain* times people save more.

---

### unchallenged

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtʃælɪndʒd/ &middot; *uhn-CHAL-injd*
**Русский:** неоспоренный, без возражений
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after *go*: *the claim went unchallenged*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *challenge*

Not questioned, argued with or competed against.

1. His story went *unchallenged* for years.
2. The mayor ran *unchallenged* in the primary.
3. That *unchallenged* assumption is where the argument falls apart.

---

### unchanged

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtʃeɪndʒd/ &middot; *uhn-CHAYNJD*
**Русский:** неизменившийся, без изменений
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *the price is unchanged*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *change*
**Contrast:** *Unchanged* says nothing has changed so far; *unchanging* says it never changes.

The same as before.

1. The rules remain *unchanged* this season.
2. Her plans were *unchanged* by the news.
3. Ten years later, the town looks *unchanged*.

---

### unchanging

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtʃeɪndʒɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-CHAYN-jing*
**Русский:** неизменный, постоянный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *change*

Always the same, never changing.

1. The *unchanging* routine of the farm suited him.
2. Her position on this has been *unchanging* for years.
3. Out there the desert sky feels *unchanging*.

---

### uncharacteristic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkɛrəktəˈrɪstɪk/ &middot; *uhn-kair-uhk-tuh-RIS-tik*
**Русский:** нехарактерный, несвойственный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncharacteristic*, *most uncharacteristic*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Form:** un- + *characteristic*

Not the way a person or thing usually is.

1. That kind of rudeness is *uncharacteristic* of him.
2. She made an *uncharacteristic* mistake in the last set.
3. The team's *uncharacteristic* sloppiness cost them the game.

---

### uncharitable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtʃɛrɪtəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-CHAIR-ih-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** немилосердный; нелестный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncharitable*, *most uncharitable*

Unkind in the way it judges someone.

1. It would be *uncharitable* to say she did it on purpose.
2. He made an *uncharitable* remark about his neighbor.
3. That's an *uncharitable* reading of what I wrote.

---

### uncharted

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtʃɑːrtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-CHAR-tid*
**Русский:** неизведанный, неисследованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — almost always before a noun: *uncharted territory*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *chart*, 'to map'

Uncharted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. New to everyone; never experienced before.** (неизведанный, новый для всех)

1. Working from home full-time was *uncharted* territory for the company.
2. This lawsuit takes the industry into *uncharted* waters.

**2. Not shown on any map.** (не нанесённый на карту)

3. Explorers pushed into *uncharted* territory.
4. The reef lies in *uncharted* waters.

---

### unchecked

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtʃɛkt/ &middot; *uhn-CHEKT*
**Русский:** неконтролируемый; непроверенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — before a noun (*unchecked growth*) and after *go* or *leave* (*left unchecked*)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *check*

Unchecked has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not stopped or held back by anything.** (бесконтрольный, ничем не сдерживаемый)

1. *Unchecked* development has swallowed the farmland.
2. If the leak goes *unchecked*, the whole floor will rot.
3. The fire burned *unchecked* for two days.

**2. Not looked at or verified.** (непроверенный)

4. The numbers went into the report *unchecked*.
5. *Unchecked* facts are how rumors start.

---

### uncivil

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsɪvəl/ &middot; *uhn-SIV-uhl*
**Русский:** невежливый, грубый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncivil*, *most uncivil*
**Form:** un- + *civil*
**Contrast:** *Uncivil* is about rude manners; *uncivilized* is about a whole way of living.

Rude, especially in a public discussion.

1. The debate turned *uncivil* in the last ten minutes.
2. He was never cruel, just a little *uncivil* to waiters.
3. *Uncivil* comments get deleted from the forum.

---

### uncivilized

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsɪvəlaɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-SIV-uh-lyzd*
**Русский:** нецивилизованный; варварский
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncivilized*, *most uncivilized*
**Form:** un- + *civilized*
**Contrast:** American spelling *uncivilized*, not *uncivilised*. Half the everyday uses are a joke about something unpleasant, like a very early meeting.

Cruel or crude, below the standard of decent life.

1. A six a.m. meeting is just *uncivilized*.
2. He called the conditions in the jail *uncivilized*.
3. Their *uncivilized* behavior at the game got them thrown out.

---

### unclaimed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkleɪmd/ &middot; *uhn-KLAYMD*
**Русский:** невостребованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *claim*

Not picked up or asked for by the owner or winner.

1. *Unclaimed* bags go to the office downstairs.
2. The prize money is still *unclaimed*.
3. The state is holding millions in *unclaimed* property.

---

### unclassified

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈklæsɪfaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-KLAS-uh-fyd*
**Русский:** несекретный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *classified*
**Opposite:** *classified*
**Contrast:** In government and military use it means 'not secret', the opposite of *classified*, not 'not sorted'.

Not kept secret by the government; open to the public.

1. The report was released in an *unclassified* version.
2. Most of the emails were *unclassified*.
3. He only had access to *unclassified* material.

---

### unclear

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈklɪr/ &middot; *uhn-KLEER*
**Русский:** неясный; не уверенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unclear*, *most unclear*
**Takes:** *about* or *on* + noun; *wh*-clause
**Form:** un- + *clear*
**Opposite:** *clear*

Unclear has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hard to understand, or not known.** (неясный, непонятный)

1. The instructions were *unclear*.
2. It's still *unclear* who will pay for the repairs.
3. The photo is too *unclear* to read the license plate.

**2. Not sure about something.** (не разобравшийся, не уверенный)

4. I'm *unclear* on what you want me to do.
5. They were *unclear* about the deadline.

---

### uncluttered

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈklʌtərd/ &middot; *uhn-KLUHT-urd*
**Русский:** незагромождённый, аккуратный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncluttered*, *most uncluttered*
**Form:** -ed from *clutter*
**Opposite:** *cluttered*

With no unnecessary things in it; neat and simple.

1. Her desk is always *uncluttered*.
2. The app has a clean, *uncluttered* design.
3. I like the *uncluttered* look of the new kitchen.

---

### uncomfortable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkʌmfərtəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-KUHM-fur-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неудобный; неловкий
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncomfortable*, *most uncomfortable*
**Takes:** *with* or *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** un- + *comfortable*
**Opposite:** *comfortable*

Uncomfortable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not pleasant to sit in, wear or be in.** (неудобный)

1. These shoes are *uncomfortable* after an hour.
2. The seats in the back row are even more *uncomfortable*.

**2. Feeling uneasy or embarrassed, or making you feel that way.** (чувствующий неловкость; неловкий, напряжённый)

3. I'm *uncomfortable* talking about money.
4. There was an *uncomfortable* silence after his joke.
5. She felt *uncomfortable* with the whole arrangement.

---

### uncommitted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkəˈmɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-kuh-MIT-id*
**Русский:** не определившийся; не связанный обязательствами
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ed from *commit*

Not yet promised or tied to a side, a plan or a person.

1. A lot of voters are still *uncommitted*.
2. He's *uncommitted* to any one plan until he sees the numbers.
3. The *uncommitted* delegates will decide the nomination.

---

### uncommon

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkɑːmən/ &middot; *uhn-KAH-muhn*
**Русский:** редкий, необычный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncommon*, *most uncommon*
**Form:** un- + *common*
**Opposite:** *common*
**Contrast:** *It is not uncommon for...* is a quiet way of saying something happens fairly often.

Not happening or found often.

1. Snow in April is *uncommon* here, but it happens.
2. It's not *uncommon* for flights to be delayed in July.
3. She has an *uncommon* talent for languages.

---

### uncommunicative

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkəˈmjuːnɪkətɪv/ &middot; *uhn-kuh-MYOO-nih-kuh-tiv*
**Русский:** неразговорчивый, замкнутый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncommunicative*, *most uncommunicative*
**Form:** un- + *communicative*

Not willing to talk or to share information.

1. He gets *uncommunicative* when he's tired.
2. The witness was *uncommunicative* for the first hour.
3. She's been distant and *uncommunicative* all week.

---

### uncompetitive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkəmˈpɛtətɪv/ &middot; *uhn-kuhm-PET-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** неконкурентоспособный
**Type:** quality — about business and sports
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncompetitive*, *most uncompetitive*
**Form:** un- + *competitive*

Not good enough to compete with others on price or quality.

1. Our prices are *uncompetitive* next to the big chains.
2. High costs have made the plant *uncompetitive*.
3. An *uncompetitive* bid never gets a second look.

---

### uncomplicated

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkɑːmplɪkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-KAHM-plih-kay-tid*
**Русский:** несложный, простой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncomplicated*, *most uncomplicated*
**Form:** un- + *complicated*
**Opposite:** *complicated*

Simple; without difficult parts.

1. The recipe is quick and *uncomplicated*.
2. He wanted an *uncomplicated* life in a small town.
3. Her surgery was *uncomplicated* and she went home the same day.

---

### uncompromising

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkɑːmprəmaɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-KAHM-pruh-my-zing*
**Русский:** бескомпромиссный, непреклонный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncompromising*, *most uncompromising*
**Takes:** *in* or *about* + noun
**Form:** un- + *compromising*, from *compromise*

Refusing to change your position or lower your standards.

1. She is *uncompromising* about safety.
2. The coach has an *uncompromising* style.
3. He was *uncompromising* in his demands.

---

### unconcerned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈsɜːrnd/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-SURND*
**Русский:** равнодушный, невозмутимый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unconcerned*, *most unconcerned*
**Takes:** *about* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *concern*
**Opposite:** *concerned*

Not worried about something, or not interested in it.

1. He seemed *unconcerned* about the deadline.
2. The cat looked completely *unconcerned*.
3. They're *unconcerned* with what the neighbors think.

---

### unconditional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈdɪʃənəl/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-DISH-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** безоговорочный, безусловный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *conditional*, from *condition*
**Opposite:** *conditional*

Given with no conditions or limits at all.

1. A parent's love should be *unconditional*.
2. The Allies demanded *unconditional* surrender.
3. The offer comes with an *unconditional* money-back guarantee.

---

### unconfirmed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈfɜːrmd/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-FURMD*
**Русский:** неподтверждённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *confirm*

Reported but not yet proved to be true.

1. There are *unconfirmed* reports of a second explosion.
2. The rumor about the merger is still *unconfirmed*.
3. We had *unconfirmed* word that the deal had fallen through.

---

### unconnected

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkəˈnɛktɪd/ &middot; *uhn-kuh-NEK-tid*
**Русский:** не связанный, не имеющий отношения
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *connect*

Having no link to something else.

1. The two cases are *unconnected*.
2. His firing was *unconnected* to the audit.
3. She raised a string of *unconnected* complaints.

---

### unconscionable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkɑːnʃənəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-KAHN-shuh-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** бессовестный, недопустимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unconscionable*, *most unconscionable*
**Form:** un- + an old adjective from *conscience*
**Contrast:** Formal and strong. In law an *unconscionable* contract is one so unfair that a court will not enforce it.

So unfair or so wrong that no decent person could accept it.

1. Charging that much for insulin is *unconscionable*.
2. The delay put an *unconscionable* burden on families.
3. It would be *unconscionable* to look the other way.

---

### unconscious

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkɑːnʃəs/ &middot; *uhn-KAHN-shuhs*
**Русский:** без сознания; неосознанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — the 'not aware' sense takes *more/most*
**Takes:** *of* + noun, in the 'not aware' sense
**Form:** un- + *conscious*
**Opposite:** *conscious*
**Contrast:** For the hidden part of the mind everyday American English says *subconscious*: *a subconscious fear*.

Unconscious has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not awake and not able to feel or think, usually after an injury.** (без сознания)

1. The driver was *unconscious* when the medics arrived.
2. She hit her head and was *unconscious* for a minute.

**2. Happening without the person knowing it.** (неосознанный, бессознательный)

3. The training is about *unconscious* bias.
4. *Unconscious* habits are the hardest ones to change.

**3. Not aware of something.** (не сознающий, не замечающий)

5. He was *unconscious* of the effect his words had.
6. They went on talking, *unconscious* of being overheard.

---

### unconstitutional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkɑːnstɪˈtuːʃənəl/ &middot; *uhn-kahn-stih-TOO-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** неконституционный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *constitutional*, from *constitution*

Against the Constitution, and so not allowed.

1. The court ruled the law *unconstitutional*.
2. Critics called the order *unconstitutional*.
3. An *unconstitutional* statute cannot be enforced.

---

### unconstrained

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈstreɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-STRAYND*
**Русский:** ничем не ограниченный, свободный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *constrain*

Not limited by rules, money or anything else.

1. Spending was *unconstrained* by any real budget.
2. She writes with *unconstrained* energy.
3. *Unconstrained* growth put a strain on the water supply.

---

### uncontested

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈtɛstɪd/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-TES-tid*
**Русский:** неоспариваемый; без соперников
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *contest*

With nobody running against it or arguing against it.

1. The mayor's seat was *uncontested* this year.
2. They filed for an *uncontested* divorce.
3. His claim to the record is *uncontested*.

---

### uncontrollable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈtroʊləbəl/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-TROH-luh-buhl*
**Русский:** неудержимый, неконтролируемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncontrollable*, *most uncontrollable*
**Form:** un- + *controllable*, from *control*
**Contrast:** *Uncontrolled* means nobody is controlling it; *uncontrollable* means nobody can.

Impossible to control or stop.

1. She broke into *uncontrollable* laughter.
2. The fire was *uncontrollable* by noon.
3. He has an *uncontrollable* temper.

---

### uncontrolled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈtroʊld/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-TROHLD*
**Русский:** бесконтрольный; неконтролируемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *control*

Not being watched, limited or treated by anyone.

1. *Uncontrolled* diabetes can damage your eyes.
2. The paper compares controlled and *uncontrolled* trials.
3. *Uncontrolled* spending drained the account in a year.

---

### uncontroversial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkɑːntrəˈvɜːrʃəl/ &middot; *uhn-kahn-truh-VUR-shuhl*
**Русский:** не вызывающий споров
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncontroversial*, *most uncontroversial*
**Form:** un- + *controversial*

Accepted by almost everyone; not causing argument.

1. The bill was *uncontroversial* and passed in a day.
2. It's an *uncontroversial* claim among biologists.
3. He picked an *uncontroversial* topic for the speech.

---

### unconventional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈvɛnʃənəl/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-VEN-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** нетрадиционный, необычный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unconventional*, *most unconventional*
**Form:** un- + *conventional*
**Opposite:** *conventional*

Not done the usual way.

1. They took an *unconventional* route to the top.
2. Her teaching methods are *unconventional*.
3. He's an *unconventional* dresser, even for an artist.

---

### unconvinced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈvɪnst/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-VINST*
**Русский:** не убеждённый, сомневающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after *be*, *look*, *remain*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* or *of* + noun; *that*-clause
**Form:** -ed from *convince*
**Contrast:** *Unconvinced* describes the listener; *unconvincing* describes the argument.

Not persuaded that something is true or right.

1. The jury looked *unconvinced*.
2. I'm *unconvinced* by his explanation.
3. She remains *unconvinced* that the plan will work.

---

### unconvincing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkənˈvɪnsɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-kuhn-VIN-sing*
**Русский:** неубедительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unconvincing*, *most unconvincing*
**Form:** -ing from *convince*
**Opposite:** *convincing*

Not good enough to make people believe it.

1. His excuse was *unconvincing*.
2. The movie's happy ending is *unconvincing*.
3. She gave an *unconvincing* smile and looked away.

---

### uncooked

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkʊkt/ &middot; *uhn-KUUKT*
**Русский:** сырой, неприготовленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *cook*
**Opposite:** *cooked*
**Contrast:** For meat and fish Americans usually say *raw*; *uncooked* is the normal word for rice, pasta and dough.

Not cooked yet.

1. Measure the rice *uncooked*, not after it swells.
2. Don't eat *uncooked* cookie dough.
3. The center of the chicken was still *uncooked*.

---

### uncool

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkuːl/ &middot; *uhn-KOOL*
**Русский:** немодный; некрасивый (о поступке)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncool*, *most uncool*
**Form:** un- + *cool*
**Opposite:** *cool*
**Contrast:** Informal and mostly spoken. The second sense is about behavior, not fashion.

Uncool has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not fashionable; embarrassing to be seen with.** (немодный, несолидный)

1. Cargo shorts were *uncool* for about a decade.
2. He was sure his mom's minivan made him *uncool*.

**2. Not an okay thing to do to someone.** (некрасивый, нечестный)

3. Reading her texts was really *uncool*.
4. It's *uncool* to bail on your friends at the last minute.

---

### uncooperative

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkoʊˈɑːpərətɪv/ &middot; *uhn-koh-AH-puh-ruh-tiv*
**Русский:** несговорчивый, отказывающийся сотрудничать
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncooperative*, *most uncooperative*
**Form:** un- + *cooperative*
**Opposite:** *cooperative*

Not willing to help or to work with others.

1. The witness was *uncooperative*.
2. My laptop is being *uncooperative* this morning.
3. He turned *uncooperative* as soon as lawyers got involved.

---

### uncoordinated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkoʊˈɔːrdɪneɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-koh-OR-dih-nay-tid*
**Русский:** неуклюжий; несогласованный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncoordinated*, *most uncoordinated*
**Form:** -ed from *coordinate*

Uncoordinated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Clumsy; bad at controlling your own movements.** (неуклюжий, неловкий)

1. I was the most *uncoordinated* kid in gym class.
2. He's tall and a little *uncoordinated*.

**2. Not organized so that the parts work together.** (несогласованный, нескоординированный)

3. The relief effort was slow and *uncoordinated*.
4. *Uncoordinated* agencies did the same work twice.

---

### uncorroborated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnkəˈrɑːbəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-kuh-RAH-buh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** неподтверждённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *corroborate*

Not backed up by other evidence or another witness.

1. The case rests on *uncorroborated* testimony.
2. His alibi is still *uncorroborated*.
3. The paper won't run an *uncorroborated* claim.

---

### uncouth

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkuːθ/ &middot; *uhn-KOOTH*
**Русский:** неотёсанный, грубый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncouth*, *most uncouth*

Rude and rough in manners or speech.

1. Talking with your mouth full is *uncouth*.
2. His *uncouth* jokes cleared the room.
3. She thought the whole crowd was loud and *uncouth*.

---

### uncritical

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkrɪtɪkəl/ &middot; *uhn-KRIT-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** некритичный, безоговорочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uncritical*, *most uncritical*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Form:** un- + *critical*

Accepting something without asking whether it is right or true.

1. His fans are completely *uncritical*.
2. *Uncritical* acceptance of a source is how mistakes spread.
3. She is *uncritical* of anything her own party does.

---

### uncut

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkʌt/ &middot; *uhn-KUHT*
**Русский:** полный, без купюр; необработанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed form of *cut*, which does not change

Uncut has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shown or printed in full, with nothing taken out.** (полный, без сокращений)

1. The DVD has the *uncut* version of the film.
2. They aired his speech *uncut*.

**2. Not cut, trimmed or shaped.** (необрезанный, неогранённый)

3. He bought an *uncut* diamond at an estate sale.
4. The lawn was *uncut* for a month.

---

### undamaged

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdæmɪdʒd/ &middot; *uhn-DAM-ijd*
**Русский:** неповреждённый, целый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *damage*

Not harmed or broken in any way.

1. The package arrived *undamaged*.
2. Only one wall was left *undamaged* by the fire.
3. *Undamaged* copies of the first edition are rare now.

---

### undaunted

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdɔːntɪd/ &middot; *uhn-DAWN-tid*
**Русский:** неустрашимый, не павший духом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb, or at the head of a clause: *Undaunted, she tried again*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *daunt*, 'to discourage'

Not discouraged by difficulty or failure.

1. She was *undaunted* by the first rejection.
2. *Undaunted*, they set out again at dawn.
3. He seems *undaunted* by the size of the job.

---

### undecided

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈsaɪdɪd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-SY-did*
**Русский:** не решивший(ся); нерешённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*; *whether*-clause
**Form:** -ed from *decide*
**Contrast:** In politics *the undecided* is also a noun: *the undecideds broke late*.

Undecided has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not having made a choice yet.** (не определившийся)

1. I'm still *undecided* about the trip.
2. Ten percent of voters are *undecided*.
3. She was *undecided* whether to major in biology.

**2. Not settled or finished.** (нерешённый, неопределившийся)

4. The game was *undecided* until the last minute.
5. The question of who pays is still *undecided*.

---

### undeclared

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈklɛrd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-KLAIRD*
**Русский:** необъявленный; незадекларированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *declare*

Not announced or reported to the authorities.

1. He came home with *undeclared* goods in his suitcase.
2. It was an *undeclared* war that lasted ten years.
3. Her major is still *undeclared*.

---

### undefeated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈfiːtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-FEE-tid*
**Русский:** непобеждённый, без поражений
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *defeat*

Having never lost.

1. The team went *undefeated* all season.
2. She's *undefeated* in twelve fights.
3. They face the last *undefeated* team in the league.

---

### undefined

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈfaɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-FYND*
**Русский:** неопределённый, нечёткий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *define*

Not given clear limits or a clear meaning.

1. His role on the project is *undefined*.
2. The contract leaves the term *undefined*.
3. There was an *undefined* ache in her shoulder.

---

### undemanding

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈmændɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-dih-MAN-ding*
**Русский:** нетребовательный; необременительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more undemanding*, *most undemanding*
**Form:** -ing from *demand*
**Opposite:** *demanding*

Needing little effort, or asking little of other people.

1. It's an *undemanding* job with steady hours.
2. He's an *undemanding* houseguest.
3. I wanted something light and *undemanding* to read on the flight.

---

### undemocratic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɛməˈkrætɪk/ &middot; *uhn-dem-uh-KRAT-ik*
**Русский:** недемократичный
**Type:** evaluative — about politics and process
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more undemocratic*, *most undemocratic*
**Form:** un- + *democratic*

Against the idea that people should have an equal say.

1. Critics called the new rule *undemocratic*.
2. It's *undemocratic* to count some votes and not others.
3. The party's *undemocratic* primary drew complaints.

---

### undeniable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈnaɪəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-dih-NY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** неоспоримый, несомненный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *deniable*, from *deny*

So clearly true that nobody can argue with it.

1. Her talent is *undeniable*.
2. There is an *undeniable* link between the two events.
3. The evidence on the tape was *undeniable*.

---

### under fire

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndər ˈfaɪər/ &middot; *uhn-dur FY-ur*
**Русский:** под огнём критики; под обстрелом
**Type:** quality — about being attacked or criticized
**Position:** predicative only — after *be*, *come*, *fall*: *the mayor came under fire*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *for* + noun or *-ing*; *from* + the people attacking
**Form:** fixed phrase — never before a noun in American English

Under fire has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Being strongly criticized.** (под огнём критики)

1. The airline is *under fire* for canceling flights.
2. She came *under fire* from both parties.
3. He stayed calm while *under fire* from reporters.

**2. Being shot at.** (под обстрелом)

4. The unit was *under fire* for three hours.
5. Medics worked *under fire* to reach the wounded.

---

### under the weather

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndər ðə ˈwɛðər/ &middot; *uhn-dur thuh WETH-ur*
**Русский:** нездоровый, приболевший
**Type:** quality — about health
**Position:** predicative only — after *be* or *feel*, never before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** idiom — a fixed phrase, informal

A little sick, but not seriously ill.

1. I'm feeling a little *under the weather* today.
2. She was *under the weather* all last week.
3. He called in and said he was *under the weather*.

---

### under-the-table

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndər ðə ˈteɪbəl/ &middot; *uhn-dur-thuh-TAY-buhl*
**Русский:** неофициальный, из-под полы
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun; after a verb it is the adverb phrase *under the table*: *they paid him under the table*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — preposition + noun phrase, hyphenated before a noun

Paid or done secretly, outside the official records.

1. He got *under-the-table* payments for years.
2. They made an *under-the-table* deal with the contractor.
3. *Under-the-table* wages leave workers with no benefits.

---

### underage

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈeɪdʒ/ &middot; *uhn-dur-AYJ*
**Русский:** несовершеннолетний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — before a noun (*underage drinking*) and after a verb (*he's underage*)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** under- + *age*
**Contrast:** American English writes it as one word, *underage*, not *under-age*.

Too young to be legally allowed to do something.

1. *Underage* drinking is a big problem on campus.
2. The bar lost its license for serving *underage* customers.
3. You can't get in — you're *underage*.

---

### undercover

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈkʌvər/ &middot; *uhn-dur-KUHV-ur*
**Русский:** тайный, под прикрытием
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In *She worked undercover for two years* the word is an adverb, not an adjective.

Working or done secretly, with a false identity.

1. An *undercover* officer made the buy.
2. The reporter ran an *undercover* investigation.
3. Two of the agents in the room were *undercover*.

---

### underdeveloped

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərdɪˈvɛləpt/ &middot; *uhn-dur-dih-VEL-uhpt*
**Русский:** недоразвитый, недостаточно развитый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** under- + *developed*
**Contrast:** Of countries, Americans now say *developing*; *underdeveloped* sounds dated and rude.

Not grown or worked out as much as it should be.

1. The character's backstory feels *underdeveloped*.
2. *Underdeveloped* muscles make that injury more likely.
3. The film came out light because it was *underdeveloped*.

---

### underdressed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈdrɛst/ &middot; *uhn-dur-DREST*
**Русский:** слишком просто одетый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after *be*, *feel*, *look*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** under- + *dressed*
**Opposite:** *overdressed*

Dressed too casually for the occasion.

1. I showed up in jeans and felt *underdressed*.
2. Everyone else wore a suit, so he was badly *underdressed*.
3. You won't be *underdressed* in that dress.

---

### underemployed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərɪmˈplɔɪd/ &middot; *uhn-dur-im-PLOYD*
**Русский:** работающий не по квалификации; частично занятый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** under- + *employed*
**Contrast:** *Unemployed* is having no job at all; *underemployed* is having too little work, or work below your training.

Working fewer hours than you want, or at a job below your skills.

1. Many graduates are *underemployed* for years.
2. The county has a large *underemployed* workforce.
3. He isn't unemployed, just badly *underemployed*.

---

### underground

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌndərɡraʊnd/ &middot; *UHN-dur-grownd*
**Русский:** подземный; подпольный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun; after a verb it is the adverb, stressed at the end: *the pipes run underground*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The American word for the London *Underground* is the *subway*.

Underground has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Below the surface of the ground.** (подземный)

1. The building has an *underground* parking garage.
2. An *underground* stream feeds the pond.

**2. Secret, or outside the official mainstream.** (подпольный, андеграундный)

3. They ran an *underground* newspaper for a year.
4. The band came out of the *underground* punk scene.

---

### underhanded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈhændɪd/ &middot; *uhn-dur-HAN-did*
**Русский:** закулисный, нечестный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more underhanded*, *most underhanded*

Secret and dishonest.

1. They won the contract in an *underhanded* way.
2. That was an *underhanded* trick to pull on a friend.
3. His tactics struck everyone as *underhanded*.

---

### underlying

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈlaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-dur-LY-ing*
**Русский:** лежащий в основе; скрытый
**Type:** participial
