**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *the underlying cause*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *underlie*

Lying under the surface and causing what you actually see.

1. The doctor looked for an *underlying* condition.
2. Nobody dealt with the *underlying* problem.
3. There's an *underlying* tension in the office.

---

### undernourished

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈnɜːrɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-dur-NUR-isht*
**Русский:** недоедающий, истощённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** under- + *nourished*, from *nourish*

Not getting enough food, or enough of the right food.

1. The puppy was thin and *undernourished*.
2. *Undernourished* children fall behind in school.
3. He looked pale and *undernourished* after the hike.

---

### undersized

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈsaɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-dur-SYZD*
**Русский:** меньше нормы, маломерный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** under- + *sized*, from *size*
**Opposite:** *oversized*

Smaller than the normal or legal size.

1. *Undersized* fish have to be thrown back.
2. He played linebacker even though he was *undersized*.
3. They crammed everything into an *undersized* closet.

---

### understaffed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈstæft/ &middot; *uhn-dur-STAFT*
**Русский:** недоукомплектованный персоналом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** under- + *staffed*, from *staff*

Not having enough workers.

1. The night shift is badly *understaffed*.
2. *Understaffed* hospitals can't keep up with the wait times.
3. We were *understaffed* all summer.

---

### understandable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈstændəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-dur-STAN-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** понятный; объяснимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more understandable*, *most understandable*
**Form:** -able from *understand*

Understandable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Natural and easy to sympathize with.** (объяснимый, естественный)

1. Her anger was completely *understandable*.
2. It's *understandable* that he wants more time.
3. That's an *understandable* mistake for a first-year teacher.

**2. Able to be understood.** (понятный, доходчивый)

4. Write the instructions so they're *understandable* to a beginner.
5. The line was bad and he was barely *understandable*.

---

### understanding

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈstændɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-dur-STAN-ding*
**Русский:** понимающий, отзывчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more understanding*, *most understanding*
**Form:** -ing from *understand*
**Contrast:** *An understanding* is also a noun meaning an informal agreement: *we have an understanding*.

Kind and willing to forgive, because you see why someone acted that way.

1. My boss was very *understanding* about the delay.
2. She's an *understanding* teacher.
3. Try to be *understanding* — he's had a hard week.

---

### understated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈsteɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-dur-STAY-tid*
**Русский:** сдержанный, неброский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more understated*, *most understated*
**Form:** -ed from *understate*

Quiet and simple in style, with nothing showy about it.

1. The dress was elegant and *understated*.
2. He gave an *understated* performance as the father.
3. Their new logo is more *understated* than the old one.

---

### underwater

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈwɔːtər/ &middot; *uhn-dur-WAW-tur*
**Русский:** подводный; (о долге) превышающий стоимость
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun; in *swim underwater* it is an adverb
**Comparison:** not gradable

Underwater has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Below the surface of the water; made for use there.** (подводный)

1. He bought an *underwater* camera for the trip.
2. They shot the *underwater* scenes in a tank.

**2. Owing more on a loan than the thing is worth.** (с долгом больше стоимости залога)

3. A lot of homeowners were *underwater* on their mortgages in 2009.
4. She's *underwater* on the car loan and can't sell it.

---

### underway

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈweɪ/ &middot; *uhn-dur-WAY*
**Русский:** идущий, начавшийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after *be* or *get*: *the project is underway*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Americans usually write it as one word; the older spelling *under way*, as two words, still shows up in careful prose.

Already started and going on now.

1. Construction is already *underway*.
2. The search got *underway* at dawn.
3. Talks are *underway* to settle the strike.

---

### underweight

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndərˈweɪt/ &middot; *uhn-dur-WAYT*
**Русский:** с недостаточным весом
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** under- + *weight*
**Opposite:** *overweight*

Weighing less than is healthy or normal.

1. The vet says the cat is *underweight*.
2. He was ten pounds *underweight* at his checkup.
3. *Underweight* babies need extra monitoring.

---

### undeserved

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈzɜːrvd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-ZURVD*
**Русский:** незаслуженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *deserve*
**Contrast:** *Undeserved* describes the thing (*undeserved praise*); *undeserving* describes the person (*undeserving of praise*).

Not earned or fair.

1. The penalty was completely *undeserved*.
2. She got an *undeserved* reputation for being difficult.
3. *Undeserved* praise doesn't help anyone get better.

---

### undeserving

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈzɜːrvɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-dih-ZUR-ving*
**Русский:** недостойный, не заслуживающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Form:** -ing from *deserve*

Not worthy of the help, praise or reward in question.

1. He felt *undeserving* of the award.
2. Politicians still talk about the deserving and the *undeserving* poor.
3. Nobody in this room is *undeserving* of help.

---

### undesirable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈzaɪərəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-dih-ZY-ruh-buhl*
**Русский:** нежелательный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more undesirable*, *most undesirable*
**Form:** un- + *desirable*, from *desire*
**Opposite:** *desirable*

Not wanted, because it brings harm or trouble.

1. The drug has some *undesirable* side effects.
2. Building a highway there would be *undesirable*.
3. The screening weeds out *undesirable* applicants.

---

### undetectable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈtɛktəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-dih-TEK-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** необнаружимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *detectable*, from *detect*

Impossible to find or notice.

1. The virus was *undetectable* in his blood.
2. The repair on the frame is almost *undetectable*.
3. They used an *undetectable* dye to mark the bills.

---

### undetected

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈtɛktɪd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-TEK-tid*
**Русский:** необнаруженный, незамеченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after *go*: *the leak went undetected*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *detect*
**Contrast:** *Undetectable* means nobody could find it; *undetected* means nobody did.

Not found or noticed, though it was there.

1. The leak went *undetected* for months.
2. *Undetected* errors made it into the final report.
3. He slipped past the guards *undetected*.

---

### undetermined

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈtɜːrmɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-TUR-mind*
**Русский:** невыясненный, неустановленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *determine*

Not yet worked out or decided.

1. The cause of the fire is still *undetermined*.
2. They'll meet again at an *undetermined* date.
3. The cost of the repairs is as yet *undetermined*.

---

### undeterred

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈtɜːrd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-TURD*
**Русский:** не остановленный трудностями
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb, or at the head of a clause: *Undeterred, he called again*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *deter*

Not stopped or put off by something unpleasant.

1. She was *undeterred* by the rain and ran anyway.
2. *Undeterred*, they filed the lawsuit a second time.
3. Fans seem *undeterred* by the ticket prices.

---

### undeveloped

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈvɛləpt/ &middot; *uhn-dih-VEL-uhpt*
**Русский:** неосвоенный, незастроенный; неразвитый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *develop*
**Contrast:** *Undeveloped* land has nothing built on it; *underdeveloped* means something grew less than it should have.

Undeveloped has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With nothing built on it yet.** (незастроенный, неосвоенный)

1. They bought ten acres of *undeveloped* land.
2. The lot next door is still *undeveloped*.

**2. Not worked on or grown to its full strength.** (неразвитый, нераскрытый)

3. His talent is real but *undeveloped*.
4. The idea is interesting and completely *undeveloped*.

---

### undiagnosed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndaɪəɡˈnoʊst/ &middot; *uhn-dy-uhg-NOHST*
**Русский:** недиагностированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after *go*: *it went undiagnosed for years*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *diagnose*

Not identified by a doctor.

1. Her celiac disease went *undiagnosed* for years.
2. *Undiagnosed* sleep apnea is more common than people think.
3. The condition is often *undiagnosed* in women.

---

### undignified

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdɪɡnɪfaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-DIG-nih-fyd*
**Русский:** недостойный, унизительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more undignified*, *most undignified*
**Form:** un- + *dignified*, from *dignity*
**Opposite:** *dignified*

Making someone look silly or lose respect.

1. There was an *undignified* scramble for the last seats.
2. He thought arguing on television was *undignified*.
3. She made an *undignified* exit through the kitchen.

---

### undiplomatic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪpləˈmætɪk/ &middot; *uhn-dip-luh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** нетактичный, недипломатичный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more undiplomatic*, *most undiplomatic*
**Form:** un- + *diplomatic*

Saying things in a way that offends people.

1. That was an *undiplomatic* thing to say to a client.
2. He can be blunt to the point of being *undiplomatic*.
3. Her *undiplomatic* email cost the team a week of apologies.

---

### undisciplined

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdɪsəplɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-DIS-uh-plind*
**Русский:** недисциплинированный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more undisciplined*, *most undisciplined*
**Form:** un- + *disciplined*, from *discipline*
**Opposite:** *disciplined*

Not controlling yourself or following the rules.

1. The team looked *undisciplined* in the first half.
2. He's a talented but *undisciplined* writer.
3. *Undisciplined* spending sank the business.

---

### undisclosed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪsˈkloʊzd/ &middot; *uhn-dis-KLOHZD*
**Русский:** неразглашаемый, нераскрытый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *an undisclosed amount*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *disclose*

Deliberately not made public.

1. The company was sold for an *undisclosed* amount.
2. He is being treated at an *undisclosed* location.
3. The terms of the settlement remain *undisclosed*.

---

### undiscovered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪsˈkʌvərd/ &middot; *uhn-dis-KUHV-urd*
**Русский:** неоткрытый, необнаруженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *discover*

Not found or recognized yet.

1. Thousands of species are still *undiscovered*.
2. She spent years as an *undiscovered* singer playing bars.
3. The error lay *undiscovered* until the audit.

---

### undisputed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈspjuːtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-SPYOO-tid*
**Русский:** бесспорный, неоспоримый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *dispute*

Accepted by everyone; not argued about.

1. She's the *undisputed* champion.
2. These facts are *undisputed* by either side.
3. He is the *undisputed* leader of the group.

---

### undisturbed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈstɜːrbd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-STURBD*
**Русский:** нетронутый; непотревоженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *disturb*

Undisturbed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not touched or moved.** (нетронутый)

1. The snow in the yard was *undisturbed*.
2. Leave the nest *undisturbed* until the birds fly.

**2. Not interrupted or worried.** (непотревоженный, невозмутимый)

3. He slept *undisturbed* through the storm.
4. She seemed *undisturbed* by the criticism.

---

### undivided

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndɪˈvaɪdɪd/ &middot; *uhn-dih-VY-did*
**Русский:** безраздельный, полный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *my undivided attention*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *divide*

Whole, given to one thing only.

1. You have my *undivided* attention.
2. The plan had the board's *undivided* support.
3. He gave the project his *undivided* focus for a month.

---

### undocumented

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈdɑːkjəmɛntɪd/ &middot; *uhn-DAH-kyuh-men-tid*
**Русский:** не имеющий документов; незадокументированный
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *documented*
**Opposite:** *documented*
**Contrast:** *Undocumented* is the neutral term in American news writing for a person without legal papers; *illegal immigrant* is dated and many people find it offensive, and *an illegal* as a noun more so.

Undocumented has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Living or working in a country without the legal papers that allow it.** (не имеющий документов, нелегальный (об иммигранте))

1. *Undocumented* workers make up a big share of the farm labor force.
2. She has been here since she was six, but she is still *undocumented*.
3. The bill offers a path to citizenship for *undocumented* immigrants.

**2. Not written down, recorded or backed by evidence.** (незадокументированный, ничем не подтверждённый)

4. The app has a few *undocumented* features.
5. His claims about the money are completely *undocumented*.

---

### undone

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdʌn/ &middot; *uhn-DUN*
**Русский:** несделанный; расстёгнутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb: *leave it undone*, *came undone*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *done*
**Opposite:** *done*
**Contrast:** *Come undone* is the usual phrase for something that unties or unbuttons itself, and, of a person, for falling apart emotionally: *He came undone after the verdict*.

Undone has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not finished; not taken care of.** (несделанный, незаконченный)

1. I hate leaving a job *undone*.
2. There is still a lot *undone* before the guests arrive.
3. Half the paperwork was left *undone*.

**2. No longer tied or buttoned.** (расстёгнутый, развязанный)

4. Your shoelace has come *undone*.
5. One button was *undone* the whole time.

---

### undressed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdrɛst/ &middot; *uhn-DREST*
**Русский:** раздетый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after *be* or *get*; before a noun use *naked*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *dressed*
**Opposite:** *dressed*
**Contrast:** *Get undressed* is the everyday way to say *раздеться*; the plain verb (*She undressed*) sounds literary. A wound that is *undressed* is one with no bandage on it.

With your clothes off.

1. Get *undressed* and put this gown on.
2. He was still *undressed* when the doorbell rang.
3. The kids got *undressed* for bed without being asked.

---

### undue

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈduː/ &middot; *uhn-DOO*
**Русский:** чрезмерный, излишний
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *undue delay*, *undue influence*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *due*, in the old sense 'proper, owed'
**Contrast:** *Undue* is formal and goes only before a noun; in speech say *too much*: *too much pressure*. The adverb *unduly* is common (*unduly harsh*). Do not confuse it with the verb *undo* or with *overdue* (late).

More than is necessary or reasonable.

1. The court found no evidence of *undue* influence.
2. Please proceed without *undue* delay.
3. She worried about putting *undue* pressure on her son.

---

### undying

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-DY-ing*
**Русский:** вечный, неувядающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *undying love*, *undying gratitude*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *dying*

Lasting forever; never coming to an end.

1. He swore his *undying* love.
2. You have my *undying* gratitude.
3. The band still has the *undying* loyalty of its fans.

---

### unearned

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɜːrnd/ &middot; *uhn-URND*
**Русский:** нетрудовой; незаслуженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *earned*
**Opposite:** *earned*

Unearned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Coming from investments or gifts rather than from work.** (нетрудовой (о доходе))

1. *Unearned* income is taxed at a different rate.
2. Most of his money is *unearned*.

**2. Not deserved.** (незаслуженный)

3. The praise felt *unearned*.
4. He has an *unearned* reputation for toughness.

---

### uneasy

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈiːzi/ &middot; *uhn-EE-zee*
**Русский:** тревожный, неспокойный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uneasy*, *most uneasy*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*; *with* + noun
**Form:** un- + *easy*, in the old sense 'at ease'
**Contrast:** *Uneasy* is not the opposite of *easy*: it means anxious or uncomfortable, never 'difficult'. For a task that is not easy say *hard* or *difficult*.

Uneasy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Worried; not comfortable about something.** (тревожный, испытывающий беспокойство)

1. I feel *uneasy* about signing anything today.
2. She grew *uneasy* as the sun went down.
3. There was an *uneasy* silence at the table.

**2. Not settled or secure; likely to break down.** (непрочный, шаткий)

4. The two sides reached an *uneasy* truce.
5. It is an *uneasy* alliance at best.

---

### unedited

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɛdɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-ED-i-tid*
**Русский:** неотредактированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *edited*
**Opposite:** *edited*

Printed, posted or shown exactly as it was written or recorded.

1. The site posted the *unedited* transcript.
2. This is raw, *unedited* footage from the scene.
3. He sent the chapter in *unedited*.

---

### uneducated

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɛdʒəkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-EJ-uh-kay-tid*
**Русский:** необразованный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uneducated*, *most uneducated*
**Form:** un- + *educated*
**Opposite:** *educated*
**Contrast:** Calling a person *uneducated* sounds like an insult in American English; about schooling itself say *has no college degree* or *did not finish high school*. An *uneducated guess* is a joke on *an educated guess* — a guess with nothing behind it.

Having had little or no schooling.

1. Her grandparents were poor and largely *uneducated*.
2. It is a mistake to assume that an *uneducated* person is a stupid one.
3. Millions of children in the region are still *uneducated*.

---

### unemotional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪˈmoʊʃənəl/ &middot; *uhn-i-MOH-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** бесстрастный, сдержанный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unemotional*, *most unemotional*
**Form:** un- + *emotional*
**Opposite:** *emotional*

Not showing feelings; calm and matter-of-fact.

1. He gave a flat, *unemotional* account of the accident.
2. She stayed *unemotional* through the whole hearing.
3. His voice was *unemotional*, almost bored.

---

### unemployable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪmˈplɔɪəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-im-PLOY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** непригодный для найма
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unemployable*, *most unemployable*
**Form:** un- + *employable*
**Opposite:** *employable*

Not able to get a job, because of a lack of skills, poor health or a record.

1. A felony conviction can leave someone effectively *unemployable*.
2. He was declared *unemployable* after the injury.
3. Without English many of them are *unemployable* here.

---

### unencumbered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈkʌmbərd/ &middot; *uhn-in-KUM-burd*
**Русский:** необременённый, свободный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — before a noun, or after a verb with *by*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** un- + *encumbered*

Free from anything that weighs you down — debts, duties or baggage.

1. He travels light, *unencumbered* by luggage.
2. The property is sold *unencumbered* by any mortgage.
3. She liked being young and *unencumbered*.

---

### unending

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɛndɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-EN-ding*
**Русский:** бесконечный, нескончаемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *an unending stream*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *ending*
**Contrast:** *Unending* and *endless* mean the same thing; *endless* is the everyday word and *unending* the more literary one.

Going on and on with no sign of stopping.

1. There is an *unending* stream of email in my inbox.
2. The summer brought *unending* heat.
3. She has an *unending* supply of stories.

---

### unenforceable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈfɔːrsəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-in-FOR-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** невыполнимый; не имеющий исковой силы
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *enforceable*
**Opposite:** *enforceable*

Impossible to make people obey, or not valid in court.

1. The judge ruled the noncompete clause *unenforceable*.
2. It is a nice rule, but it is *unenforceable*.
3. Such a ban would be practically *unenforceable*.

---

### unenthusiastic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnθuːziˈæstɪk/ &middot; *uhn-in-thoo-zee-AS-tik*
**Русский:** без энтузиазма, равнодушный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unenthusiastic*, *most unenthusiastic*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** un- + *enthusiastic*
**Opposite:** *enthusiastic*

Not excited about something; showing little interest.

1. My parents were *unenthusiastic* about the whole idea.
2. She gave an *unenthusiastic* shrug.
3. The reviews were polite but *unenthusiastic*.

---

### unenviable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɛnviəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-EN-vee-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** незавидный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *an unenviable task*, *an unenviable position*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *enviable*
**Opposite:** *enviable*

Difficult or unpleasant — not something anyone would want.

1. She has the *unenviable* job of telling him.
2. The team is in the *unenviable* position of having to win both games.
3. He holds the *unenviable* record for most strikeouts.

---

### unequal

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈiːkwəl/ &middot; *uhn-EE-kwuhl*
**Русский:** неравный; неравноценный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unequal*, *most unequal*
**Takes:** *to* + noun, in sense 2 (*unequal to the task*)
**Form:** un- + *equal*
**Opposite:** *equal*

Unequal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not the same in size, amount or treatment; unfair.** (неравный, неодинаковый)

1. The two sides got *unequal* time to speak.
2. Pay is still *unequal* for the same work.
3. The country is far *more unequal* than it was in 1980.

**2. Not strong or good enough for something.** (не справляющийся, не соответствующий)

4. He proved *unequal* to the task.
5. The old bridge was *unequal* to the traffic.

---

### unequaled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈiːkwəld/ &middot; *uhn-EE-kwuhld*
**Русский:** непревзойдённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — often after the noun: *a record unequaled in baseball*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *equaled*
**Contrast:** American spelling has one *l*: *unequaled*, *unrivaled*, *unparalleled*. British writing doubles it: *unequalled*.

Better or greater than anything else of its kind.

1. Her record of seven titles remains *unequaled*.
2. The museum has an *unequaled* collection of Mayan art.
3. He wrote with an *unequaled* eye for detail.

---

### unequivocal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪˈkwɪvəkəl/ &middot; *uhn-i-KWIV-uh-kuhl*
**Русский:** недвусмысленный, однозначный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *equivocal*
**Opposite:** *ambiguous*
**Contrast:** The adverb *unequivocally* (uhn-i-KWIV-uh-kuh-lee) is the form you meet most often: *He unequivocally denied it*.

Leaving no room for doubt; completely clear.

1. The answer was an *unequivocal* no.
2. Scientists are *unequivocal* on this point.
3. She gave the plan her *unequivocal* support.

---

### unethical

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɛθɪkəl/ &middot; *uhn-ETH-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** неэтичный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unethical*, *most unethical*
**Form:** un- + *ethical*
**Opposite:** *ethical*
**Contrast:** *Unethical* is about professional or moral standards, *illegal* about the law. Plenty of things are *unethical* but perfectly legal.

Against accepted standards of right behavior, especially in a profession.

1. It would be *unethical* for a lawyer to represent both sides.
2. The paper accused the firm of *unethical* business practices.
3. Doctors face discipline for *unethical* conduct.

---

### uneven

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈiːvən/ &middot; *uhn-EE-vuhn*
**Русский:** неровный; неравномерный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uneven*, *most uneven*
**Form:** un- + *even*
**Opposite:** *even*
**Contrast:** For numbers Americans say *odd*, not *uneven*: *an odd number*.

Uneven has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not flat or smooth.** (неровный, ухабистый)

1. Watch your step — the sidewalk is *uneven*.
2. The old floor is *uneven* in the corners.
3. She tripped on the *uneven* pavement.

**2. Not the same all the way through; varying in quality.** (неравномерный, неровный (по качеству))

4. The album is *uneven* — two great songs and a lot of filler.
5. His play this season has been *uneven*.

**3. Not equal; one-sided.** (неравный, неравноценный)

6. It was an *uneven* contest from the start.
7. The workload is *uneven* across the team.

---

### uneventful

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪˈvɛntfəl/ &middot; *uhn-i-VENT-fuhl*
**Русский:** спокойный, без происшествий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uneventful*, *most uneventful*
**Form:** un- + *eventful*
**Opposite:** *eventful*

With nothing unusual or exciting happening — often a good thing.

1. The flight was long but *uneventful*.
2. We had an *uneventful* week at the lake.
3. Her recovery has been thankfully *uneventful*.

---

### unexceptional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪkˈsɛpʃənəl/ &middot; *uhn-ik-SEP-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** заурядный, ничем не примечательный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unexceptional*, *most unexceptional*
**Form:** un- + *exceptional*
**Opposite:** *exceptional*
**Contrast:** Do not confuse it with *unexceptionable*, a rare formal word meaning 'that nobody could object to'.

Ordinary; not especially good or interesting.

1. The food was decent but *unexceptional*.
2. He was an *unexceptional* student who became a great teacher.
3. It is an *unexceptional* house on an *unexceptional* street.

---

### unexciting

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪkˈsaɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-ik-SY-ting*
**Русский:** скучный, невыразительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unexciting*, *most unexciting*
**Form:** un- + *exciting*
**Opposite:** *exciting*

Dull; not interesting or thrilling.

1. It is a safe, *unexciting* investment.
2. The game was scoreless and *unexciting*.
3. My weekend was pleasant but *unexciting*.

---

### unexpected

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪkˈspɛktɪd/ &middot; *uhn-ik-SPEK-tid*
**Русский:** неожиданный, непредвиденный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unexpected*, *most unexpected*
**Form:** un- + *expected*
**Opposite:** *expected*
**Contrast:** The adverb is *unexpectedly* (*He died unexpectedly*), and *the unexpected* works as a noun: *Be ready for the unexpected*.

Not planned for; coming as a surprise.

1. We had an *unexpected* visitor last night.
2. The repair bill was an *unexpected* 400 dollars.
3. His answer was *unexpected*, to say the least.

---

### unexplainable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪkˈspleɪnəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-ik-SPLAY-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** необъяснимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *explainable*
**Opposite:** *explainable*
**Contrast:** *Inexplicable* means the same thing and is the commoner word in writing; *unexplainable* is the plainer, more spoken one.

Impossible to explain.

1. For some *unexplainable* reason, I said yes.
2. The pain was *unexplainable* — no test showed anything.
3. There is something *unexplainable* about that house.

---

### unexplained

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪkˈspleɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-ik-SPLAYND*
**Русский:** необъяснённый, невыясненный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *explained*
**Contrast:** *Unexplained* means nobody has explained it yet; *unexplainable* means nobody ever could.

Without a known cause or reason; still not accounted for.

1. The death remains *unexplained*.
2. She has been out for two weeks for *unexplained* reasons.
3. There were *unexplained* charges on the card.

---

### unexplored

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪkˈsplɔːrd/ &middot; *uhn-ik-SPLORD*
**Русский:** неисследованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *explored*

Unexplored has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not yet traveled through or mapped.** (неисследованный, неизученный)

1. Parts of the cave system are still *unexplored*.
2. The deep ocean is largely *unexplored*.

**2. Not yet thought about or looked into.** (нерассмотренный (о варианте, идее))

3. That is an *unexplored* option.
4. The idea went *unexplored* for years.

---

### unfailing

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfeɪlɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-FAY-ling*
**Русский:** неизменный, неиссякаемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *unfailing support*, *unfailing good humor*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *failing*

Always there; never running out or letting you down.

1. Thank you for your *unfailing* support.
2. She met every crisis with *unfailing* good humor.
3. He had an *unfailing* eye for talent.

---

### unfair

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfɛr/ &middot; *uhn-FAIR*
**Русский:** несправедливый; нечестный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfair*, *most unfair*
**Takes:** *to* + person (*unfair to the kids*)
**Form:** un- + *fair*
**Opposite:** *fair*
**Contrast:** The preposition is *to*, not *for*: *That is unfair to your sister*. *Unfair* covers both Russian *несправедливый* and *нечестный*; in a game, cheating is *cheating*, not *unfair play*.

Not right or just; treating people unequally.

1. It is *unfair* to blame the teachers for this.
2. He got fired for something he did not do — it is completely *unfair*.
3. They gained an *unfair* advantage.

---

### unfaithful

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfeɪθfəl/ &middot; *uhn-FAYTH-fuhl*
**Русский:** неверный (супругу)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfaithful*, *most unfaithful*
**Takes:** *to* + person
**Form:** un- + *faithful*
**Opposite:** *faithful*
**Contrast:** *Unfaithful* is almost always about marriage or a couple; for friends and promises say *disloyal*. The everyday phrase is *cheat on someone*: *He cheated on his wife*.

Having a romantic or sexual relationship with someone other than your partner.

1. He was *unfaithful* to her for years.
2. She found out that he had been *unfaithful*.
3. An *unfaithful* spouse is still the reason most often given.

---

### unfamiliar

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnfəˈmɪljər/ &middot; *uhn-fuh-MIL-yur*
**Русский:** незнакомый; несведущий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfamiliar*, *most unfamiliar*
**Takes:** *to* + person (it is new to them); *with* + noun (they do not know it)
**Form:** un- + *familiar*
**Opposite:** *familiar*
**Contrast:** Watch the preposition: a thing is *unfamiliar to* a person, but a person is *unfamiliar with* a thing — *The street was unfamiliar to me* / *I was unfamiliar with the street*.

Unfamiliar has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not known to you; strange.** (незнакомый, непривычный)

1. She woke up in an *unfamiliar* room.
2. The name was *unfamiliar* to me.
3. He sang in a language *unfamiliar* to most of the audience.

**2. Not knowing much about something.** (несведущий, не знакомый (с чем-либо))

4. I am *unfamiliar* with that software.
5. For readers *unfamiliar* with the case, here is a summary.

---

### unfathomable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfæðəməbəl/ &middot; *uhn-FATH-uh-muh-buhl*
**Русский:** непостижимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfathomable*, *most unfathomable*
**Form:** un- + *fathomable*, from *fathom* 'to measure the depth of'

Too strange, too deep or too large to be understood.

1. The loss is *unfathomable* to anyone who has not lived it.
2. For *unfathomable* reasons, the city cut the bus line.
3. The scale of the universe is *unfathomable*.

---

### unfavorable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfeɪvərəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-FAY-vuh-ruh-buhl*
**Русский:** неблагоприятный; отрицательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfavorable*, *most unfavorable*
**Form:** un- + *favorable*
**Opposite:** *favorable*
**Contrast:** American spelling drops the *u*: *unfavorable*, not *unfavourable*.

Unfavorable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Showing disapproval; negative.** (отрицательный, неодобрительный)

1. The book got *unfavorable* reviews.
2. His poll numbers are *unfavorable* in every age group.

**2. Not good for your plans; working against you.** (неблагоприятный (об условиях))

3. The weather was *unfavorable* for launch.
4. They sold at an *unfavorable* exchange rate.

---

### unfettered

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfɛtərd/ &middot; *uhn-FET-urd*
**Русский:** ничем не скованный, свободный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — before a noun, or after a verb with *by*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** un- + *fettered*, from *fetters* 'chains'

Free from limits or control.

1. The law gives the agency *unfettered* access to the records.
2. He wanted *unfettered* freedom to write what he liked.
3. Markets are rarely *unfettered* by regulation.

---

### unfilled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfɪld/ &middot; *uhn-FILD*
**Русский:** незаполненный, вакантный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *filled*

Still empty or still open — of a job, a seat or an order.

1. The district has 200 *unfilled* teaching jobs.
2. Her prescription sat *unfilled* for a week.
3. Half the seats on the board are *unfilled*.

---

### unfinished

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfɪnɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-FIN-isht*
**Русский:** незаконченный; необработанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *finished*
**Opposite:** *finished*
**Contrast:** An *unfinished* basement in American real estate is one with bare concrete and no walls or flooring — a selling point in the price, not a defect.

Unfinished has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not completed.** (незаконченный, недоделанный)

1. He left an *unfinished* novel on his desk.
2. My dinner sat *unfinished*.
3. We still have some *unfinished* business.

**2. Of wood or furniture: not painted, stained or varnished.** (необработанный, неокрашенный (о дереве))

4. They sell *unfinished* pine bookcases.
5. The attic is still *unfinished*.

---

### unfit

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfɪt/ &middot; *uhn-FIT*
**Русский:** непригодный; не в форме
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfit*, *most unfit*
**Takes:** *for* + noun; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** un- + *fit*
**Opposite:** *fit*
**Contrast:** For the second sense Americans usually say *out of shape*; *unfit* meaning 'not in condition' is mainly British.

Unfit has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not good enough or not suitable for something.** (непригодный, неподходящий)

1. The water was declared *unfit* to drink.
2. A judge found him *unfit* to stand trial.
3. The building is *unfit* for human habitation.

**2. Not in good physical condition.** (не в форме, нетренированный)

4. I was too *unfit* to finish the hike.
5. Years at a desk left him *unfit*.

---

### unflappable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈflæpəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-FLAP-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** невозмутимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unflappable*, *most unflappable*
**Form:** un- + *flappable*, from *get into a flap* 'to panic'

Staying calm no matter what happens.

1. Our pediatrician is completely *unflappable*.
2. She is the *unflappable* type — nothing rattles her.
3. He was *unflappable* under cross-examination.

---

### unflattering

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈflætərɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-FLAT-ur-ing*
**Русский:** невыгодный; нелестный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unflattering*, *most unflattering*
**Form:** un- + *flattering*
**Opposite:** *flattering*

Unflattering has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Making someone look worse than they really do.** (невыгодный, не красящий)

1. It is an *unflattering* photo — the lighting was terrible.
2. Those pants are *unflattering* on everybody.

**2. Showing someone in a bad light; not complimentary.** (нелестный)

3. The article paints an *unflattering* picture of the mayor.
4. He made some *unflattering* comparisons.

---

### unflinching

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈflɪntʃɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-FLIN-ching*
**Русский:** непоколебимый; беспощадно прямой
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *unflinching honesty*, *an unflinching look*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *flinching*

Not turning away from something hard or ugly; steady and brave.

1. The film is an *unflinching* look at addiction.
2. She answered with *unflinching* honesty.
3. He showed *unflinching* loyalty to his friends.

---

### unfocused

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfoʊkəst/ &middot; *uhn-FOH-kuhst*
**Русский:** несобранный; расплывчатый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfocused*, *most unfocused*
**Form:** un- + *focused*
**Opposite:** *focused*
**Contrast:** American English spells it with one *s*: *focused*, *unfocused*; British writing often doubles it.

Unfocused has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Without a clear aim; not concentrating.** (несобранный, бессистемный)

1. The campaign was *unfocused* and lost badly.
2. I have been tired and *unfocused* all week.

**2. Blurry; not sharp.** (нерезкий, расплывчатый)

3. The background is deliberately *unfocused*.
4. Her eyes were *unfocused*.

---

### unforced

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfɔːrst/ &middot; *uhn-FORST*
**Русский:** невынужденный; непринуждённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *an unforced error*, *unforced charm*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *forced*
**Contrast:** *Unforced error* has moved out of tennis into politics and business: *The resignation was an unforced error*.

Unforced has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In sports, of a mistake: caused by the player, not by the opponent.** (невынужденный (об ошибке))

1. She made 42 *unforced* errors.
2. The loss came down to *unforced* errors in the last set.

**2. Natural; not done under pressure.** (непринуждённый, естественный)

3. The movie has an *unforced* charm.
4. There was an easy, *unforced* warmth to his singing.

---

### unforeseen

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnfɔːrˈsiːn/ &middot; *uhn-for-SEEN*
**Русский:** непредвиденный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *unforeseen circumstances*, *unforeseen consequences*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *foreseen*

Not expected or predicted.

1. The event was canceled due to *unforeseen* circumstances.
2. There were *unforeseen* delays at the border.
3. Every fix brings *unforeseen* problems.

---

### unforgettable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnfərˈɡɛtəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-fur-GET-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** незабываемый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *forgettable*
**Opposite:** *forgettable*

So striking that you will always remember it.

1. It was an *unforgettable* trip.
2. Her performance was *unforgettable*.
3. The smell of that kitchen is *unforgettable*.

---

### unforgivable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnfərˈɡɪvəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-fur-GIV-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** непростительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *forgivable*
**Opposite:** *forgivable*
**Contrast:** *Unforgivable* judges the act; *unforgiving* describes a person or a thing that shows no mercy.

So bad that it cannot be excused.

1. What he said to her was *unforgivable*.
2. Missing your own daughter's wedding would be *unforgivable*.
3. It was an *unforgivable* lapse in judgment.

---

### unforgiving

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnfərˈɡɪvɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-fur-GIV-ing*
**Русский:** суровый, безжалостный; непрощающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unforgiving*, *most unforgiving*
**Form:** un- + *forgiving*

Unforgiving has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Harsh; leaving no room for mistakes.** (суровый, не прощающий ошибок)

1. The schedule is *unforgiving*.
2. It is *unforgiving* terrain, all rock and ice.
3. Video is an *unforgiving* medium.

**2. Not willing to forgive people.** (непрощающий, злопамятный)

4. He is an *unforgiving* man.
5. She can be *unforgiving* when you let her down.

---

### unfortunate

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfɔːrtʃənət/ &middot; *uhn-FOR-chuh-nuht*
**Русский:** неудачный; несчастный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfortunate*, *most unfortunate*
**Form:** un- + *fortunate*
**Opposite:** *fortunate*
**Contrast:** *Unfortunately* at the head of a sentence is the everyday way to soften bad news: *Unfortunately, we cannot refund that*.

Unfortunate has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Regrettable; turning out in a way you wish it had not.** (неудачный, досадный)

1. It was an *unfortunate* choice of words.
2. The timing was *unfortunate*.
3. There was an *unfortunate* mix-up with the tickets.

**2. Having bad luck.** (несчастный, невезучий)

4. Some *unfortunate* soul has to clean this up.
5. He was *unfortunate* enough to be standing right there.

---

### unfounded

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfaʊndɪd/ &middot; *uhn-FOWN-did*
**Русский:** необоснованный, беспочвенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *founded*

Not based on facts or evidence.

1. The rumors turned out to be *unfounded*.
2. He made *unfounded* accusations against his neighbor.
3. Her fears were not *unfounded*.

---

### unfriendly

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfrɛndli/ &middot; *uhn-FREND-lee*
**Русский:** недружелюбный, неприветливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *unfriendlier*, *unfriendliest*; also *more unfriendly*
**Takes:** *to* or *toward* + person
**Form:** un- + *friendly*
**Opposite:** *friendly*
**Contrast:** At the end of a compound, *-friendly* means 'good for' or 'easy for' (*kid-friendly*, *user-friendly*); its opposite is written with a hyphen too: *an environmentally unfriendly plastic*.

Not warm or welcoming toward people.

1. The waiter was not rude, just *unfriendly*.
2. It is a beautiful town with an *unfriendly* streak.
3. The new manager is even *unfriendlier* than the last one.

---

### unfulfilled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnfʊlˈfɪld/ &middot; *uhn-fuul-FILD*
**Русский:** неудовлетворённый; невыполненный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfulfilled*, *most unfulfilled*
**Form:** un- + *fulfilled*
**Opposite:** *fulfilled*

Unfulfilled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not satisfied with your life or your work.** (неудовлетворённый, не нашедший себя)

1. He was well paid and completely *unfulfilled*.
2. She felt *unfulfilled* after the kids left home.

**2. Not carried out or not achieved — of a promise, a hope or potential.** (невыполненный, нереализованный)

3. It is another *unfulfilled* campaign promise.
4. So much of his talent went *unfulfilled*.

---

### unfunded

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfʌndɪd/ &middot; *uhn-FUN-did*
**Русский:** не обеспеченный финансированием
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *funded*
**Opposite:** *funded*

With no money set aside to pay for it.

1. Congress keeps passing *unfunded* mandates.
2. The state has billions in *unfunded* pension liabilities.
3. The program is popular but still *unfunded*.

---

### unfunny

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfʌni/ &middot; *uhn-FUN-ee*
**Русский:** несмешной
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unfunny*, *most unfunny*
**Form:** un- + *funny*
**Opposite:** *funny*

Meant to be funny but not.

1. The monologue was long and *unfunny*.
2. He tells the same *unfunny* joke every year.
3. It was an *unfunny* comedy about a funny subject.

---

### unfurnished

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈfɜːrnɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-FUR-nisht*
**Русский:** без мебели, немеблированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *furnished*
**Opposite:** *furnished*

Rented with no furniture in it.

1. The apartment is *unfurnished*.
2. We are looking for an *unfurnished* two-bedroom.
3. *Unfurnished* units rent for a few hundred less.

---

### unglamorous

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɡlæmərəs/ &middot; *uhn-GLAM-uh-ruhs*
**Русский:** будничный, непривлекательный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unglamorous*, *most unglamorous*
**Form:** un- + *glamorous*
**Opposite:** *glamorous*
**Contrast:** The noun keeps the *u* (*glamour*), but the adjective drops it in American spelling: *glamorous*, *unglamorous*.

Dull and ordinary; not exciting or attractive.

1. Most of police work is *unglamorous* paperwork.
2. It is an *unglamorous* job, but somebody has to do it.
3. The town is *unglamorous* and cheap to live in.

---

### ungodly

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɡɑːdli/ &middot; *uhn-GAHD-lee*
**Русский:** несусветный; безбожный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *an ungodly hour*, *an ungodly amount*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *godly*

Ungodly has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Outrageous — unreasonably early, loud or large. Informal.** (несусветный, дикий)

1. He calls at some *ungodly* hour every Sunday.
2. They spent an *ungodly* amount of money on the wedding.

**2. Not religious; wicked.** (безбожный, нечестивый)

3. The preacher warned the young people against *ungodly* music.
4. They thought the whole city led an *ungodly* life.

---

### ungrammatical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɡrəˈmætɪkəl/ &middot; *uhn-gruh-MAT-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** грамматически неправильный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ungrammatical*, *most ungrammatical*
**Form:** un- + *grammatical*
**Opposite:** *grammatical*

Breaking the rules of grammar.

1. 'He don't know' is *ungrammatical* in standard English.
2. The sentence is *ungrammatical*, but everybody says it.
3. Her essay had several *ungrammatical* sentences.

---

### ungrateful

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɡreɪtfəl/ &middot; *uhn-GRAYT-fuhl*
**Русский:** неблагодарный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ungrateful*, *most ungrateful*
**Takes:** *to* + person; *for* + noun
**Form:** un- + *grateful*
**Opposite:** *grateful*

Not showing thanks for what you have been given.

1. I do not want to sound *ungrateful*, but I wanted the blue one.
2. He was *ungrateful* for everything they did.
3. She called him a spoiled, *ungrateful* kid.

---

### unguarded

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɡɑːrdɪd/ &middot; *uhn-GAR-did*
**Русский:** неосторожный; неохраняемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — sense 1 only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *guarded*
**Opposite:** *guarded*

Unguarded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Careless; said without thinking — of a moment or a remark.** (неосторожный, неосмотрительный)

1. In an *unguarded* moment he admitted it.
2. One *unguarded* comment cost him the job.

**2. Not protected or watched.** (неохраняемый)

3. The back gate was left *unguarded*.
4. Nothing at the site is *unguarded* at night.

---

### unhappy

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhæpi/ &middot; *uhn-HAP-ee*
**Русский:** несчастный; недовольный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *unhappier*, *unhappiest*; also *more unhappy*
**Takes:** *about* or *with* + noun; *that*-clause
**Form:** un- + *happy*
**Opposite:** *happy*

Unhappy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Sad; not happy.** (несчастный, грустный)

1. She had an *unhappy* childhood.
2. He looked *unhappy* all evening.
3. I have never seen her *unhappier* than she was that winter.

**2. Not satisfied or pleased with something.** (недовольный)

4. Customers are *unhappy* about the new fee.
5. The coach was *unhappy* with the effort.

**3. Unlucky or badly chosen — of an event or a decision.** (неудачный, злополучный)

6. It was an *unhappy* coincidence.
7. The whole project came to an *unhappy* end.

---

### unharmed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhɑːrmd/ &middot; *uhn-HARMD*
**Русский:** невредимый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb: *escaped unharmed*; before a noun use *uninjured*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *harmed*

Not hurt or damaged at all.

1. The children were found *unharmed*.
2. He walked away *unharmed*.
3. The painting came through the fire *unharmed*.

---

### unhealthy

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhɛlθi/ &middot; *uhn-HEL-thee*
**Русский:** вредный для здоровья; нездоровый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *unhealthier*, *unhealthiest*; also *more unhealthy*
**Form:** un- + *healthy*
**Opposite:** *healthy*

Unhealthy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Bad for your health.** (вредный для здоровья)

1. Fried food every day is *unhealthy*.
2. The air in the valley is *unhealthier* in August.
3. He has a lot of *unhealthy* habits.

**2. Not in good health; looking sick.** (нездоровый, болезненный)

4. The plant looks *unhealthy*.
5. She had an *unhealthy* gray color.

**3. Not normal or not good for you — of an interest or a relationship.** (нездоровый (об интересе, отношениях))

6. He has an *unhealthy* obsession with the case.
7. It was an *unhealthy* relationship from the start.

---

### unheard-of

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhɜːrd ʌv/ &middot; *uhn-HURD-uv*
**Русский:** неслыханный, невиданный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — before a noun (*an unheard-of price*) or after a verb (*It is not unheard-of*)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — un- + *heard of*
**Contrast:** Keep the hyphen before a noun (*an unheard-of delay*); after a verb both *unheard of* and *unheard-of* are written.

So unusual that it has never happened before.

1. A rookie winning the title was *unheard-of*.
2. They paid an *unheard-of* price for the house.
3. Snow in May is not *unheard-of* here.

---

### unhelpful

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhɛlpfəl/ &middot; *uhn-HELP-fuhl*
**Русский:** бесполезный; неотзывчивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unhelpful*, *most unhelpful*
**Form:** un- + *helpful*
**Opposite:** *helpful*

Not useful, or not willing to help.

1. The support line was polite but *unhelpful*.
2. That is an *unhelpful* way to frame the question.
3. He was being deliberately *unhelpful*.

---

### unheralded

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhɛrəldɪd/ &middot; *uhn-HER-uhl-did*
**Русский:** незаметный, оставшийся без внимания
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *an unheralded rookie*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *heralded*, from *herald* 'to announce'

Arriving or succeeding with no advance attention or praise.

1. An *unheralded* rookie won the starting job.
2. The *unheralded* album became a classic.
3. She spent years doing *unheralded* work behind the scenes.

---

### unhinged

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhɪndʒd/ &middot; *uhn-HINJD*
**Русский:** невменяемый, слетевший с катушек
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unhinged*, *most unhinged*
**Form:** un- + *hinged* — the picture is a door off its hinges

Mentally unbalanced; wildly irrational.

1. The email was completely *unhinged*.
2. He sounded *unhinged* on the phone.
3. It was the *most unhinged* thing I have ever watched.

---

### unholy

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhoʊli/ &middot; *uhn-HOH-lee*
**Русский:** жуткий; нечестивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *an unholy mess*, *an unholy alliance*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *holy*
**Opposite:** *holy*

Unholy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shockingly bad or shockingly great. Informal, in fixed phrases.** (жуткий, невообразимый)

1. The kitchen was an *unholy* mess.
2. They made an *unholy* racket until two in the morning.

**2. Wicked; not sacred.** (нечестивый, богопротивный)

3. The two parties formed an *unholy* alliance.
4. *Unholy* practices were denounced from the pulpit.

---

### unhygienic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnhaɪˈdʒɛnɪk/ &middot; *uhn-hy-JEN-ik*
**Русский:** антисанитарный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unhygienic*, *most unhygienic*
**Form:** un- + *hygienic*
**Opposite:** *hygienic*
**Contrast:** In everyday American speech the usual word is *unsanitary*: *unsanitary conditions*.

Dirty in a way that can spread disease.

1. The kitchen was closed for *unhygienic* conditions.
2. Sharing a razor is *unhygienic*.
3. Inspectors called the whole plant *unhygienic*.

---

### unidentifiable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnaɪˈdɛntɪfaɪəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-y-DEN-tuh-fy-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** неопознаваемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *identifiable*
**Opposite:** *identifiable*

Impossible to recognize or to name.

1. There was an *unidentifiable* smell in the fridge.
2. The remains were *unidentifiable* without dental records.
3. He was playing some *unidentifiable* instrument.

---

### unidentified

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnaɪˈdɛntɪfaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-y-DEN-tuh-fyd*
**Русский:** неопознанный, неустановленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *identified*
**Opposite:** *identified*

Whose name or nature is not yet known.

1. Police are looking for an *unidentified* man in a red jacket.
2. The victim remains *unidentified*.
3. An *unidentified* source told the paper about the deal.

---

### unified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːnɪfaɪd/ &middot; *YOO-nuh-fyd*
**Русский:** единый, объединённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unified*, *most unified*
**Form:** -ed from *unify*
**Opposite:** *fragmented*
**Contrast:** In California and several other states a *unified school district* is one that runs both the elementary and the high schools.

Joined into one; working as a single whole.

1. We need a *unified* response from the states.
2. The company moved to a *unified* billing system.
3. The party is *more unified* than it looks from outside.

---

### uniform

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːnɪfɔːrm/ &middot; *YOO-nuh-form*
**Русский:** одинаковый, единообразный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uniform*, *most uniform*
**Form:** Latin *uni-* 'one' + *form*
**Opposite:** *varied*
**Contrast:** The noun is far commoner (*a school uniform*, *in uniform*); as an adjective the word is formal or technical. The adverb *uniformly* is frequent: *uniformly excellent reviews*.

The same everywhere and at every moment; not varying.

1. Cut the vegetables into *uniform* pieces.
2. The rules are not *uniform* across the country.
3. Bake it until the color is *uniform*.

---

### uniformed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːnɪfɔːrmd/ &middot; *YOO-nuh-formd*
**Русский:** одетый в форму
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a uniformed officer*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the noun *uniform*
**Contrast:** One letter apart from *uninformed*, which means 'knowing nothing about it': *a uniformed officer* is in uniform, *an uninformed officer* has not been told.

Wearing a uniform, especially a police or military one.

1. Two *uniformed* officers came to the door.
2. *Uniformed* security stood at every entrance.
3. The parade was led by *uniformed* veterans.

---

### unilateral

**Pronunciation:** /ˌjuːnɪˈlætərəl/ &middot; *yoo-nuh-LAT-uh-ruhl*
**Русский:** односторонний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a unilateral decision*, *unilateral action*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *uni-* 'one' + *lateral* 'side'
**Opposite:** *bilateral*
**Contrast:** Compare *bilateral* (two sides) and *multilateral* (many). The adverb *unilaterally* is common: *They unilaterally changed the terms*.

Done by one side alone, without agreement from the others.

1. The president took *unilateral* action.
2. They announced a *unilateral* ceasefire.
3. It was a *unilateral* decision, and nobody else was consulted.

---

### unimaginable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪˈmædʒɪnəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-i-MAJ-uh-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** невообразимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *imaginable*
**Opposite:** *imaginable*

Too extreme or too strange to picture.

1. The families have suffered *unimaginable* loss.
2. Such wealth was *unimaginable* to my grandparents.
3. Ten years ago this would have been *unimaginable*.

---

### unimaginative

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪˈmædʒɪnətɪv/ &middot; *uhn-i-MAJ-uh-nuh-tiv*
**Русский:** лишённый воображения, банальный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unimaginative*, *most unimaginative*
**Form:** un- + *imaginative*
**Opposite:** *imaginative*

Dull; showing no new ideas.

1. The menu is safe and *unimaginative*.
2. It was an *unimaginative* remake of a great movie.
3. He is careful but *unimaginative*.

---

### unimpeded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪmˈpiːdɪd/ &middot; *uhn-im-PEE-did*
**Русский:** беспрепятственный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — before a noun, or after a verb with *by*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** un- + *impeded*

Not blocked or slowed by anything.

1. The river runs *unimpeded* to the sea.
2. Aid trucks now have *unimpeded* access.
3. He rose through the ranks *unimpeded* by scandal.

---

### unimportant

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪmˈpɔːrtənt/ &middot; *uhn-im-POR-tuhnt*
**Русский:** неважный, незначительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unimportant*, *most unimportant*
**Form:** un- + *important*
**Opposite:** *important*

Not mattering much.

1. The details are *unimportant* right now.
2. He felt small and *unimportant* at the meeting.
3. It is an *unimportant* game on paper.

---

### unimpressed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪmˈprɛst/ &middot; *uhn-im-PREST*
**Русский:** не впечатлённый, равнодушный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb: *was unimpressed*
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *thoroughly*, *distinctly* or *singularly*
**Takes:** *by* or *with* + noun
**Form:** un- + *impressed*
**Opposite:** *impressed*

Not thinking much of something you have seen or heard.

1. The judge was *unimpressed* by that argument.
2. My kids were *unimpressed* with the fancy hotel.
3. She listened and looked thoroughly *unimpressed*.

---

### unimpressive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪmˈprɛsɪv/ &middot; *uhn-im-PRES-iv*
**Русский:** невыразительный, не впечатляющий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unimpressive*, *most unimpressive*
**Form:** un- + *impressive*
**Opposite:** *impressive*

Not good enough to impress anyone.

1. His numbers this season are *unimpressive*.
2. It is an *unimpressive* building from the outside.
3. The demo was short and *unimpressive*.

---

### unincorporated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈkɔːrpəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-in-KOR-puh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** не входящий в черту города; не зарегистрированный как корпорация
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *unincorporated Los Angeles County*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *incorporated*
**Opposite:** *incorporated*

Unincorporated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of land: outside the limits of any city or town, so the county governs it.** (не входящий в черту города)

1. They live in an *unincorporated* part of the county.
2. The fire started on *unincorporated* land.

**2. Of a business or group: not legally registered as a corporation.** (не зарегистрированный как юридическое лицо)

3. It is an *unincorporated* nonprofit run by volunteers.
4. An *unincorporated* business gives the owner no liability protection.

---

### uninformed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈfɔːrmd/ &middot; *uhn-in-FORMD*
**Русский:** неосведомлённый, несведущий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uninformed*, *most uninformed*
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Form:** un- + *informed*
**Opposite:** *informed*
**Contrast:** *Uninformed* is not *uniformed* (wearing a uniform), and not *misinformed*, which means you were told the wrong thing.

Not knowing the facts about something.

1. Voters should not have to make an *uninformed* choice.
2. He is completely *uninformed* about how the system works.
3. That is an *uninformed* opinion, not a fact.

---

### uninhabitable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈhæbɪtəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-in-HAB-i-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** непригодный для жизни
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *inhabitable*
**Opposite:** *habitable*
**Contrast:** *Inhabitable* means the same as *habitable* — fit to live in — so the *in-* there is not a negative. The negative word is *uninhabitable*.

Impossible to live in.

1. The fire left the building *uninhabitable*.
2. Parts of the coast may become *uninhabitable*.
3. The inspector declared the apartment *uninhabitable*.

---

### uninhabited

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈhæbɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-in-HAB-i-tid*
**Русский:** необитаемый, нежилой
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *inhabited*
**Opposite:** *inhabited*

With nobody living there.

1. They landed on an *uninhabited* island.
2. The house had been *uninhabited* for years.
3. Most of the region is *uninhabited*.

---

### uninhibited

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈhɪbɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-in-HIB-i-tid*
**Русский:** раскованный, несдержанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uninhibited*, *most uninhibited*
**Form:** un- + *inhibited*
**Opposite:** *inhibited*

Free and open in the way you behave; not shy or held back.

1. She is an *uninhibited* dancer.
2. Two drinks in, he gets loud and *uninhibited*.
3. The show is famous for its *uninhibited* humor.

---

### uninspired

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈspaɪərd/ &middot; *uhn-in-SPY-urd*
**Русский:** посредственный, банальный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uninspired*, *most uninspired*
**Form:** un- + *inspired*
**Opposite:** *inspired*
**Contrast:** *Uninspired* describes work with no spark in it; *uninspiring* describes a thing that gives you no spark: *an uninspired performance* by a tired band, *an uninspiring menu* that leaves you cold.

Dull and ordinary; showing no spark.

1. The cooking was competent but *uninspired*.
2. It was an *uninspired* performance.
3. The new logo looks *uninspired*.

---

### uninspiring

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈspaɪərɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-in-SPY-ur-ing*
**Русский:** не вдохновляющий, скучный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uninspiring*, *most uninspiring*
**Form:** -ing — what fails to excite you
**Opposite:** *inspiring*

Not exciting or encouraging anyone.

1. It was an *uninspiring* start to the season.
2. The choices on the ballot are *uninspiring*.
3. He gave a flat, *uninspiring* talk.

---

### uninsured

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈʃʊrd/ &middot; *uhn-in-SHUURD*
**Русский:** незастрахованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *insured*
**Opposite:** *insured*

Uninsured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a person: having no health or car insurance.** (незастрахованный (о человеке))

1. Millions of Americans are still *uninsured*.
2. An *uninsured* driver hit her car.

**2. Of property or a loss: not covered by insurance.** (не покрытый страховкой)

3. The flood damage was *uninsured*.
4. They face thousands of dollars in *uninsured* losses.

---

### unintelligible

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈtɛlɪdʒəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-in-TEL-i-juh-buhl*
**Русский:** невнятный, непонятный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unintelligible*, *most unintelligible*
**Takes:** *to* + person
**Form:** un- + *intelligible*
**Opposite:** *intelligible*

Impossible to understand or to make out.

1. The recording is *unintelligible* in places.
2. His speech was slurred and *unintelligible*.
3. The form is *unintelligible* to anyone but a lawyer.

---

### unintended

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈtɛndɪd/ &middot; *uhn-in-TEN-did*
**Русский:** непреднамеренный, непредусмотренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *intended*
**Opposite:** *intended*

Not planned or not meant to happen.

1. The law had *unintended* consequences.
2. There was an *unintended* pause that got the biggest laugh.
3. Any resemblance to real people is *unintended*.

---

### unintentional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈtɛnʃənəl/ &middot; *uhn-in-TEN-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** непреднамеренный, нечаянный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *intentional*
**Opposite:** *intentional*
**Contrast:** The adverb *unintentionally* is the common form: *I unintentionally left her off the list*.

Not done on purpose.

1. The insult was *unintentional*, but it still stung.
2. It was an *unintentional* slip.
3. Any errors in the list are *unintentional*.

---

### uninteresting

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɪntrəstɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-IN-truh-sting*
**Русский:** неинтересный, скучный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uninteresting*, *most uninteresting*
**Form:** un- + *interesting*
**Opposite:** *interesting*
**Contrast:** *Uninteresting* is the thing; *uninterested* is the person (*He was uninterested in the offer*). *Disinterested* is a third word: it means 'having nothing to gain' — a *disinterested* judge, not a bored one.

Not holding your attention; dull.

1. The first half of the book is *uninteresting*.
2. He found the whole subject *uninteresting*.
3. It is a technically *uninteresting* problem.

---

### uninterrupted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪntəˈrʌptɪd/ &middot; *uhn-in-tuh-RUP-tid*
**Русский:** непрерывный; ничем не заслонённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — before a noun, or after a verb: *She worked uninterrupted*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *interrupted*

Going on without a break, or not blocked by anything.

1. I need two *uninterrupted* hours to finish this.
2. The room has an *uninterrupted* view of the bay.
3. He slept *uninterrupted* for ten hours.

---

### uninvited

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnɪnˈvaɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-in-VY-tid*
**Русский:** незваный, непрошеный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — before a noun, or after a verb: *showed up uninvited*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *invited*
**Opposite:** *invited*

Coming or given without being asked for.

1. An *uninvited* guest showed up with a six-pack.
2. He walked in *uninvited*.
3. She is tired of *uninvited* advice about her kids.

---

### unionized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːnjənaɪzd/ &middot; *YOON-yuh-nyzd*
**Русский:** объединённый в профсоюз
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *unionize*
**Opposite:** *nonunion*
**Contrast:** American English writes *unionize*, *unionized* with a *z*; the *-ise* spelling is British.

Belonging to a labor union — of workers or of a workplace.

1. The plant is fully *unionized*.
2. *Unionized* workers earn about 15 percent more on average.
3. Only one of their stores is *unionized*.

---

### unisex

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːnɪsɛks/ &middot; *YOO-nuh-seks*
**Русский:** унисекс, для мужчин и женщин
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a unisex bathroom*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *uni-* 'one' + *sex*

Made for both men and women.

1. The gym has a *unisex* locker room.
2. It is a *unisex* fragrance.
3. The shirts come in *unisex* sizes only.

---

### united

**Pronunciation:** /juˈnaɪtɪd/ &middot; *yoo-NY-tid*
**Русский:** объединённый; единодушный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more united*, *most united*
**Takes:** *in* + noun or *-ing*, in sense 2 (*united in grief*)
**Form:** -ed from *unite*
**Opposite:** *divided*
**Contrast:** Capitalized in names: *the United States*, *the United Nations*, *the United Kingdom*.

United has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Joined together into one country, group or organization.** (объединённый, единый)

1. A *united* Germany came in 1990.
2. They campaigned for a *united* Ireland.

**2. In agreement; acting together.** (единодушный, сплочённый)

3. The family is *united* in its grief.
4. The party is *more united* than it was a year ago.

---

### unjust

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdʒʌst/ &middot; *uhn-JUST*
**Русский:** несправедливый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unjust*, *most unjust*
**Form:** un- + *just*
**Opposite:** *just*
**Contrast:** *Unjust* is formal and belongs to laws, rulings and systems; for everyday complaints say *unfair*: *It is unfair that she got a bigger piece*.

Not fair or right — especially of a law, a system or a punishment.

1. They call the sentence *unjust*.
2. He spent his life fighting an *unjust* system.
3. Nothing is *more unjust* than punishing the wrong person.

---

### unjustifiable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌndʒʌstɪˈfaɪəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-jus-tuh-FY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** неоправданный, непростительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *justifiable*
**Opposite:** *justifiable*

Impossible to defend with any good reason.

1. The delay is *unjustifiable*.
2. He called the spending *unjustifiable*.
3. It was an *unjustifiable* use of force.

---

### unjustified

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈdʒʌstɪfaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-JUS-tuh-fyd*
**Русский:** неоправданный, необоснованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *justified*
**Opposite:** *justified*
**Contrast:** *Unjustified* says there was no good reason for it this time; *unjustifiable* says there could never be one.

Not supported by a good reason; not deserved.

1. The fear turned out to be *unjustified*.
2. She complained about *unjustified* charges on the bill.
3. His confidence was entirely *unjustified*.

---

### unkempt

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkɛmpt/ &middot; *uhn-KEMPT*
**Русский:** неопрятный, растрёпанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unkempt*, *most unkempt*
**Form:** un- + *kempt*, an old participle meaning *combed*
**Opposite:** *neat*, *well-groomed*

Messy and not taken care of, said of hair, clothes or a yard.

1. He showed up for the interview in an *unkempt* suit.
2. Her hair was *unkempt* and her eyes were red.
3. The yard grew *more unkempt* every week the family was away.

---

### unkind

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkaɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-KYND*
**Русский:** недобрый, злой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *unkinder*, *unkindest*; also *more unkind*, *most unkind*
**Takes:** *to* + person
**Form:** un- + *kind*
**Opposite:** *kind*

Saying or doing things that hurt people.

1. It was *unkind* of you to laugh at her accent.
2. Don't be *unkind* to your little brother.
3. That was the *unkindest* thing anyone has ever said to me.

---

### unknowing

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈnoʊɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-NOH-ing*
**Русский:** не подозревающий, невольный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unknowing*, *most unknowing*
**Form:** un- + *knowing*
**Contrast:** *Unknowing* describes the person who doesn't realize; the adverb *unknowingly* describes the act: *he unknowingly signed away his rights*.

Not aware of what is really going on.

1. The scam used *unknowing* students to move the money.
2. She was an *unknowing* witness to the whole argument.
3. Tourists make *unknowing* mistakes that the locals find funny.

---

### unlabeled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈleɪbəld/ &middot; *uhn-LAY-buhld*
**Русский:** без этикетки, немаркированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *labeled*
**Contrast:** American English writes one *l*: *unlabeled*. British books print *unlabelled*.

Having no label or name on it.

1. There were three *unlabeled* jars at the back of the fridge.
2. Never open an *unlabeled* file from a stranger.
3. Half the boxes in the garage are still *unlabeled*.

---

### unlawful

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlɔːfəl/ &middot; *uhn-LAW-fuhl*
**Русский:** незаконный, противоправный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *lawful*
**Opposite:** *lawful*, *legal*
**Contrast:** *Illegal* is the everyday word. *Unlawful* belongs to legal language and to fixed phrases: *unlawful entry*, *unlawful detention*.

Against the law.

1. The court found the search *unlawful*.
2. He was charged with *unlawful* possession of a firearm.
3. Firing someone for reporting a safety problem is *unlawful*.

---

### unleaded

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlɛdɪd/ &middot; *uhn-LED-id*
**Русский:** неэтилированный (о бензине)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *leaded*, from *lead* the metal
**Contrast:** The *lead* here is the metal, said like *led*, so the word rhymes with *shredded*. At the pump Americans also use it as a noun: *fill it up with unleaded*.

Made without added lead, said of gasoline.

1. Most cars built after 1975 need *unleaded* gas.
2. The station sells three grades of *unleaded* fuel.
3. Older engines were never designed to run on *unleaded* gasoline.

---

### unlicensed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlaɪsənst/ &middot; *uhn-LY-suhnst*
**Русский:** не имеющий лицензии, нелицензированный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *licensed*
**Opposite:** *licensed*
**Contrast:** American English spells both the noun and the verb *license*, so the adjective is *unlicensed*; British English writes the noun *licence*.

Without the official permit the law requires.

1. An *unlicensed* contractor did the wiring, and it shows.
2. The city shut down two *unlicensed* day care centers.
3. Practicing medicine while *unlicensed* is a felony.

---

### unlikable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlaɪkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-LY-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** несимпатичный, неприятный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unlikable*, *most unlikable*
**Form:** un- + *likable*
**Opposite:** *likable*
**Contrast:** American English drops the *e*: *likable*, *unlikable*. *Unlikeable* is the British spelling.

Hard to like.

1. Every character in the novel is *unlikable* in a different way.
2. He isn't evil, just *unlikable*.
3. The remake made the hero *more unlikable* than the villain.

---

### unlimited

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlɪmɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-LIM-i-tid*
**Русский:** неограниченный, безлимитный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *limited*
**Opposite:** *limited*

With no limit on the amount, the number or the time.

1. The plan comes with *unlimited* texting.
2. Kids get *unlimited* refills at that diner.
3. Your access to the archive is *unlimited* for a year.

---

### unlisted

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlɪstɪd/ &middot; *uhn-LIS-tid*
**Русский:** не внесённый в справочник; доступный только по ссылке
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *listed*
**Contrast:** An *unlisted* phone number is one the phone company keeps out of the directory; the British term is *ex-directory*.

Unlisted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Kept out of a public directory or list.** (не внесённый в справочник, незарегистрированный)

1. Her number is *unlisted*, so you'll have to email her.
2. The house is *unlisted*, which is why it never came up in my search.

**2. Visible only to people who have the link, said of a video or a page.** (доступный только по ссылке, скрытый из поиска)

3. I made the video *unlisted* so only the family can watch it.
4. *Unlisted* posts don't show up in search results.

---

### unlit

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlɪt/ &middot; *uhn-LIT*
**Русский:** неосвещённый; незажжённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *lit*, the past participle of *light*
**Opposite:** *lit*

Unlit has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having no lights; dark.** (неосвещённый, тёмный)

1. Don't walk home down that *unlit* alley.
2. The parking lot is *unlit* after midnight.

**2. Not burning, said of a candle or a cigarette.** (незажжённый)

3. An *unlit* cigarette hung from his mouth.
4. The birthday candles were still *unlit* when we started singing.

---

### unlivable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlɪvəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-LIV-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** непригодный для жизни
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unlivable*, *most unlivable*
**Form:** un- + *livable*
**Opposite:** *livable*
