**Contrast:** American English drops the *e*: *livable*, *unlivable*. British books print *unliveable*.

Impossible, or almost impossible, to live in.

1. The mold made the apartment *unlivable*.
2. Rents like these make the city *unlivable* for teachers.
3. Every August the top floor gets *more unlivable*.

---

### unlocked

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlɑːkt/ &middot; *uhn-LAHKT*
**Русский:** незапертый; разблокированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *locked*
**Opposite:** *locked*

Unlocked has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not fastened with a lock.** (незапертый, открытый)

1. She left the back door *unlocked* again.
2. An *unlocked* bike downtown won't last an hour.

**2. Not tied to one carrier, said of a phone.** (разблокированный (о телефоне))

3. I bought an *unlocked* phone so I could switch plans.
4. Is the phone *unlocked*, or is it still on a contract?

---

### unloved

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlʌvd/ &middot; *uhn-LUVD*
**Русский:** нелюбимый, никому не нужный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unloved*, *most unloved*
**Form:** un- + *loved*
**Opposite:** *loved*

Getting no love or affection from anyone.

1. No child should grow up feeling *unloved*.
2. The old piano sat *unloved* in the corner of the basement.
3. He felt *more unloved* after the divorce than he ever had as a kid.

---

### unlucky

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈlʌki/ &middot; *uhn-LUK-ee*
**Русский:** невезучий, несчастливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *unluckier*, *unluckiest*
**Takes:** *in* + noun (*unlucky in love*); *to*-infinitive
**Form:** un- + *lucky*
**Opposite:** *lucky*

Unlucky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having bad luck.** (невезучий, неудачливый)

1. We were *unlucky* with the weather all week.
2. She was *unlucky* to hit every red light on Main Street.
3. He is the *unluckiest* driver I know.

**2. Believed to bring bad luck.** (несчастливый, приносящий несчастье)

4. Some hotels skip the thirteenth floor because the number is *unlucky*.
5. My grandmother thinks it's *unlucky* to open an umbrella indoors.

---

### unmade

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmeɪd/ &middot; *uhn-MAYD*
**Русский:** незастеленная (постель)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *an unmade bed* is far more common than *the bed is unmade*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *made*
**Contrast:** It goes with beds and almost nothing else. For a room that hasn't been tidied, say *the room is a mess*, not *the room is unmade*.

Not straightened out after being slept in, said of a bed.

1. He left for work with an *unmade* bed, as usual.
2. The camera panned across a messy room and an *unmade* bed.
3. Housekeeping never leaves an *unmade* bed behind.

---

### unmanageable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmænɪdʒəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-MAN-i-juh-buhl*
**Русский:** неуправляемый, неподдающийся контролю
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unmanageable*, *most unmanageable*
**Form:** un- + *manageable*
**Opposite:** *manageable*

Too big, too difficult or too wild to control.

1. My hair gets *unmanageable* in humid weather.
2. The class became *unmanageable* the minute the substitute walked in.
3. The debt grew *more unmanageable* every month.

---

### unmanned

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmænd/ &middot; *uhn-MAND*
**Русский:** беспилотный; без персонала
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *manned*
**Contrast:** *Uncrewed* is now preferred at NASA and in science writing, but *an unmanned drone* is still what you hear on the news.

Working with nobody on board or on duty.

1. An *unmanned* aircraft photographed the fire.
2. The gate is *unmanned* after six, so bring your badge.
3. NASA sent several *unmanned* probes to Mars first.

---

### unmarked

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmɑːrkt/ &middot; *uhn-MAHRKT*
**Русский:** без опознавательных знаков; непомеченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *marked*

Carrying no sign, label or markings that would identify it.

1. An *unmarked* police car pulled us over.
2. The trail is *unmarked* past the river, so bring a map.
3. They buried him in an *unmarked* grave.

---

### unmarried

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmɛrid/ &middot; *uhn-MER-eed*
**Русский:** неженатый, незамужняя
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *married*
**Opposite:** *married*
**Contrast:** Forms and conversation usually offer *single*. *Unmarried* is the more formal word and the one used in *an unmarried couple* living together.

Not married.

1. Both of my uncles are *unmarried*.
2. The form asks whether you are married or *unmarried*.
3. An *unmarried* couple bought the house next door.

---

### unmatched

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmætʃt/ &middot; *uhn-MACHT*
**Русский:** непревзойдённый; непарный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *matched*
**Contrast:** For socks and shoes Americans usually say *mismatched*. On its own, *unmatched* almost always means *better than all the rest*.

Unmatched has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Better than anything else of its kind.** (непревзойдённый, несравненный)

1. Her record in the 400 meters is still *unmatched*.
2. The view from the top of the ridge is *unmatched*.

**2. Not forming a pair.** (непарный, разрозненный)

3. He wore two *unmatched* socks to school.
4. There is a whole drawer of *unmatched* gloves by the door.

---

### unmistakable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnmɪˈsteɪkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-mi-STAY-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** безошибочный, явный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *mistakable*, from *mistake*
**Contrast:** Watch the spelling: the final *e* of *mistake* drops out — *unmistakable*, not *unmistakeable*.

So clear that you cannot be wrong about it.

1. There was an *unmistakable* smell of gas in the hallway.
2. Her handwriting is *unmistakable*.
3. The look on his face was *unmistakable* — he had forgotten.

---

### unmitigated

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmɪtɪɡeɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-MIT-i-gay-tid*
**Русский:** полный, абсолютный (о чём-то плохом)
**Type:** emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only — *an unmitigated disaster*, never *the disaster was unmitigated*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *mitigated*, from *mitigate* 'to make less severe'
**Contrast:** It nearly always sits in front of a bad noun: *an unmitigated disaster*, *an unmitigated failure*, *unmitigated nonsense*.

Complete, with nothing to soften it.

1. Opening night was an *unmitigated* disaster.
2. Calling the merger an *unmitigated* failure is putting it kindly.
3. What he wrote in that letter was *unmitigated* nonsense.

---

### unmotivated

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmoʊtɪveɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-MOH-ti-vay-tid*
**Русский:** немотивированный, безынициативный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unmotivated*, *most unmotivated*
**Form:** un- + *motivated*
**Opposite:** *motivated*

Having no desire to work or make an effort.

1. By March the whole class was tired and *unmotivated*.
2. He isn't lazy, just *unmotivated* by this particular job.
3. Working from home left her *more unmotivated* than she expected.

---

### unmoved

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈmuːvd/ &middot; *uhn-MOOVD*
**Русский:** равнодушный, не тронутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *she was unmoved*, not *an unmoved judge*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** un- + *moved*

Feeling no emotion or pity about something.

1. The jury seemed *unmoved* by his apology.
2. She listened to the whole story *unmoved*.
3. Voters were *unmoved* by the last-minute ads.

---

### unnamed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈneɪmd/ &middot; *uhn-NAYMD*
**Русский:** неназванный, безымянный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — usually before a noun: *an unnamed source*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *named*

Whose name is not given.

1. Two *unnamed* officials confirmed the story.
2. The money came from an *unnamed* donor.
3. The buyer wishes to stay *unnamed*.

---

### unnatural

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈnætʃərəl/ &middot; *uhn-NACH-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** неестественный; противоестественный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unnatural*, *most unnatural*
**Form:** un- + *natural*
**Opposite:** *natural*

Unnatural has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not normal; going against the way things usually are.** (неестественный, ненормальный)

1. The room was filled with an *unnatural* silence.
2. Eighty degrees in February feels *unnatural*.

**2. Forced; not the way a person really behaves.** (наигранный, деланный)

3. His laugh sounded *unnatural* on camera.
4. She held the smile so long that it looked *unnatural*.
5. Reading from a script makes some people sound *more unnatural* than they really are.

---

### unnecessary

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈnɛsəsɛri/ &middot; *uhn-NES-uh-ser-ee*
**Русский:** ненужный, лишний
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *completely* or *totally*
**Form:** un- + *necessary*
**Opposite:** *necessary*
**Contrast:** Russian speakers often reach for *needless*. *Unnecessary* is the everyday word; *needless* survives mainly in *needless to say*.

Not needed.

1. The surgery turned out to be *unnecessary*.
2. You made an *unnecessary* trip — I could have emailed it.
3. All that shouting was completely *unnecessary*.

---

### unnoticed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈnoʊtɪst/ &middot; *uhn-NOH-tist*
**Русский:** незамеченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — almost always after *go*, *pass* or *slip*: *the typo went unnoticed*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *noticed*
**Contrast:** It lives in the pattern *go unnoticed* or *pass unnoticed*. Before a noun it sounds wrong: say *a mistake nobody caught*, not *an unnoticed mistake*.

Not seen or noticed by anyone.

1. The typo went *unnoticed* until the book was printed.
2. She slipped out of the party *unnoticed*.
3. A leak that size does not stay *unnoticed* for long.

---

### unobstructed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəbˈstrʌktɪd/ &middot; *uhn-uhb-STRUK-tid*
**Русский:** беспрепятственный, ничем не загороженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *obstructed*
**Opposite:** *obstructed*

With nothing standing in the way.

1. The room has an *unobstructed* view of the lake.
2. Keep the fire exit *unobstructed* at all times.
3. From row A the stage is completely *unobstructed*.

---

### unobtrusive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəbˈtruːsɪv/ &middot; *uhn-uhb-TROO-siv*
**Русский:** ненавязчивый, незаметный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unobtrusive*, *most unobtrusive*
**Form:** un- + *obtrusive*
**Opposite:** *obtrusive*

Not attracting attention; quiet and easy to ignore.

1. The security cameras are small and *unobtrusive*.
2. A good waiter is friendly but *unobtrusive*.
3. We picked the *most unobtrusive* table in the room.

---

### unoccupied

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɑːkjəpaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-AHK-yuh-pyd*
**Русский:** незанятый, пустующий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *occupied*
**Opposite:** *occupied*

Empty; with nobody living in it or using it.

1. The house has been *unoccupied* since spring.
2. Take any *unoccupied* seat in the back.
3. Two offices on that floor are still *unoccupied*.

---

### unofficial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈfɪʃəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-FISH-uhl*
**Русский:** неофициальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *official*
**Opposite:** *official*
**Contrast:** *Unofficial* means *not confirmed by whoever is in charge*; *informal* means *relaxed in style*. An *unofficial* count is not yet certified; an *informal* dinner has no dress code.

Not confirmed or approved by the people in charge.

1. The *unofficial* count has her ahead by two hundred votes.
2. Sunday practice is *unofficial*, so nobody has to come.
3. He is the team's *unofficial* captain.

---

### unopened

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈoʊpənd/ &middot; *uhn-OH-puhnd*
**Русский:** нераспечатанный, неоткрытый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *opened*

Still sealed or closed; never opened.

1. There is an *unopened* bottle of wine in the pantry.
2. Her mail sat *unopened* for two weeks.
3. The store takes returns only on *unopened* boxes.

---

### unopposed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈpoʊzd/ &middot; *uhn-uh-POHZD*
**Русский:** не встречающий сопротивления; без соперников
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb: *she ran unopposed*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *opposed*

With nobody competing against you or arguing against it.

1. She ran *unopposed* for a second term.
2. The motion passed *unopposed*.
3. The bill cleared the committee *unopposed*.

---

### unorganized

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɔːrɡənaɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-OR-guh-nyzd*
**Русский:** неорганизованный, беспорядочный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unorganized*, *most unorganized*
**Form:** un- + *organized*
**Opposite:** *organized*
**Contrast:** For a messy person or room, *disorganized* is the usual word. *Unorganized* is common in American speech and is the standard term for workers with no union: *an unorganized workforce*.

Not arranged or planned; without any order.

1. My notes for this class are completely *unorganized*.
2. The move was *unorganized* from the first day.
3. Their closet is *more unorganized* than mine, which is saying something.

---

### unoriginal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnəˈrɪdʒənəl/ &middot; *uhn-uh-RIJ-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** неоригинальный, банальный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unoriginal*, *most unoriginal*
**Form:** un- + *original*
**Opposite:** *original*

Copied from, or too much like, something that already exists.

1. The plot is *unoriginal*, but the acting is good.
2. My costume was *unoriginal* — three other people came as pirates.
3. Nothing is *more unoriginal* than a band name that starts with *The*.

---

### unorthodox

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈɔːrθədɑːks/ &middot; *uhn-OR-thuh-dahks*
**Русский:** нетрадиционный, необычный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unorthodox*, *most unorthodox*
**Form:** un- + *orthodox*
**Opposite:** *orthodox*, *conventional*
**Contrast:** Lowercase *unorthodox* just means *done in an unusual way*. Capitalized *Orthodox* names a church or a branch of Judaism — a different word.

Not done the usual way.

1. She has an *unorthodox* pitching motion that somehow works.
2. His teaching methods are *unorthodox* but effective.
3. The *most unorthodox* solution turned out to be the cheapest.

---

### unpaid

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpeɪd/ &middot; *uhn-PAYD*
**Русский:** неоплаченный; неоплачиваемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *paid*
**Opposite:** *paid*

Unpaid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not yet paid, said of a bill or a debt.** (неоплаченный, непогашенный)

1. Two invoices from March are still *unpaid*.
2. They shut off the water over an *unpaid* bill.

**2. Done without pay, said of work or leave.** (неоплачиваемый)

3. She took three months of *unpaid* leave.
4. Most of the campaign staff were *unpaid* volunteers.

---

### unpalatable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpælətəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-PAL-uh-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** невкусный; неприятный, тяжёлый (о правде)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unpalatable*, *most unpalatable*
**Form:** un- + *palatable*, from *palate* 'the roof of the mouth'

Unpalatable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Unpleasant to eat.** (невкусный, несъедобный)

1. The soup was cold and frankly *unpalatable*.
2. Hospital food has an *unpalatable* reputation.

**2. Hard to accept.** (неприятный, неудобный (о правде, выборе))

3. The report contains some *unpalatable* facts about the school.
4. Both options were *unpalatable*, so we waited.
5. The longer he stalled, the *more unpalatable* the choices became.

---

### unparalleled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpɛrəlɛld/ &middot; *uhn-PER-uh-leld*
**Русский:** беспрецедентный, непревзойдённый
**Type:** emphasizing
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *paralleled*, from *parallel*
**Contrast:** Watch the spelling: one *r*, two *l*'s in the middle, one *l* at the end — *un-par-al-leled*.

Greater or better than anything else of its kind.

1. The museum has an *unparalleled* collection of Native American baskets.
2. She brings *unparalleled* energy to the job.
3. The storm caused damage *unparalleled* in the county's history.

---

### unpasteurized

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpæstʃəraɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-PAS-chuh-ryzd*
**Русский:** непастеризованный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *pasteurized*, from the chemist Louis *Pasteur*
**Contrast:** American spelling ends in *-ized*; British books print *unpasteurised*.

Not heated to kill bacteria, said of milk, juice or cheese.

1. *Unpasteurized* milk cannot be sold across state lines.
2. The cheese is made from *unpasteurized* goat's milk.
3. Doctors tell pregnant women to avoid anything *unpasteurized*.

---

### unpatriotic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnpeɪtriˈɑːtɪk/ &middot; *uhn-pay-tree-AH-tik*
**Русский:** непатриотичный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unpatriotic*, *most unpatriotic*
**Form:** un- + *patriotic*
**Opposite:** *patriotic*
**Contrast:** In American English the first syllable rhymes with *pay*: *uhn-pay-tree-AH-tik*. British speakers say *pat-* instead.

Showing no loyalty to your country, or seeming not to.

1. Criticizing a war is not *unpatriotic*.
2. He was called *unpatriotic* for refusing to stand.
3. Nothing is *more unpatriotic*, she argued, than lying to voters.

---

### unpaved

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpeɪvd/ &middot; *uhn-PAYVD*
**Русский:** немощёный, грунтовый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *paved*
**Opposite:** *paved*

Not covered with asphalt or concrete.

1. The last two miles of the road are *unpaved*.
2. An *unpaved* driveway leads up to the cabin.
3. Half the streets in the old town are still *unpaved*.

---

### unperturbed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnpərˈtɜːrbd/ &middot; *uhn-pur-TURBD*
**Русский:** невозмутимый, спокойный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *he seemed unperturbed*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** un- + *perturbed*

Calm; not worried or upset by what is happening.

1. The cat sat *unperturbed* while the dog barked at it.
2. She was *unperturbed* by the boos from the crowd.
3. He looked *unperturbed* when the lights went out.

---

### unplanned

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈplænd/ &middot; *uhn-PLAND*
**Русский:** незапланированный, случайный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *planned*
**Opposite:** *planned*

Not arranged in advance; happening by chance.

1. We made an *unplanned* stop in Memphis.
2. The pregnancy was *unplanned* but very welcome.
3. The best parts of the trip were all *unplanned*.

---

### unplayable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpleɪəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-PLAY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** непригодный для игры; неиграбельный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *playable*
**Opposite:** *playable*

Unplayable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Impossible to play on, said of a field or a golf lie.** (непригодный для игры (о поле))

1. Three days of rain left the field *unplayable*.
2. His ball came to rest in a bush, *unplayable*.

**2. Impossible to play, said of a game, a disc or a recording.** (неиграбельный; не воспроизводящийся)

3. The game shipped so buggy that it was *unplayable* for a month.
4. The old DVD is scratched and *unplayable*.

---

### unpleasant

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈplɛzənt/ &middot; *uhn-PLEZ-uhnt*
**Русский:** неприятный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unpleasant*, *most unpleasant*
**Form:** un- + *pleasant*
**Opposite:** *pleasant*

Unpleasant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not enjoyable; leaving you with a bad feeling.** (неприятный, противный)

1. There was an *unpleasant* smell coming from the basement.
2. That flight was the *most unpleasant* six hours of my life.

**2. Rude or unfriendly, said of a person.** (неприветливый, грубый)

3. The clerk was openly *unpleasant* to everyone in line.
4. He can be *unpleasant* when he hasn't slept.

---

### unpolished

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpɑːlɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-PAH-lisht*
**Русский:** неотшлифованный; сырой, неотработанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unpolished*, *most unpolished*
**Form:** un- + *polished*
**Opposite:** *polished*

Unpolished has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not made smooth or shiny.** (неполированный, неотшлифованный)

1. *Unpolished* floors show every scratch.
2. The stone is *unpolished*, so it looks dull until you wet it.

**2. Rough; not yet worked into finished shape.** (сырой, неотработанный)

3. The draft is *unpolished*, but the argument is all there.
4. His delivery is *unpolished*, and audiences like him for it.
5. Her early songs were *more unpolished* and, some say, better.

---

### unpopular

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpɑːpjələr/ &middot; *uhn-PAH-pyuh-lur*
**Русский:** непопулярный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unpopular*, *most unpopular*
**Takes:** *with* + person
**Form:** un- + *popular*
**Opposite:** *popular*

Disliked by most people.

1. The new tax was *unpopular* with almost everyone.
2. She made the *unpopular* decision to cancel the trip.
3. The mayor is *more unpopular* now than when she took office.

---

### unprecedented

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈprɛsɪdɛntɪd/ &middot; *uhn-PRES-i-den-tid*
**Русский:** беспрецедентный, небывалый
**Type:** emphasizing
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *precedented*, from *precedent* 'an earlier case'
**Contrast:** Stress falls on the second syllable and the vowel is short: *uhn-PRES-i-den-tid*, never *un-pre-SEE-*.

Never having happened before.

1. The city saw *unprecedented* flooding in October.
2. She won three titles in one year, an *unprecedented* run.
3. Demand for the vaccine was *unprecedented*.

---

### unpredictable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnprɪˈdɪktəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-prih-DIK-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** непредсказуемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unpredictable*, *most unpredictable*
**Form:** un- + *predictable*
**Opposite:** *predictable*

Impossible to know in advance; changing without warning.

1. Mountain weather in April is *unpredictable*.
2. He is a good player but *unpredictable* under pressure.
3. The market has been *more unpredictable* than usual this year.

---

### unprepared

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnprɪˈpɛrd/ &middot; *uhn-prih-PAIRD*
**Русский:** неподготовленный, не готовый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unprepared*, *most unprepared*
**Takes:** *for* + noun; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** un- + *prepared*
**Opposite:** *prepared*

Not ready for what is about to happen.

1. We were completely *unprepared* for the cold.
2. He walked into the exam *unprepared*.
3. She was *unprepared* to answer questions about the layoffs.

---

### unpretentious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnprɪˈtɛnʃəs/ &middot; *uhn-prih-TEN-shuhs*
**Русский:** непритязательный, скромный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unpretentious*, *most unpretentious*
**Form:** un- + *pretentious*
**Opposite:** *pretentious*

Simple and modest; not trying to seem important.

1. It's an *unpretentious* little diner with very good pie.
2. For a famous writer he is remarkably *unpretentious*.
3. The *most unpretentious* house on the street is also the oldest.

---

### unprincipled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈprɪnsəpəld/ &middot; *uhn-PRIN-suh-puhld*
**Русский:** беспринципный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unprincipled*, *most unprincipled*
**Form:** un- + *principled*, from *principle*
**Opposite:** *principled*

Willing to do wrong things to get what you want.

1. An *unprincipled* landlord will find a way around the rule.
2. The whole campaign was *unprincipled* from the first week.
3. He is the *most unprincipled* person I have ever worked for.

---

### unprocessed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈprɑːsɛst/ &middot; *uhn-PRAH-sest*
**Русский:** необработанный; нерафинированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *processed*
**Opposite:** *processed*

Unprocessed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not changed from its natural state, said of food.** (необработанный, натуральный)

1. The diet is built around *unprocessed* foods.
2. *Unprocessed* oats take longer to cook than instant ones.

**2. Not yet dealt with, said of paperwork or data.** (необработанный, нерассмотренный)

3. Thousands of claims are still *unprocessed*.
4. My refund sat *unprocessed* for six weeks.

---

### unproductive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnprəˈdʌktɪv/ &middot; *uhn-pruh-DUK-tiv*
**Русский:** непродуктивный, безрезультатный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unproductive*, *most unproductive*
**Form:** un- + *productive*
**Opposite:** *productive*

Producing little or nothing useful.

1. The meeting was two *unproductive* hours.
2. Arguing about who is to blame is *unproductive*.
3. That was the *most unproductive* week I have had all year.

---

### unprofessional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnprəˈfɛʃənəl/ &middot; *uhn-pruh-FESH-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** непрофессиональный, недопустимый (о поведении)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unprofessional*, *most unprofessional*
**Form:** un- + *professional*
**Opposite:** *professional*
**Contrast:** In American English it is almost always about behavior, not skill. Someone who does poor work is *incompetent*; someone who shouts at a client is *unprofessional*.

Not meeting the standards of behavior expected at work.

1. Yelling at a customer is *unprofessional*.
2. The email was *unprofessional* and a little cruel.
3. Nothing looks *more unprofessional* than a typo in the subject line.

---

### unprofitable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈprɑːfɪtəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-PRAH-fi-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** убыточный, нерентабельный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unprofitable*, *most unprofitable*
**Form:** un- + *profitable*
**Opposite:** *profitable*

Not making money.

1. They closed three *unprofitable* stores.
2. The airline's shortest routes are the *most unprofitable*.
3. Farming that land is *unprofitable* without subsidies.

---

### unprompted

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈprɑːmptɪd/ &middot; *uhn-PRAHMP-tid*
**Русский:** непрошеный, сделанный по собственной инициативе
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *prompted*
**Contrast:** The adverb *unpromptedly* exists but is rare; Americans use the adjective where Russian would use an adverb: *he offered to help, unprompted*.

Done without anyone asking or suggesting it.

1. He offered to pay, completely *unprompted*.
2. An *unprompted* apology means more than one you had to ask for.
3. She named all fifty states *unprompted*.

---

### unpronounceable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnprəˈnaʊnsəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-pruh-NOWN-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** непроизносимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *pronounceable*, from *pronounce*
**Contrast:** Keep the *e* of *pronounce* before *-able*: *pronounceable*, *unpronounceable*.

Too difficult to say out loud.

1. The ingredient list is full of *unpronounceable* chemicals.
2. Her last name is beautiful and, for most Americans, *unpronounceable*.
3. He gave the dog an *unpronounceable* Welsh name.

---

### unprotected

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnprəˈtɛktɪd/ &middot; *uhn-pruh-TEK-tid*
**Русский:** незащищённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *protected*
**Opposite:** *protected*

With nothing to keep it safe from harm.

1. Don't leave your laptop *unprotected* on an open network.
2. The beach is *unprotected* from the north wind.
3. *Unprotected* seedlings won't survive a frost.

---

### unproven

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpruːvən/ &middot; *uhn-PROO-vuhn*
**Русский:** недоказанный; непроверенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *proven*
**Contrast:** *Unproven* and *unproved* are both correct, but *unproven* is far more common in American writing.

Unproven has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not shown to be true.** (недоказанный)

1. The claim is *unproven* and probably false.
2. *Unproven* remedies sell very well online.

**2. Not yet tested in real conditions.** (непроверенный, необкатанный)

3. The team is starting an *unproven* quarterback.
4. They bet the company on an *unproven* technology.

---

### unprovoked

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnprəˈvoʊkt/ &middot; *uhn-pruh-VOHKT*
**Русский:** неспровоцированный, беспричинный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *provoked*

Happening without anything being done to cause it.

1. It was an *unprovoked* attack on a man waiting for a bus.
2. The report lists six *unprovoked* shark encounters last year.
3. Her outburst was sudden and *unprovoked*.

---

### unpublished

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpʌblɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-PUB-lisht*
**Русский:** неопубликованный, неизданный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *published*
**Opposite:** *published*

Not printed or made public.

1. Three of her novels are still *unpublished*.
2. The library bought a box of *unpublished* letters.
3. The study remains *unpublished* two years after it was finished.

---

### unpunished

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈpʌnɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-PUN-isht*
**Русский:** безнаказанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — almost always after *go*: *the crime went unpunished*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *punished*
**Contrast:** It lives in the pattern *go unpunished*. Before a noun it sounds wrong: say *a crime nobody was punished for*.

Not punished.

1. The vandalism went *unpunished*.
2. No good deed goes *unpunished*.
3. He can't let an insult go *unpunished*.

---

### unqualified

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkwɑːlɪfaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-KWAH-li-fyd*
**Русский:** неквалифицированный; безоговорочный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *for* + noun; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** un- + *qualified*
**Contrast:** Two almost opposite feelings. After a verb it means *not trained enough*: *he is unqualified*. Before a noun like *success* or *support* it means *total*: *an unqualified success*.

Unqualified has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not having the training, skills or right to do something.** (неквалифицированный, не имеющий права)

1. He is *unqualified* for the job and everyone knows it.
2. An *unqualified* aide was left in charge of the class.
3. She is *unqualified* to give medical advice.

**2. Complete, with nothing held back.** (безоговорочный, полный)

4. The concert was an *unqualified* success.
5. She gave the plan her *unqualified* support.

---

### unquestionable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkwɛstʃənəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-KWES-chuh-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** несомненный, бесспорный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *questionable*
**Opposite:** *questionable*

Certain; impossible to doubt.

1. Her talent is *unquestionable*.
2. The evidence for the link is by now *unquestionable*.
3. He has an *unquestionable* right to see the file.

---

### unquestioning

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈkwɛstʃənɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-KWES-chuh-ning*
**Русский:** беспрекословный, слепой
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *unquestioning loyalty*, *unquestioning obedience*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *questioning*
**Contrast:** Don't mix it up with *unquestionable*. *Unquestioning loyalty* is loyalty that never asks why; *unquestionable loyalty* is loyalty nobody can doubt.

Accepting something completely, without ever asking why.

1. The group demands *unquestioning* loyalty.
2. He gave the coach four years of *unquestioning* obedience.
3. *Unquestioning* trust in a website is how people get scammed.

---

### unranked

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈræŋkt/ &middot; *uhn-RANKT*
**Русский:** не входящий в рейтинг, без места в рейтинге
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *ranked*
**Opposite:** *ranked*

Not among the teams or players on the official ranking list.

1. An *unranked* team beat the number two seed.
2. She was *unranked* coming into the tournament.
3. The Tigers open the season *unranked* for the first time in years.

---

### unreachable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈriːtʃəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-REE-chuh-buhl*
**Русский:** недосягаемый; недоступный для связи
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *reachable*

Unreachable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Impossible to get to or touch.** (недосягаемый, недоступный)

1. The nest sits on an *unreachable* ledge.
2. The top shelf is *unreachable* without a stool.

**2. Impossible to contact.** (недоступный, не выходящий на связь)

3. She's *unreachable* until Monday morning.
4. The server was *unreachable* all afternoon.

---

### unread

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈrɛd/ &middot; *uhn-RED*
**Русский:** непрочитанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *read*, the past participle
**Contrast:** Here *read* is the past participle, said like *red*: *uhn-RED*, never *uhn-REED*. The related *unreadable* does use the long vowel.

Not read by anyone.

1. I have four hundred *unread* emails.
2. The report sat *unread* on his desk for a month.
3. Mark all the messages as *unread*.

---

### unreadable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈriːdəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-REE-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** нечитаемый; неразборчивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unreadable*, *most unreadable*
**Form:** un- + *readable*
**Contrast:** Note the vowel change: *unread* is *uhn-RED*, but *unreadable* is *uhn-REE-duh-buhl*.

Unreadable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Impossible to make out.** (неразборчивый, нечитаемый)

1. His handwriting is *unreadable*.
2. The disk is damaged and *unreadable*.

**2. Too dull or too difficult to get through.** (нечитабельный, невыносимо скучный)

3. The manual is technically accurate and completely *unreadable*.
4. It is the *most unreadable* book on the syllabus.

---

### unreal

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈriːəl/ &middot; *uhn-REE-uhl*
**Русский:** нереальный; невероятный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *real*
**Opposite:** *real*
**Contrast:** In casual American speech *unreal* is praise: *that concert was unreal* means it was fantastic.

Unreal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not real, or too strange to feel real.** (нереальный, как во сне)

1. Everything about that week felt *unreal*.
2. The colors in the photo look *unreal*.

**2. Amazing — informal.** (потрясающий, невероятный)

3. The view from the summit was *unreal*.
4. That catch in the ninth inning was *unreal*.

---

### unrealistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnriːəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *uhn-ree-uh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** нереалистичный, нереальный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unrealistic*, *most unrealistic*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive
**Form:** un- + *realistic*
**Opposite:** *realistic*

Not sensible about what is actually possible.

1. The deadline is *unrealistic*.
2. It's *unrealistic* to expect a reply the same day.
3. Their budget was *more unrealistic* than the schedule.

---

### unrealized

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈriːəlaɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-REE-uh-lyzd*
**Русский:** нереализованный; нефиксированный (о прибыли)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *unrealized potential*, *unrealized gains*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *realized*
**Contrast:** American spelling ends in *-ized*; British books print *unrealised*.

Unrealized has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Never achieved or developed.** (нереализованный, несбывшийся)

1. The whole article is about *unrealized* potential.
2. His plan for the park is one more *unrealized* dream.

**2. Existing on paper but not yet turned into cash, said of gains or losses.** (нефиксированный, бумажный (о прибыли, убытках))

3. You owe no tax on *unrealized* gains.
4. The fund is carrying a large *unrealized* loss.

---

### unreasonable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈriːzənəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-REE-zuh-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неразумный, необоснованный; чрезмерный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unreasonable*, *most unreasonable*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive
**Form:** un- + *reasonable*
**Opposite:** *reasonable*

Unreasonable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not fair or sensible.** (неразумный, несправедливый)

1. Her boss makes *unreasonable* demands.
2. It isn't *unreasonable* to ask for two days' notice.
3. He gets *more unreasonable* the later the meeting runs.

**2. Far greater than is fair, said of a price or an amount.** (чрезмерный, непомерный)

4. They charge an *unreasonable* amount for parking.
5. The rent increase was plainly *unreasonable*.

---

### unrecognizable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɛkəɡˈnaɪzəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-rek-uhg-NY-zuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неузнаваемый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *recognizable*
**Contrast:** American spelling uses *z*: *unrecognizable*. British books print *unrecognisable*.

So changed that you cannot tell what or who it is.

1. Downtown is *unrecognizable* after ten years away.
2. He shaved his beard and was almost *unrecognizable*.
3. The fire left the car *unrecognizable*.

---

### unrecognized

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈrɛkəɡnaɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-REK-uhg-nyzd*
**Русский:** непризнанный; неузнанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *recognized*

Unrecognized has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not noticed or identified.** (незамеченный, неузнанный)

1. The disease often goes *unrecognized* for years.
2. She walked through the hotel lobby *unrecognized*.

**2. Not officially accepted.** (непризнанный)

3. The territory is an *unrecognized* state.
4. His degree is *unrecognized* in the United States.

---

### unrecoverable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈkʌvərəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-rih-KUV-ur-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** невосстановимый, безвозвратный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *recoverable*
**Opposite:** *recoverable*

Impossible to get back.

1. The files on the burned drive are *unrecoverable*.
2. Once you empty the trash, deleted photos may be *unrecoverable*.
3. Most of that loan is *unrecoverable*.

---

### unrefined

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈfaɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-rih-FYND*
**Русский:** нерафинированный; грубый, неотёсанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unrefined*, *most unrefined*
**Form:** un- + *refined*
**Opposite:** *refined*

Unrefined has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not processed; still close to its natural state.** (нерафинированный, неочищенный)

1. The recipe calls for *unrefined* coconut oil.
2. *Unrefined* sugar keeps more of the molasses.

**2. Rough; lacking polish or good manners.** (грубый, неотёсанный)

3. His humor is *unrefined* but never mean.
4. She thought their taste in furniture was *unrefined*.
5. The earlier draft was *more unrefined*, and funnier for it.

---

### unregistered

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈrɛdʒɪstərd/ &middot; *uhn-REJ-i-sturd*
**Русский:** незарегистрированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *registered*
**Opposite:** *registered*

Not entered on an official list.

1. Driving an *unregistered* car will get you a ticket.
2. Thousands of eligible voters are still *unregistered*.
3. The gun turned out to be *unregistered*.

---

### unregulated

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈrɛɡjəleɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-REG-yuh-lay-tid*
**Русский:** нерегулируемый, без государственного контроля
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *regulated*
**Opposite:** *regulated*

Not controlled by government rules.

1. The supplement industry is largely *unregulated*.
2. *Unregulated* lending helped cause the crash.
3. He worries about an *unregulated* market for these chemicals.

---

### unrehearsed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈhɜːrst/ &middot; *uhn-rih-HURST*
**Русский:** неотрепетированный, импровизированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *rehearsed*
**Opposite:** *rehearsed*

Done without practicing it first.

1. The interview was completely *unrehearsed*.
2. They gave an *unrehearsed* performance and it worked.
3. Her answer sounded *unrehearsed*, which is why people believed it.

---

### unrelated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈleɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-rih-LAY-tid*
**Русский:** не связанный; не состоящий в родстве
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** un- + *related*
**Opposite:** *related*

Unrelated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not connected with something else.** (не связанный, посторонний)

1. The two cases are *unrelated*.
2. She quit for reasons *unrelated* to the lawsuit.
3. On an *unrelated* note, did anyone fix the printer?

**2. Not from the same family.** (не родственный, не состоящий в родстве)

4. The two Smiths in my class are *unrelated*.
5. They share a last name but are *unrelated*.

---

### unreleased

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈliːst/ &middot; *uhn-rih-LEEST*
**Русский:** невыпущенный, неизданный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *released*

Not yet made available to the public.

1. The album includes two *unreleased* tracks.
2. Fans trade *unreleased* demos online.
3. The movie has been finished but *unreleased* for two years.

---

### unrelenting

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈlɛntɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-rih-LEN-ting*
**Русский:** неослабевающий, беспощадный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *relenting*, from *relent* 'to give in'

Never stopping or getting any weaker.

1. The heat in August was *unrelenting*.
2. She faced *unrelenting* pressure from the board.
3. His critics have been *unrelenting*.

---

### unreliable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈlaɪəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-rih-LY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** ненадёжный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unreliable*, *most unreliable*
**Form:** un- + *reliable*
**Opposite:** *reliable*

Not to be trusted to work, to be true, or to do what was promised.

1. The bus out to the airport is *unreliable*.
2. He is a warm person but an *unreliable* friend.
3. That website is the *most unreliable* source on the list.

---

### unremarkable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈmɑːrkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-rih-MAHR-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** ничем не примечательный, заурядный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unremarkable*, *most unremarkable*
**Form:** un- + *remarkable*
**Opposite:** *remarkable*

Ordinary; with nothing about it worth noticing.

1. From the street it's an *unremarkable* brick building.
2. The meal was pleasant and *unremarkable*.
3. He has one of the *most unremarkable* faces I have ever seen.

---

### unremitting

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈmɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-rih-MIT-ing*
**Русский:** непрекращающийся, неустанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *unremitting pressure*, *unremitting rain*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *remitting*, from *remit* 'to let up'
**Contrast:** *Unremitting* and *unrelenting* mean much the same thing, but *unremitting* is the more formal, more bookish of the two.

Continuing without a break, usually of something hard to bear.

1. Three weeks of *unremitting* rain flooded the fields.
2. The job demands *unremitting* attention to detail.
3. They lived under *unremitting* pressure to sell the farm.

---

### unrepentant

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈpɛntənt/ &middot; *uhn-rih-PEN-tuhnt*
**Русский:** нераскаявшийся, не сожалеющий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Form:** un- + *repentant*, from *repent*

Not sorry for something you did wrong.

1. He was *unrepentant* at his sentencing.
2. She is *unrepentant* about the post.
3. The paper called him an *unrepentant* liar.

---

### unreported

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈpɔːrtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-rih-POR-tid*
**Русский:** незаявленный, несообщённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *reported*

Not told to the authorities or to the public.

1. Most cases of the illness go *unreported*.
2. He had thousands of dollars in *unreported* income.
3. The crash stayed *unreported* for a week.

---

### unrequited

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈkwaɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-rih-KWY-tid*
**Русский:** безответный (о любви, чувстве)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — almost always before a noun, in the phrase *unrequited love*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *requited*, from the old verb *requite* 'to return in kind'
**Contrast:** You will meet it in one phrase, *unrequited love*, and hardly anywhere else.

Not returned, said of love or affection.

1. The whole album is about *unrequited* love.
2. He spent a year on an *unrequited* crush.
3. Her affection for him was *unrequited*.

---

### unresolved

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈzɑːlvd/ &middot; *uhn-rih-ZAHLVD*
**Русский:** нерешённый, неразрешённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *resolved*
**Opposite:** *resolved*

Not settled or decided.

1. The dispute is still *unresolved*.
2. There are two *unresolved* questions in the report.
3. He left the meeting with a lot of *unresolved* anger.

---

### unresponsive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈspɑːnsɪv/ &middot; *uhn-rih-SPAHN-siv*
**Русский:** не реагирующий; без сознания
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** un- + *responsive*
**Opposite:** *responsive*

Unresponsive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not answering or reacting to people.** (не реагирующий, не отвечающий)

1. The agency has been *unresponsive* to every complaint.
2. He sat *unresponsive* through the whole meeting.

**2. Not reacting to treatment, to input, or to anything at all.** (не поддающийся (лечению); не реагирующий; без сознания)

3. The tumor was *unresponsive* to chemotherapy.
4. The touchscreen goes *unresponsive* in cold weather.
5. Paramedics found her *unresponsive* on the kitchen floor.

---

### unrestrained

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈstreɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-rih-STRAYND*
**Русский:** необузданный, безудержный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *restrained*
**Opposite:** *restrained*

Not held back or kept under control.

1. The crowd greeted her with *unrestrained* cheering.
2. He wrote about the trip with *unrestrained* joy.
3. Her anger that night was completely *unrestrained*.

---

### unrestricted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnrɪˈstrɪktɪd/ &middot; *uhn-rih-STRIK-tid*
**Русский:** неограниченный, свободный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *restricted*
**Opposite:** *restricted*

With no rules limiting it.

1. Members get *unrestricted* access to the archive.
2. The grant money is *unrestricted*, so we can spend it where it's needed.
3. *Unrestricted* travel between the two countries resumed in June.

---

### unripe

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈraɪp/ &middot; *uhn-RYP*
**Русский:** незрелый, неспелый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unripe*, *most unripe*
**Form:** un- + *ripe*
**Opposite:** *ripe*

Not yet ready to eat or pick.

1. The peaches at the store were hard and *unripe*.
2. *Unripe* tomatoes are good fried.
3. The fruit at the bottom of the box was *more unripe* than the rest.

---

### unrivaled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈraɪvəld/ &middot; *uhn-RY-vuhld*
**Русский:** непревзойдённый, несравненный
**Type:** emphasizing
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *rivaled*, from *rival*
**Contrast:** American English writes one *l*: *unrivaled*. British books print *unrivalled*.

Better than anything or anyone else of its kind.

1. The store has an *unrivaled* selection of used records.
2. Her knowledge of the river is *unrivaled*.
3. For pure speed the train is *unrivaled*.

---

### unruly

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈruːli/ &middot; *uhn-ROO-lee*
**Русский:** непослушный, буйный; непокорный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *unrulier*, *unruliest*; also *more unruly*, *most unruly*
**Form:** un- + *rule* + -y
**Contrast:** It ends in *-ly* but it is an adjective, not an adverb — like *friendly* and *lonely*. There is no adverb *unrulily*; say *in an unruly way*.

Unruly has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hard to control, said of a person or a crowd.** (непослушный, буйный, неуправляемый)

1. An *unruly* fan was escorted out in the third inning.
2. The class got *unruly* right after lunch.
3. It was the *unruliest* crowd the arena had seen all year.

**2. Sticking out and hard to comb, said of hair.** (непослушный, торчащий (о волосах))

4. He pushed his *unruly* hair out of his eyes.
5. *Unruly* curls run in the family.

---

### unsafe

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈseɪf/ &middot; *uhn-SAYF*
**Русский:** небезопасный, опасный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *unsafe working conditions*, *The bridge is unsafe*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsafe*, *most unsafe*; *unsafer* is rare
**Takes:** *for* + person (*unsafe for children*); *to*-infinitive (*unsafe to drink*)
**Form:** un- + *safe*
**Opposite:** *safe*

Likely to cause harm, or not protected from harm.

1. The inspector closed the plant after finding *unsafe* conditions.
2. The water here is *unsafe* to drink.
3. She felt *unsafe* walking home after dark.

---

### unsaid

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsɛd/ &middot; *uhn-SED*
**Русский:** невысказанный, несказанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — after *leave*, *go* or *remain*: *left unsaid*, *what went unsaid*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *said*
**Contrast:** Not the same as *unspoken*. An *unspoken* rule is understood without being stated; something *unsaid* was simply never said, often on purpose: *Some things are better left unsaid*.

Not said, though it could have been.

1. Some things are better left *unsaid*.
2. A lot went *unsaid* at dinner that night.
3. The apology remained *unsaid* for twenty years.

---

### unsalted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsɔːltɪd/ &middot; *uhn-SAWL-tid*
**Русский:** несолёный, без соли
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *unsalted butter*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *salted*
**Opposite:** *salted*

Made or cooked without salt added.

1. The recipe calls for a stick of *unsalted* butter.
2. We buy *unsalted* nuts for the kids.
3. These crackers are completely *unsalted*.

---

### unsanitary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsænɪtɛri/ &middot; *uhn-SAN-ih-tair-ee*
**Русский:** антисанитарный, негигиеничный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsanitary*, *most unsanitary*
**Form:** un- + *sanitary*
**Opposite:** *sanitary*
**Contrast:** American writing uses *unsanitary*; the British word for the same thing is *insanitary*.

Dirty enough to be a danger to health.

1. The restaurant was shut down for *unsanitary* conditions.
2. Workers were living in crowded, *unsanitary* housing.
3. Storing raw meat that way is *unsanitary*.

---

### unsatisfactory

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˌsætɪsˈfæktəri/ &middot; *uhn-sat-is-FAK-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** неудовлетворительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsatisfactory*, *most unsatisfactory*
**Form:** un- + *satisfactory*
**Opposite:** *satisfactory*
**Contrast:** Three neighbors that learners mix up: a result is *unsatisfactory* (not good enough), a person is *unsatisfied* (not pleased), and an experience is *unsatisfying* (it leaves you wanting more).

Not good enough for what is needed.

1. His explanation was *unsatisfactory*, so we asked again.
2. The company gave us an *unsatisfactory* answer about the delay.
3. Two students received an *unsatisfactory* grade in the course.

---

### unsatisfied

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsætɪsfaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-SAT-is-fyd*
**Русский:** неудовлетворённый; неудовлетворённый (о спросе)
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsatisfied*, *most unsatisfied*
**Takes:** *with* + noun (*unsatisfied with the service*)
**Form:** un- + *satisfied*
**Opposite:** *satisfied*

Unsatisfied has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not pleased, because something fell short of what you wanted.** (неудовлетворённый, недовольный)

1. Customers who are *unsatisfied* can return the item for a refund.
2. She left the meeting *unsatisfied* with the answers.
3. He was *unsatisfied* even after a second helping.

**2. Not met or filled, said of a need, a demand or a condition.** (неудовлетворённый, невыполненный)

4. There is still a large *unsatisfied* demand for affordable housing.
5. The loan will not close while two conditions remain *unsatisfied*.

---

### unsatisfying

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsætɪsfaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-SAT-is-fy-ing*
**Русский:** не приносящий удовлетворения, пустой
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both — *an unsatisfying job*, *The ending was unsatisfying*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsatisfying*, *most unsatisfying*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling; un- + *satisfying*
**Opposite:** *satisfying*
**Contrast:** The *-ing* word describes the thing (*an unsatisfying movie*); the *-ed* word describes the person (*I was unsatisfied*).

Leaving you wanting more; not giving a feeling of satisfaction.

1. The movie had a rushed, *unsatisfying* ending.
2. A salad alone makes for an *unsatisfying* lunch.
3. After ten years the work had become *unsatisfying*.

---

### unsaturated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsætʃəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-SACH-uh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** ненасыщенный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *unsaturated fats*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *saturated*
**Opposite:** *saturated*

Of a fat or oil, having double bonds between carbon atoms, and so generally better for your health.

1. Olive oil is high in *unsaturated* fat.
2. The label lists both *unsaturated* and saturated fats.
3. Most of the fat in walnuts is *unsaturated*.

---

### unsaved

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈseɪvd/ &middot; *uhn-SAYVD*
**Русский:** несохранённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *unsaved changes*, *The document is unsaved*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *saved*

Not yet stored on a computer, so it can be lost.

1. A warning popped up about *unsaved* changes.
2. The laptop died and an hour of *unsaved* work went with it.
3. The draft is still *unsaved*, so do not close the tab.

---

### unsavory

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈseɪvəri/ &middot; *uhn-SAY-vuh-ree*
**Русский:** сомнительный, неприглядный; невкусный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsavory*, *most unsavory*
**Form:** un- + *savory*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *unsavory*; British books print *unsavoury*. The usual meaning today is moral, not about food.

Unsavory has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Morally unpleasant; the kind of person or business you would not want to be seen with.** (сомнительный, тёмный, неприглядный)

1. He got mixed up with some *unsavory* characters downtown.
2. The report revealed *unsavory* details about the company.
3. The bar has an *unsavory* reputation.

**2. Unpleasant to taste or smell.** (невкусный, неаппетитный)

4. The stew was gray and frankly *unsavory*.
5. An *unsavory* smell came from the refrigerator.

---

### unscathed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈskeɪðd/ &middot; *uhn-SKAYTHD*
**Русский:** невредимый, целый и невредимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after *escape*, *emerge*, *come through* or *survive*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun (*unscathed by the scandal*)
**Contrast:** It almost never stands before a noun. Say *He walked away unscathed*, not *an unscathed man*.

Not hurt or damaged at all by something dangerous.

1. Both drivers walked away *unscathed*.
2. The old church came through the fire *unscathed*.
3. Her career emerged *unscathed* from the scandal.

---

### unscheduled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈskɛdʒuːld/ &middot; *uhn-SKEJ-oold*
**Русский:** внеплановый, незапланированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *an unscheduled stop*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *scheduled*
**Opposite:** *scheduled*

Not planned in advance, and not on the official schedule.

1. The plane made an *unscheduled* landing in Denver.
2. We had an *unscheduled* visit from the fire marshal.
3. An *unscheduled* power outage shut the lab for a day.

---

### unscientific

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˌsaɪənˈtɪfɪk/ &middot; *uhn-sy-uhn-TIF-ik*
**Русский:** ненаучный
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unscientific*, *most unscientific*
**Form:** un- + *scientific*
**Opposite:** *scientific*

Not following the methods of science; not based on evidence.

1. My survey of six friends was thoroughly *unscientific*.
2. The claim rests on *unscientific* reasoning.
3. Testing one sample is an *unscientific* way to decide.

---

### unscripted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈskrɪptɪd/ &middot; *uhn-SKRIP-tid*
**Русский:** неотрепетированный, импровизированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unscripted*, *most unscripted*
**Form:** un- + *scripted*
**Opposite:** *scripted*

Not written or rehearsed in advance.

1. The best part of the show was an *unscripted* moment with the audience.
2. He gave an *unscripted* answer that made headlines.
3. Their conversation felt completely *unscripted*.

---

### unscrupulous

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈskruːpjələs/ &middot; *uhn-SKROO-pyuh-luhs*
**Русский:** беспринципный, недобросовестный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unscrupulous*, *most unscrupulous*
**Form:** un- + *scrupulous*
**Opposite:** *scrupulous*

Willing to cheat or hurt people to get what you want.

1. *Unscrupulous* landlords take advantage of new arrivals.
2. The fund was run by an *unscrupulous* broker.
3. He is ambitious but not *unscrupulous*.

---

### unsealed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsiːld/ &middot; *uhn-SEELD*
**Русский:** незапечатанный, вскрытый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *sealed*
**Opposite:** *sealed*

Not closed tightly, or no longer kept closed.

1. The envelope arrived *unsealed*.
2. Do not store flour in an *unsealed* container.
3. The court records were *unsealed* last week.

---

### unseasonable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsiːzənəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-SEE-zuh-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** не по сезону, несвойственный времени года
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *unseasonable warmth*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unseasonable*, *most unseasonable*
**Form:** un- + *seasonable*
**Contrast:** The adverb *unseasonably* is far more common than the adjective: *unseasonably warm for October*. Do not confuse it with *unseasoned*, which is about food without salt or spices.

Not the weather you expect at that time of year.

1. We got *unseasonable* heat in early April.
2. An *unseasonable* frost killed the young plants.
3. The rain was heavy and quite *unseasonable*.

---

### unseasoned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsiːzənd/ &middot; *uhn-SEE-zuhnd*
**Русский:** без приправ; неопытный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the 'inexperienced' sense goes before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unseasoned*, *most unseasoned*
**Form:** un- + *seasoned*
**Opposite:** *seasoned*

Unseasoned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cooked without salt, pepper or other flavorings.** (без приправ, несолёный)

1. Plain *unseasoned* chicken tastes like cardboard.
2. The rice came to the table *unseasoned*.

**2. Without experience in a job, especially compared with older colleagues.** (неопытный, необстрелянный)

3. The team started an *unseasoned* rookie at quarterback.
4. An *unseasoned* manager might have panicked.

---

### unsecured

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsɪˈkjʊrd/ &middot; *uhn-sih-KYURD*
**Русский:** необеспеченный (залогом); незащищённый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *secured*
**Opposite:** *secured*

Unsecured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a loan or debt, not backed by property the lender can take.** (необеспеченный залогом)

1. Credit card debt is *unsecured*, so the rate is higher.
2. The bank offers small *unsecured* loans to new customers.

**2. Not locked, fastened or protected.** (незакреплённый, незащищённый)

3. Never log in to your bank on an *unsecured* network.
4. The ladder was left *unsecured* in the back of the truck.

---

### unseeded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsiːdɪd/ &middot; *uhn-SEE-did*
**Русский:** несеяный (о спортсмене)
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** both — *an unseeded player*, *She was unseeded*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *seeded*
**Opposite:** *seeded*
**Contrast:** A sports word. In tennis the top players are ranked, or *seeded*, before a tournament; everyone else is *unseeded*.

Not given a ranking in a tournament draw.

1. An *unseeded* teenager beat the defending champion.
2. She went into the tournament *unseeded*.
3. Two *unseeded* players reached the semifinals.

---

### unseemly

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsiːmli/ &middot; *uhn-SEEM-lee*
**Русский:** неподобающий, неприличный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *an unseemly argument*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unseemly*, *most unseemly*
**Form:** -ly adjective, from *seemly*
**Contrast:** The *-ly* ending makes it look like an adverb, but it is an adjective. Say *He behaved in an unseemly way*, not *He behaved unseemly*.

Not proper for the occasion, in a way that embarrasses people.

1. There was an *unseemly* scramble for the last seats.
2. Arguing over money at a funeral is *unseemly*.
3. The senator called the remark *unseemly* and beneath the office.

---

### unseen

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsiːn/ &middot; *uhn-SEEN*
**Русский:** невидимый, незамеченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — also after the noun: *forces unseen*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *seen*
**Contrast:** *Sight unseen* is a fixed phrase meaning without looking at the thing first: *He bought the car sight unseen*.

Not seen or noticed by anyone.

1. She slipped out the back door *unseen*.
2. An *unseen* hand seems to be guiding the market.
3. The museum is showing previously *unseen* photographs.

---

### unselfish

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsɛlfɪʃ/ &middot; *uhn-SEL-fish*
**Русский:** бескорыстный, самоотверженный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unselfish*, *most unselfish*
**Form:** un- + *selfish*
**Opposite:** *selfish*

Putting other people first instead of yourself.

1. It was an *unselfish* decision to give up the room.
2. The point guard is famous for *unselfish* passing.
3. She is the most *unselfish* person I know.

---

### unsent

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsɛnt/ &middot; *uhn-SENT*
**Русский:** неотправленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *sent*

Written or prepared but never sent.

1. She kept a folder of *unsent* letters to her father.
2. The email sat *unsent* in my drafts for a week.
3. He found an *unsent* text on her phone.

---

### unsentimental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˌsɛntɪˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *uhn-sen-tih-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** несентиментальный, трезвый
**Type:** evaluative; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsentimental*, *most unsentimental*
**Form:** un- + *sentimental*
**Opposite:** *sentimental*

Looking at things plainly, without letting feelings soften the picture.

1. The book gives an *unsentimental* account of the war.
2. She was *unsentimental* about selling the old house.
3. He has an *unsentimental* view of what the team needs.

---

### unsettled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsɛtəld/ &middot; *uhn-SET-uhld*
**Русский:** нерешённый; беспокойный; неустойчивый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsettled*, *most unsettled*
**Form:** un- + *settled*

Unsettled has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not decided or finished.** (нерешённый, неулаженный)

1. The question of who pays is still *unsettled*.
2. Several claims remain *unsettled* after two years.
3. The law in this area is *unsettled*.

**2. Uneasy and worried, unable to relax.** (встревоженный, неспокойный)

4. The news left me *unsettled* all afternoon.
5. The baby was *unsettled* after the long flight.

**3. Of weather, likely to change and turn bad.** (неустойчивый (о погоде))

6. The forecast calls for *unsettled* weather through the weekend.
7. Conditions stayed *unsettled* until Thursday.

---

### unsexy

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsɛksi/ &middot; *uhn-SEK-see*
**Русский:** непривлекательный; скучный, невыигрышный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsexy*, *most unsexy*
**Form:** un- + *sexy*
**Opposite:** *sexy*

Unsexy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dull, not exciting, and so easy to ignore.** (скучный, невыигрышный, непривлекательный (о теме, работе))

1. Maintenance is the *unsexy* part of the job that keeps everything running.
2. It is an *unsexy* topic, but it decides the budget.
3. Reliable and boring beats exciting and broken, however *unsexy* that sounds.

**2. Not physically attractive.** (непривлекательный, несексуальный)

4. He complained that the uniform was deeply *unsexy*.
5. There is nothing *unsexy* about confidence.

---

### unshackled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈʃækəld/ &middot; *uhn-SHAK-uhld*
**Русский:** освобождённый, сбросивший оковы
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *from* or *by* + noun (*unshackled from the rules*)
**Form:** un- + *shackled*

Freed from something that was holding you back.

1. *Unshackled* from the old contract, the band recorded whatever it wanted.
2. Critics warned about an *unshackled* presidency.
3. He played like a man *unshackled*.

---

### unshakable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈʃeɪkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-SHAY-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** непоколебимый, твёрдый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *unshakable faith*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *shake* + -able
**Contrast:** Also spelled *unshakeable*; American writing prefers *unshakable*. It describes beliefs and feelings, not physical objects.

So firm that nothing can change it.

1. She has an *unshakable* belief in her students.
2. His confidence at the plate is *unshakable*.
3. The two men formed an *unshakable* friendship.

---

### unshaken

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈʃeɪkən/ &middot; *uhn-SHAY-kuhn*
**Русский:** непоколебленный, твёрдый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *his faith remained unshaken*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun (*unshaken by the defeat*)
**Form:** un- + *shaken*
**Contrast:** *Unshaken* says that something did not change under pressure; *unshakable* says that nothing ever could.

Still as firm as before, in spite of what happened.

1. Her faith in him was *unshaken*.
2. The coach seemed *unshaken* by the loss.
3. Support for the plan remains *unshaken*.

---

### unshaven

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈʃeɪvən/ &middot; *uhn-SHAY-vuhn*
**Русский:** небритый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *an unshaven face*, *He was unshaven*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *shaven*, an old participle of *shave*

Not having shaved, so the face looks rough.

1. He came to the door *unshaven* and half asleep.
2. An *unshaven* man in a gray coat was waiting outside.
3. She liked him better *unshaven*.

---

### unsightly

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsaɪtli/ &middot; *uhn-SYT-lee*
**Русский:** неприглядный, некрасивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *unsightly stains*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsightly*, *most unsightly*
**Form:** -ly adjective, from *sight*
**Contrast:** Another *-ly* adjective, never an adverb. It is about how something looks, not about eyesight.

Ugly to look at.

1. An *unsightly* pile of trash had built up behind the store.
2. The cream is supposed to hide *unsightly* scars.
3. The new fence is frankly *unsightly*.

---

### unsigned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsaɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-SYND*
**Русский:** неподписанный; без контракта
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *signed*
**Opposite:** *signed*

Unsigned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Without a signature, and so not yet valid.** (неподписанный)

1. The bank returned the *unsigned* check.
2. An *unsigned* note was taped to the door.
3. The contract is still *unsigned*.

**2. Of a musician or athlete, not under contract with a label or team.** (не имеющий контракта)

4. The festival gives a slot to one *unsigned* band each year.
5. He was *unsigned* until the second week of free agency.

---

### unsinkable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsɪŋkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-SINK-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** непотопляемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *sink* + -able

Impossible to sink, or, of a person, impossible to defeat.

1. The ship was advertised as *unsinkable*.
2. This little boat is practically *unsinkable*.
3. She is the *unsinkable* candidate who survives every scandal.

---

### unskilled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈskɪld/ &middot; *uhn-SKILD*
**Русский:** неквалифицированный
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* or *at* + noun (*unskilled in negotiation*)
**Form:** un- + *skilled*
**Opposite:** *skilled*

Unskilled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a person, having no special training for a job.** (неквалифицированный (о работнике))

1. The factory hired mostly *unskilled* workers.
2. He arrived in the city young and *unskilled*.

**2. Of work, needing no special training.** (не требующий квалификации (о работе))

3. *Unskilled* labor pays less than it used to.
4. Most of the summer jobs were *unskilled*.

---

### unsociable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsoʊʃəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-SOH-shuh-buhl*
**Русский:** необщительный, нелюдимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsociable*, *most unsociable*
**Form:** un- + *sociable*
**Opposite:** *sociable*
**Contrast:** *Unsociable* only means not fond of company. *Antisocial* is much stronger and means harmful to other people: *antisocial behavior*.

Not enjoying the company of other people.

1. He is not rude, just *unsociable* after a long day.
2. I felt *unsociable* and skipped the party.
3. Her *unsociable* roommate never came out of her room.

---

### unsold

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsoʊld/ &middot; *uhn-SOHLD*
**Русский:** непроданный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *sold*

Still not bought by anyone.

1. *Unsold* tickets go on sale at half price an hour before the show.
2. The house sat *unsold* for most of the year.
3. The store donates *unsold* bread to a shelter.

---

### unsolicited

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsəˈlɪsɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-suh-LIS-ih-tid*
**Русский:** незапрошенный, непрошеный
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *unsolicited advice*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *solicited*

Sent or given without being asked for.

1. My aunt is a rich source of *unsolicited* advice.
2. The company does not accept *unsolicited* manuscripts.
3. We get a dozen *unsolicited* emails a day.

---

### unsolvable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsɑːlvəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-SAHL-vuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неразрешимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *seemingly* or *apparently*
**Form:** un- + *solve* + -able
**Contrast:** *Unsolvable* means there is no possible answer; *unsolved* means nobody has found the answer yet.

Impossible to solve.

1. The puzzle turned out to be *unsolvable*.
2. Traffic here feels like an *unsolvable* problem.
3. No problem in this class is *unsolvable*.

---

### unsolved

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsɑːlvd/ &middot; *uhn-SAHLVD*
**Русский:** нераскрытый, нерешённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *solved*
**Opposite:** *solved*

Still without an answer, and in police work still without an arrest.

1. The case has been *unsolved* since 1978.
2. The city has dozens of *unsolved* burglaries.
3. One famous math problem remains *unsolved*.

---

### unsophisticated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsəˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-suh-FIS-tih-kay-tid*
**Русский:** простой, незамысловатый; наивный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsophisticated*, *most unsophisticated*
**Form:** un- + *sophisticated*
**Opposite:** *sophisticated*

Unsophisticated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of equipment or methods, simple and basic.** (простой, примитивный)

1. The attack used *unsophisticated* tools that anyone can download.
2. Their testing was *unsophisticated* but good enough.

**2. Of a person, having little experience of art, fashion or city life.** (простодушный, неискушённый)

3. He played an *unsophisticated* country lawyer.
4. I felt *unsophisticated* next to her friends from New York.

---

### unsound

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsaʊnd/ &middot; *uhn-SOWND*
**Русский:** несостоятельный; ненадёжный, аварийный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsound*, *most unsound*
**Form:** un- + *sound*
**Opposite:** *sound*
**Contrast:** In legal writing *of unsound mind* means not mentally able to make decisions. Outside that phrase, do not use *unsound* about people.

Unsound has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of reasoning or a plan, not based on good judgment or good evidence.** (несостоятельный, необоснованный)

1. The argument is *unsound* because it rests on one bad number.
2. Economists called the policy financially *unsound*.

**2. Of a building or structure, not safe or solid.** (аварийный, ненадёжный (о постройке))

3. The inspector found the roof structurally *unsound*.
4. They tore down the *unsound* porch.

---

### unsparing

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈspɛrɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-SPAIR-ing*
**Русский:** беспощадный; щедрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsparing*, *most unsparing*
**Takes:** *in* + noun or *-ing* (*unsparing in her criticism*)
**Form:** un- + *sparing*
**Contrast:** Two nearly opposite meanings, and only the context tells them apart: *unsparing criticism* is harsh, *unsparing generosity* is large-hearted.

Unsparing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Holding nothing back in criticism, and so harsh.** (беспощадный, не щадящий)

1. The review was *unsparing* about the acting.
2. She is *unsparing* in her judgment of her own work.

**2. Giving freely and without limit.** (щедрый, не жалеющий сил)

3. He was *unsparing* with his time when a student needed help.
4. Their *unsparing* support kept the shelter open.

---

### unspeakable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈspiːkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-SPEE-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** невыразимый, чудовищный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *almost* or *truly*
**Form:** un- + *speak* + -able

Too extreme to describe, almost always about something terrible.

1. The children suffered *unspeakable* cruelty.
2. What happened in that village was *unspeakable*.
3. She lived through *unspeakable* loss and kept working.

---

### unspecific

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnspəˈsɪfɪk/ &middot; *uhn-spuh-SIF-ik*
**Русский:** неконкретный, общий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unspecific*, *most unspecific*
**Form:** un- + *specific*
**Opposite:** *specific*
**Contrast:** In medicine the usual word is *nonspecific* (*nonspecific symptoms*), and in everyday speech Americans more often say *vague*.

Not giving details, so it could mean almost anything.

1. His promises were warm but *unspecific*.
2. The complaint was so *unspecific* that we could not act on it.
3. She gave an *unspecific* answer about the date.

---

### unspecified

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈspɛsɪfaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-SPES-uh-fyd*
**Русский:** неуказанный, неназванный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *an unspecified amount*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *specified*

Not named or stated, usually on purpose.

1. The case was settled for an *unspecified* sum.
2. He will be away for an *unspecified* period.
3. The company cited *unspecified* security concerns.

---

### unspectacular

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnspɛkˈtækjələr/ &middot; *uhn-spek-TAK-yuh-lur*
**Русский:** ничем не примечательный, заурядный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unspectacular*, *most unspectacular*
**Form:** un- + *spectacular*
**Opposite:** *spectacular*

Solid enough but not impressive or exciting.

1. He had a long, *unspectacular* career as a backup catcher.
2. The results were steady and *unspectacular*.
3. It was an *unspectacular* meal at a fair price.

---

### unspent

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈspɛnt/ &middot; *uhn-SPENT*
**Русский:** неизрасходованный, непотраченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
