**Form:** un- + *spent*

Not yet used up, said of money or energy.

1. *Unspent* funds go back to the state at the end of the year.
2. He came home with most of his allowance *unspent*.
3. The boy has a lot of *unspent* energy by bedtime.

---

### unspoiled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈspɔɪld/ &middot; *uhn-SPOYLD*
**Русский:** нетронутый, первозданный; неизбалованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unspoiled*, *most unspoiled*
**Form:** un- + *spoiled*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *unspoiled*; British books print *unspoilt*.

Unspoiled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a place, still beautiful because nothing has been built on it.** (нетронутый, первозданный)

1. They camped on an *unspoiled* stretch of coast.
2. The valley is one of the last *unspoiled* places in the state.

**2. Of a person, not made selfish by money or success.** (неизбалованный, не испорченный успехом)

3. Fame has left her completely *unspoiled*.
4. The children were polite and *unspoiled*.

---

### unspoken

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈspoʊkən/ &middot; *uhn-SPOH-kuhn*
**Русский:** негласный, невысказанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *an unspoken rule*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *spoken*
**Contrast:** An *unspoken* agreement is understood by everyone without being stated. Something *unsaid* was simply never said at all.

Understood by everyone but never stated out loud.

1. There is an *unspoken* rule about the parking spots.
2. They had an *unspoken* agreement never to mention it.
3. His disappointment stayed *unspoken*.

---

### unsportsmanlike

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈspɔːrtsmənlaɪk/ &middot; *uhn-SPORTS-muhn-lyk*
**Русский:** неспортивный, нечестный (в игре)
**Type:** evaluative; also compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *unsportsmanlike conduct*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsportsmanlike*, *most unsportsmanlike*
**Form:** un- + *sportsman* + -like
**Contrast:** *Unsportsmanlike conduct* is the name of the penalty in American football and basketball, so this is the form you hear on TV.

Not fair or not respectful toward opponents.

1. He was flagged for *unsportsmanlike* conduct.
2. Taunting the goalie is *unsportsmanlike*.
3. The coach apologized for his *unsportsmanlike* remarks.

---

### unstable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsteɪbəl/ &middot; *uhn-STAY-buhl*
**Русский:** неустойчивый; нестабильный; неуравновешенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unstable*, *most unstable*
**Form:** un- + *stable*
**Opposite:** *stable*

Unstable has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not firm, and likely to fall or move.** (неустойчивый, шаткий)

1. Do not stand on that ladder, it is *unstable*.
2. The shelf is *unstable* without the back panel.

**2. Likely to change suddenly for the worse, said of a situation.** (нестабильный, неустойчивый (о положении))

3. The region has been politically *unstable* for a decade.
4. Prices are *unstable* this quarter.

**3. Of a person, having sudden changes of mood or mental state.** (неуравновешенный, психически неустойчивый)

5. The note came from someone clearly *unstable*.
6. He grew more *unstable* in the months before the arrest.

---

### unstated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsteɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-STAY-tid*
**Русский:** невысказанный, не названный прямо
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *an unstated assumption*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *stated*

Not said directly, though it is part of the thinking.

1. The plan rests on an *unstated* assumption about growth.
2. His real reason went *unstated*.
3. There is an *unstated* rule that juniors answer the phone.

---

### unsteady

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈstɛdi/ &middot; *uhn-STED-ee*
**Русский:** неустойчивый, шаткий; дрожащий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsteady*, *most unsteady*
**Form:** un- + *steady*
**Opposite:** *steady*

Unsteady has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not firm on its feet or its base, so it might fall.** (неустойчивый, шаткий)

1. She was still *unsteady* on her feet after the surgery.
2. The table is *unsteady* on this floor.

**2. Shaking, or not staying at one level.** (дрожащий, неровный)

3. He poured the coffee with an *unsteady* hand.
4. Her voice was *unsteady* when she read the letter.

---

### unstinting

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈstɪntɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-STIN-ting*
**Русский:** щедрый, неустанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *unstinting support*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unstinting*, *most unstinting*
**Takes:** *in* + noun (*unstinting in his praise*)
**Form:** un- + *stinting*, from *stint* (to hold back)

Given generously and without holding anything back.

1. Her parents gave *unstinting* support through school.
2. He was *unstinting* in his praise of the team.
3. The town offered *unstinting* help to the families.

---

### unstoppable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈstɑːpəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-STAH-puh-buhl*
**Русский:** неудержимый, неостановимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *seemingly*, *virtually* or *truly*
**Form:** un- + *stop* + -able

Impossible to stop.

1. In the second half she was *unstoppable*.
2. The technology now looks *unstoppable*.
3. They built a seemingly *unstoppable* lead.

---

### unstructured

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈstrʌktʃərd/ &middot; *uhn-STRUHK-churd*
**Русский:** неструктурированный, свободный
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unstructured*, *most unstructured*
**Form:** un- + *structured*
**Opposite:** *structured*

Without a fixed plan, order or organization.

1. Kids need *unstructured* time to play.
2. The interview was deliberately *unstructured*.
3. Most of the data we collect is *unstructured*.

---

### unsubstantiated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsəbˈstænʃieɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-suhb-STAN-shee-ay-tid*
**Русский:** неподтверждённый, бездоказательный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *substantiated*

Not backed up by evidence.

1. The paper would not print *unsubstantiated* claims.
2. Two of the three complaints were *unsubstantiated*.
3. His story remains *unsubstantiated*.

---

### unsubtle

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsʌtəl/ &middot; *uhn-SUHT-uhl*
**Русский:** прямолинейный, грубый, неделикатный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsubtle*, *most unsubtle*
**Form:** un- + *subtle*
**Opposite:** *subtle*
**Contrast:** The *b* is silent, as in *subtle*: *uhn-SUHT-uhl*.

Obvious and heavy-handed, when something gentler was called for.

1. He made an *unsubtle* hint about the bill.
2. The movie has an *unsubtle* message about greed.
3. Her glance at the clock was distinctly *unsubtle*.

---

### unsuccessful

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsəkˈsɛsfəl/ &middot; *uhn-suhk-SES-fuhl*
**Русский:** неудачный, безуспешный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsuccessful*, *most unsuccessful*
**Takes:** *in* + noun or *-ing* (*unsuccessful in finding work*)
**Form:** un- + *successful*
**Opposite:** *successful*

Not achieving what was wanted.

1. After an *unsuccessful* first surgery, he tried a different hospital.
2. She was *unsuccessful* in her run for mayor.
3. The rescue attempt was *unsuccessful*.

---

### unsuitable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsuːtəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-SOO-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неподходящий, непригодный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *unsuitable shoes*, *The room is unsuitable*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsuitable*, *most unsuitable*
**Takes:** *for* + noun or *-ing* (*unsuitable for children*)
**Form:** un- + *suitable*
**Opposite:** *suitable*
**Contrast:** *Unsuitable* goes comfortably before a noun; *unsuited* almost always follows a verb and takes *to* or *for*.

Not right for a particular purpose, person or occasion.

1. The film is *unsuitable* for young children.
2. She wore shoes that were *unsuitable* for hiking.
3. They called it an *unsuitable* site for housing.

---

### unsuited

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈsuːtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-SOO-tid*
**Русский:** неподходящий, не приспособленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *He is unsuited to the job*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsuited*, *most unsuited*
**Takes:** *to* or *for* + noun (*unsuited to office work*)
**Form:** un- + *suited*
**Contrast:** Do not use it before a noun. Say *an unsuitable candidate*, but *The candidate is unsuited to the job*.

Not having the right qualities for something or someone.

1. He is *unsuited* to a job with that much travel.
2. The soil here is *unsuited* for corn.
3. They were badly *unsuited* to each other.

---

### unsung

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsʌŋ/ &middot; *uhn-SUHNG*
**Русский:** невоспетый, незаслуженно забытый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *an unsung hero*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *sung*, from the old sense of songs praising someone
**Contrast:** In practice it appears almost only in *unsung hero* and *unsung heroes*.

Doing important work that nobody praises.

1. School nurses are the *unsung* heroes of the district.
2. He was the *unsung* star of the defense.
3. The book is about the *unsung* women who ran the lab.

---

### unsupervised

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈsuːpərvaɪzd/ &middot; *uhn-SOO-pur-vyzd*
**Русский:** без присмотра, безнадзорный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *unsupervised children*, *The kids were unsupervised*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *supervised*
**Opposite:** *supervised*

Without an adult or a manager watching.

1. Never leave a toddler *unsupervised* near the pool.
2. *Unsupervised* screen time adds up fast.
3. The interns were working *unsupervised* all summer.

---

### unsupported

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsəˈpɔːrtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-suh-POR-tid*
**Русский:** неподтверждённый; неподдерживаемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun (*unsupported by evidence*)
**Form:** un- + *supported*
**Opposite:** *supported*

Unsupported has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not backed by evidence or facts.** (неподтверждённый, ничем не подкреплённый)

1. The claim is *unsupported* by any data.
2. The judge threw out the *unsupported* allegations.

**2. No longer maintained or made to work, said of software and devices.** (неподдерживаемый (о программе, устройстве))

3. That phone runs an *unsupported* version of the operating system.
4. The browser is *unsupported* after January.

---

### unsupportive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsəˈpɔːrtɪv/ &middot; *uhn-suh-POR-tiv*
**Русский:** не оказывающий поддержки, безучастный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsupportive*, *most unsupportive*
**Takes:** *of* + noun (*unsupportive of her plans*)
**Form:** un- + *supportive*
**Opposite:** *supportive*

Not giving encouragement or help to someone who needs it.

1. Her family was *unsupportive* when she changed careers.
2. He works for an *unsupportive* manager.
3. I do not mean to be *unsupportive*, but the numbers worry me.

---

### unsure

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈʃʊr/ &middot; *uhn-SHUUR*
**Русский:** неуверенный, не уверенный
**Type:** emotion; also quality
**Position:** predicative only — *I am unsure*; before a noun use *uncertain*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsure*, *most unsure*
**Takes:** *about* or *of* + noun; a *wh*-clause (*unsure what to do*); *whether*-clause
**Form:** un- + *sure*
**Opposite:** *sure*

Unsure has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not certain about a fact or a decision.** (не уверенный, сомневающийся)

1. I am *unsure* about the date of the meeting.
2. She was *unsure* whether to take the offer.
3. He looked *unsure* what to say next.

**2. Lacking confidence in yourself.** (неуверенный в себе)

4. He is still *unsure* of himself in front of a class.
5. She was shy and *unsure* at fifteen.

---

### unsurpassed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsərˈpæst/ &middot; *uhn-sur-PAST*
**Русский:** непревзойдённый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — also after the noun: *a record unsurpassed for forty years*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* or *for* + noun (*unsurpassed in quality*)
**Form:** un- + *surpassed*

Better than anything else of its kind.

1. The view from the ridge is *unsurpassed*.
2. She had an *unsurpassed* record in the 400 meters.
3. The shop is *unsurpassed* for repairs of this kind.

---

### unsurprising

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsərˈpraɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-sur-PRY-zing*
**Русский:** неудивительный, ожидаемый
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsurprising*, *most unsurprising*
**Form:** un- + *surprising*
**Opposite:** *surprising*
**Contrast:** The adverb *unsurprisingly* is even more common and usually opens the sentence: *Unsurprisingly, nobody came*.

Just what you would expect.

1. The result was *unsurprising* to anyone who follows the team.
2. It is *unsurprising* that prices went up.
3. She gave the *unsurprising* answer that she needed more time.

---

### unsuspecting

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnsəˈspɛktɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-suh-SPEK-ting*
**Русский:** ничего не подозревающий, доверчивый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *an unsuspecting victim*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *suspecting*

Not knowing that something bad is about to happen.

1. The scam targets *unsuspecting* older customers.
2. He walked into the room, entirely *unsuspecting*.
3. They sold the fake tickets to *unsuspecting* fans.

---

### unsweetened

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈswiːtənd/ &middot; *uhn-SWEE-tuhnd*
**Русский:** без сахара, неподслащённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *unsweetened tea*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *sweetened*
**Opposite:** *sweetened*

With no sugar or sweetener added.

1. I drink my iced tea *unsweetened*.
2. The recipe uses *unsweetened* cocoa powder.
3. Check the label, since most of these brands are not *unsweetened*.

---

### unswerving

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈswɜːrvɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-SWUR-ving*
**Русский:** непоколебимый, неизменный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *unswerving loyalty*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *swerving*, from *swerve* (to turn aside)

Never turning aside from a belief, a purpose or a person.

1. He showed *unswerving* loyalty to his old coach.
2. Her *unswerving* devotion to the work kept the project alive.
3. The company has an *unswerving* focus on safety.

---

### unsympathetic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˌsɪmpəˈθɛtɪk/ &middot; *uhn-sim-puh-THET-ik*
**Русский:** несочувствующий, чёрствый; неблагосклонный
**Type:** emotion; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unsympathetic*, *most unsympathetic*
**Takes:** *to* or *toward* + noun (*unsympathetic to the strikers*)
**Form:** un- + *sympathetic*
**Opposite:** *sympathetic*
**Contrast:** A false friend: English *sympathetic* has nothing to do with Russian *симпатичный*. It means feeling for someone, so *unsympathetic* means cold, not ugly.

Unsympathetic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not feeling or showing pity for someone in trouble.** (не сочувствующий, чёрствый)

1. The doctor was competent but *unsympathetic*.
2. She gave an *unsympathetic* shrug and went back to work.

**2. Not in favor of an idea, a group or a plan.** (не поддерживающий, неблагосклонный)

3. The court has been *unsympathetic* to these arguments.
4. Management is *unsympathetic* toward the union.

---

### untainted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈteɪntɪd/ &middot; *uhn-TAYN-tid*
**Русский:** незапятнанный, незагрязнённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun (*untainted by scandal*)
**Form:** un- + *tainted*

Not spoiled by something bad that touched everything around it.

1. He left office *untainted* by the scandal.
2. The evidence must stay *untainted*.
3. It was one of the few *untainted* wells in the area.

---

### untamed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈteɪmd/ &middot; *uhn-TAYMD*
**Русский:** неприручённый, дикий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more untamed*, *most untamed*
**Form:** un- + *tamed*
**Opposite:** *tame*

Wild, not controlled by people.

1. The island still has *untamed* forest at its center.
2. These horses are *untamed*.
3. He wrote about the *untamed* energy of the city.

---

### untapped

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtæpt/ &middot; *uhn-TAPT*
**Русский:** неиспользованный, неосвоенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *untapped potential*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *tapped*

Available but not yet used.

1. The kid has a lot of *untapped* potential.
2. Rural areas are an *untapped* market for the service.
3. The country has *untapped* reserves of natural gas.

---

### untarnished

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtɑːrnɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-TAR-nisht*
**Русский:** незапятнанный; непотускневший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun (*untarnished by the defeat*)
**Form:** un- + *tarnished*

Untarnished has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a reputation, still clean and respected.** (незапятнанный (о репутации))

1. His record is *untarnished* after thirty years on the bench.
2. Her reputation came through the trial *untarnished*.

**2. Of metal, still bright and not dull.** (непотускневший, не потемневший)

3. The silver was *untarnished* in its lined box.
4. An *untarnished* brass plate marked the door.

---

### untaxed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtækst/ &middot; *uhn-TAKST*
**Русский:** не облагаемый налогом
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *taxed*
**Opposite:** *taxed*

Not having tax charged on it.

1. The first part of the benefit is *untaxed*.
2. The state is losing money to *untaxed* online sales.
3. He kept the tips *untaxed* and off the books.

---

### untenable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtɛnəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-TEN-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** несостоятельный, невыносимый (о положении)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more untenable*, *most untenable*; often *increasingly untenable*
**Form:** un- + *tenable*, from Latin *tenere* (to hold)
**Opposite:** *tenable*

Impossible to defend or to keep going.

1. His position on the board became *untenable*.
2. That argument is *untenable* once you read the data.
3. Rising rents made staying in the city *untenable*.

---

### untested

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtɛstɪd/ &middot; *uhn-TES-tid*
**Русский:** непроверенный, неиспытанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *tested*
**Opposite:** *tested*

Untested has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not yet tried out, so nobody knows if it works.** (непроверенный, неиспытанный)

1. The treatment is promising but *untested* in humans.
2. Thousands of kits sat *untested* in storage.

**2. Of a person, not yet proven in a difficult situation.** (не проявивший себя, необстрелянный)

3. The team is starting an *untested* quarterback.
4. She is smart but *untested* in a crisis.

---

### untethered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtɛðərd/ &middot; *uhn-TETH-urd*
**Русский:** отвязанный; ничем не связанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *from* + noun (*untethered from reality*)
**Form:** un- + *tethered*, from *tether* (a rope that holds an animal)

No longer tied to anything, in fact or in a figure of speech.

1. An *untethered* balloon drifted over the field.
2. Stock prices seem *untethered* from company earnings.
3. After the layoff he felt completely *untethered*.

---

### unthinkable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈθɪŋkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-THING-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** немыслимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *It was unthinkable*
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *almost* or *simply*
**Takes:** *that*-clause (*unthinkable that she would lie*); *to*-infinitive (*unthinkable to refuse*)
**Form:** un- + *think* + -able

So shocking or extreme that you cannot imagine it happening.

1. Twenty years ago this would have been *unthinkable*.
2. It is *unthinkable* that they would close the hospital.
3. The war forced families into *unthinkable* choices.

---

### unthreatening

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈθrɛtənɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-THRET-uh-ning*
**Русский:** не представляющий угрозы, безобидный
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unthreatening*, *most unthreatening*
**Form:** un- + *threatening*
**Opposite:** *threatening*

Not frightening or dangerous, and easy to be around.

1. He has a quiet, *unthreatening* manner with patients.
2. The dog looked big but *unthreatening*.
3. They wanted a mascot that was friendly and *unthreatening*.

---

### untidy

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtaɪdi/ &middot; *uhn-TY-dee*
**Русский:** неопрятный, неубранный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *untidier*, *untidiest*; *more untidy*, *most untidy*
**Form:** un- + *tidy*
**Opposite:** *tidy*
**Contrast:** Americans usually say *messy* about a room and *unkempt* about a person; *untidy* sounds a little formal or British.

Not neat; with things left out of place.

1. His desk is permanently *untidy*.
2. She came in with *untidy* hair and muddy boots.
3. The garage got *untidier* every weekend.

---

### untied

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-TYD*
**Русский:** развязанный, незавязанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *your shoelace is untied*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *tied*
**Opposite:** *tied*
**Contrast:** Watch the spelling: *untied* (not tied) is one letter away from *united*.

With the knot loose or undone.

1. Your shoelace is *untied*.
2. He walked around all day with an *untied* apron.
3. The boat was left *untied* at the dock.

---

### untimely

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtaɪmli/ &middot; *uhn-TYM-lee*
**Русский:** безвременный, преждевременный; несвоевременный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *his untimely death*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more untimely*, *most untimely*
**Form:** -ly adjective, un- + *timely*
**Contrast:** It ends in *-ly* but it is an adjective, not an adverb. Say *The remark came at an untimely moment*, not *He spoke untimely*.

Untimely has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happening far too early, especially of a death.** (безвременный, преждевременный)

1. The article marked the tenth year since his *untimely* death.
2. An *untimely* frost ruined the crop.

**2. Coming at a bad moment.** (несвоевременный, неуместный)

3. Her joke was *untimely*, to say the least.
4. The report landed at an *untimely* point in the campaign.

---

### untiring

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtaɪərɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-TY-uh-ring*
**Русский:** неутомимый, неустанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *untiring efforts*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + noun (*untiring in her support*)
**Form:** un- + *tiring*

Working on and on without getting tired or giving up.

1. We thanked the volunteers for their *untiring* work.
2. She was *untiring* in her support of the library.
3. He is an *untiring* advocate for these families.

---

### untitled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtaɪtəld/ &middot; *uhn-TY-tuhld*
**Русский:** без названия, безымянный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *titled*

Having no title or name.

1. The gallery is showing an *untitled* painting from 1962.
2. He is working on an as yet *untitled* album.
3. The album is still *untitled*.

---

### untold

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtoʊld/ &middot; *uhn-TOHLD*
**Русский:** неисчислимый; нерассказанный
**Type:** quantity; also participial
**Position:** attributive only — *untold damage*, *the untold story*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *told*

Untold has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too great to be counted or measured.** (неисчислимый, несказанный)

1. The storm did *untold* damage to the coast.
2. The family had *untold* wealth.

**2. Never told to anyone.** (нерассказанный, неизвестный)

3. The film covers the *untold* story of the first crew.
4. Her side of it remains the *untold* part.

---

### untouchable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtʌtʃəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-TUHCH-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** неприкосновенный; недосягаемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *touch* + -able

Untouchable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Safe from punishment, criticism or being fired.** (неприкосновенный, которого не тронут)

1. For years the coach was *untouchable* at that school.
2. The program is politically *untouchable*.
3. Nobody in this company is *untouchable*.

**2. So good that no one can match it.** (недосягаемый, непревзойдённый)

4. On clay she was simply *untouchable*.
5. They built an *untouchable* lead by halftime.

---

### untouched

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtʌtʃt/ &middot; *uhn-TUHCHT*
**Русский:** нетронутый; незатронутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun (*untouched by the fire*)
**Form:** un- + *touched*

Untouched has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not handled, eaten or used.** (нетронутый)

1. She left her dinner *untouched*.
2. The money sat *untouched* in the account for years.
3. She sent back an *untouched* plate of pasta.

**2. Not affected by something that changed everything around it.** (незатронутый, не пострадавший)

4. Two blocks were *untouched* by the fire.
5. No family in the town was left *untouched* by the closing.

---

### untoward

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtɔːrd/ &middot; *uhn-TORD*
**Русский:** неблагоприятный, нежелательный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — also after *nothing*, *anything* or *something*: *nothing untoward*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Formal and slightly old-fashioned. Americans say it in two syllables, *uhn-TORD*, and most often in the phrase *nothing untoward*.

Unexpected and unwelcome, though usually not serious.

1. The nurse reported nothing *untoward* overnight.
2. The drug caused no *untoward* effects.
3. Neighbors noticed nothing *untoward* about the house.

---

### untraceable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtreɪsəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-TRAY-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** неотслеживаемый, не поддающийся установлению
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *virtually* or *effectively*
**Form:** un- + *trace* + -able

Impossible to follow back to its source or owner.

1. The payment was made in *untraceable* cash.
2. He called from an *untraceable* number.
3. The poison is virtually *untraceable*.

---

### untrained

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtreɪnd/ &middot; *uhn-TRAYND*
**Русский:** необученный, неподготовленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + noun (*untrained in first aid*)
**Form:** un- + *trained*
**Opposite:** *trained*
**Contrast:** *To the untrained eye* is a fixed phrase meaning to someone without expert knowledge.

Not taught the skills a job or task needs.

1. *Untrained* staff were left to run the shift.
2. To the *untrained* eye the two paintings look identical.
3. The dog is friendly but completely *untrained*.

---

### untranslatable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌntrænzˈleɪtəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-tranz-LAY-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** непереводимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *nearly* or *essentially*
**Takes:** *into* + language (*untranslatable into English*)
**Form:** un- + *translate* + -able

Impossible to put into another language without losing the meaning.

1. The pun is *untranslatable*.
2. She says the word is *untranslatable* into English.
3. Some jokes are nearly *untranslatable*.

---

### untreated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtriːtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-TREE-tid*
**Русский:** нелеченый; необработанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *treated*
**Opposite:** *treated*

Untreated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not given medical care.** (нелеченый, оставшийся без лечения)

1. *Untreated* high blood pressure damages the heart.
2. The infection went *untreated* for weeks.

**2. Not put through a chemical or industrial process.** (необработанный, неочищенный)

3. *Untreated* sewage was running into the river.
4. The deck is built from *untreated* pine.

---

### untroubled

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtrʌbəld/ &middot; *uhn-TRUH-buhld*
**Русский:** безмятежный, спокойный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more untroubled*, *most untroubled*
**Takes:** *by* + noun (*untroubled by doubt*)
**Form:** un- + *troubled*
**Opposite:** *troubled*

Calm, and not worried by anything.

1. He seemed *untroubled* by the criticism.
2. She had an *untroubled* childhood on the coast.
3. The lake was flat and *untroubled* at dawn.

---

### untrue

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈtruː/ &middot; *uhn-TROO*
**Русский:** неверный, ложный; неверный (изменяющий)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *That is untrue*
**Comparison:** not gradable — strengthen with *completely*, *simply* or *patently*
**Takes:** *to* + person for the second sense (*untrue to his wife*)
**Form:** un- + *true*
**Opposite:** *true*
**Contrast:** Before a noun Americans usually say *false* or *a false claim* rather than *an untrue claim*.

Untrue has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not true; not matching the facts.** (неверный, не соответствующий действительности)

1. The rumor is completely *untrue*.
2. The form warns against making *untrue* statements.
3. It is simply *untrue* that we were warned.

**2. Not faithful or loyal to someone.** (неверный, изменяющий)

4. He had been *untrue* to her for years.
5. She refused to be *untrue* to her own principles.

---

### untrustworthy

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtrʌstˌwɜːrði/ &middot; *uhn-TRUHST-wur-thee*
**Русский:** ненадёжный, не заслуживающий доверия
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more untrustworthy*, *most untrustworthy*
**Form:** un- + *trustworthy*
**Opposite:** *trustworthy*

Not to be trusted, whether a person or a source of information.

1. Voters found both candidates *untrustworthy*.
2. The site is an *untrustworthy* source for medical advice.
3. He proved *untrustworthy* with money.

---

### untruthful

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtruːθfəl/ &middot; *uhn-TROOTH-fuhl*
**Русский:** лживый, неправдивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more untruthful*, *most untruthful*
**Form:** -ful from *truth*; un- + *truthful*
**Opposite:** *truthful*
**Contrast:** A softer, more formal way of saying that someone lied: *He was untruthful about his hours*.

Not telling the truth, or not true.

1. He was *untruthful* about where he had been.
2. The application contained *untruthful* statements.
3. I do not think she is being *untruthful*, just careless.

---

### untucked

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈtʌkt/ &middot; *uhn-TUKT*
**Русский:** невправленный, навыпуск (о рубашке)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *His shirt was untucked*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *tucked*
**Opposite:** *tucked in*

Of a shirt: hanging loose instead of being tucked into pants or a skirt.

1. He showed up in jeans and an *untucked* shirt.
2. Your shirt is *untucked* in the back.
3. The dress code says no *untucked* shirts.

---

### unusable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈjuːzəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-YOO-zuh-buhl*
**Русский:** непригодный, негодный к использованию
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *usable*
**Opposite:** *usable*

Not able to be used, because it is broken, damaged or badly made.

1. The flood left the whole first floor *unusable*.
2. Without a charger the laptop is *unusable*.
3. Half the seats have such a bad view that they're basically *unusable*.

---

### unused

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈjuːzd/ &middot; *uhn-YOOZD*
**Русский:** неиспользованный; непривычный (к чему-то)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *unused to* only after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun or *-ing* in the sense 'not accustomed' (*unused to the heat*, *unused to being alone*)
**Form:** un- + *used*
**Contrast:** Two words share one spelling. *Unused* 'not used' ends in a z sound, uhn-YOOZD; *unused to* 'not accustomed to' ends in an s sound, uhn-YOOST, like *used to*. After *unused to* comes a noun or *-ing*, never a bare verb: *unused to working nights*.

Unused has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not used; still new, or never taken advantage of.** (неиспользованный, нетронутый)

1. She has three weeks of *unused* vacation.
2. The back bedroom sits *unused* most of the year.
3. Return any *unused* portion for a refund.

**2. unused to: not accustomed to something.** (непривычный, не привыкший (к чему-то))

4. *Unused* to the cold, he wore two jackets.
5. She was *unused* to being interrupted.

---

### unusual

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈjuːʒuəl/ &middot; *uhn-YOO-zhoo-uhl*
**Русский:** необычный, редкий
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unusual*, *most unusual*
**Takes:** *for* + noun with a *to*-infinitive (*It's unusual for her to be late*)
**Form:** un- + *usual*
**Opposite:** *usual*, *ordinary*

Not normal or common; different from what you expect.

1. It's *unusual* for him to call this late.
2. She has an *unusual* name.
3. There was nothing *unusual* about the car.

---

### unvaccinated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnˈvæksəneɪtɪd/ &middot; *uhn-VAK-suh-nay-tid*
**Русский:** непривитый, невакцинированный
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *against* + a disease (*unvaccinated against measles*)
**Form:** un- + *vaccinated*
**Opposite:** *vaccinated*
**Contrast:** With *the* and no noun it becomes a group noun: *the unvaccinated*.

Not having been given a vaccine against a disease.

1. *Unvaccinated* children can't enroll until the fall.
2. He's still *unvaccinated* against the flu.
3. The clinic tracks how many patients remain *unvaccinated*.

---

### unvarnished

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈvɑːrnɪʃt/ &middot; *uhn-VAHR-nisht*
**Русский:** неприукрашенный; нелакированный
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun in the 'plain' sense
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *varnished*

Unvarnished has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Plain and honest, with nothing softened or left out.** (неприукрашенный, без прикрас)

1. He gave us the *unvarnished* truth.
2. I want your *unvarnished* opinion, not the polite version.

**2. Not covered with varnish.** (нелакированный, необработанный)

3. The shelves are *unvarnished* pine.
4. We left the floor *unvarnished* on purpose.

---

### unverifiable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌnvɛrɪˈfaɪəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-vair-uh-FY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** не поддающийся проверке, непроверяемый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *verifiable*
**Opposite:** *verifiable*

Impossible to check or prove true.

1. The whole story rests on *unverifiable* claims.
2. Most of what he posted online is *unverifiable*.
3. Anonymous tips are often *unverifiable*.

---

### unverified

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈvɛrɪfaɪd/ &middot; *uhn-VAIR-uh-fyd*
**Русский:** непроверенный, неподтверждённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *verified*
**Contrast:** *Unverified* means nobody has checked it yet; *unverifiable* means it cannot be checked at all.

Not yet checked or confirmed.

1. *Unverified* reports say the plant is closing.
2. The number is still *unverified*.
3. Please don't pass along *unverified* information.

---

### unwanted

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɑːntɪd/ &middot; *uhn-WAHN-tid*
**Русский:** нежелательный, ненужный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *wanted*
**Opposite:** *wanted*

Not wanted or welcome.

1. The drug has some *unwanted* side effects.
2. She was getting a lot of *unwanted* attention at work.
3. Drop off any *unwanted* clothes by Friday.

---

### unwarranted

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɔːrəntɪd/ &middot; *uhn-WOR-uhn-tid*
**Русский:** неоправданный, необоснованный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *warranted*
**Opposite:** *justified*

Not justified by the facts or by the situation.

1. The criticism was completely *unwarranted*.
2. He called the search an *unwarranted* invasion of privacy.
3. Their fear turned out to be *unwarranted*.

---

### unwary

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɛri/ &middot; *uhn-WAIR-ee*
**Русский:** неосторожный, беспечный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *wary*
**Opposite:** *wary*, *careful*
**Contrast:** Mostly written, in phrases like *the unwary buyer* or *a trap for the unwary*. In speech Americans say *careless* or *not paying attention*.

Not watching out for danger or for a trick.

1. The scam targets *unwary* shoppers.
2. *Unwary* hikers sometimes walk right past the trailhead.
3. Small print like that is written for the *unwary* customer.

---

### unwashed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɑːʃt/ &middot; *uhn-WAHSHT*
**Русский:** немытый, грязный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *washed*
**Contrast:** *The great unwashed* is an old, snobbish way of saying 'ordinary people' — there the word is a noun, not the adjective.

Not washed; still dirty.

1. Don't eat *unwashed* berries.
2. A pile of *unwashed* dishes filled the sink.
3. His hair was *unwashed* and sticking up in back.

---

### unwavering

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈweɪvərɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-WAY-vuh-ring*
**Русский:** непоколебимый, неизменный
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *wavering*
**Opposite:** *wavering*

Staying firm and steady; never weakening or changing.

1. She has had his *unwavering* support from day one.
2. He kept an *unwavering* focus on the goal.
3. Her loyalty to the team was *unwavering*.

---

### unwed

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɛd/ &middot; *uhn-WED*
**Русский:** не состоящий в браке; внебрачный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *an unwed mother*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *wed*
**Opposite:** *married*
**Contrast:** Formal and somewhat dated, mostly in *unwed mother* and in legal writing. Everyday American English says *single* or *unmarried*.

Not married.

1. The article was about *unwed* mothers in the 1950s.
2. The form asks whether the parents were *unwed* at the time of the birth.
3. *Unwed* couples weren't eligible for the benefit back then.

---

### unwelcome

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɛlkəm/ &middot; *uhn-WEL-kuhm*
**Русский:** нежеланный, неприятный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unwelcome*, *most unwelcome*
**Form:** un- + *welcome*
**Opposite:** *welcome*

Not wanted; causing unhappiness or discomfort.

1. The bill came as an *unwelcome* surprise.
2. They made her feel *unwelcome* at the meeting.
3. It was *unwelcome* news for investors.

---

### unwell

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɛl/ &middot; *uhn-WEL*
**Русский:** нездоровый, больной
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — *She felt unwell*; before a noun use *sick*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *well*
**Opposite:** *well*, *healthy*
**Contrast:** Formal and softer than *sick*. Americans normally say *I'm sick* or *I don't feel good*; *unwell* belongs to notes, news and polite speech. It never goes before a noun: *a sick child*, not *an unwell child*.

Slightly ill; not feeling healthy.

1. She went home early because she felt *unwell*.
2. He's been *unwell* for several weeks.
3. If you are *unwell*, please stay home.

---

### unwholesome

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈhoʊlsəm/ &middot; *uhn-HOHL-suhm*
**Русский:** нездоровый, вредный; сомнительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unwholesome*, *most unwholesome*
**Form:** un- + *wholesome*
**Opposite:** *wholesome*

Bad for your health, your mind or your character.

1. The diet was cheap but *unwholesome*.
2. They worried about the *unwholesome* influence of his new friends.
3. There was something *unwholesome* about the whole arrangement.

---

### unwieldy

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwiːldi/ &middot; *uhn-WEEL-dee*
**Русский:** громоздкий, неудобный в обращении
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unwieldy*, *most unwieldy*
**Form:** un- + *wieldy*, from the old verb *wield*

Unwieldy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Big or awkward, and therefore hard to carry, move or handle.** (громоздкий, неудобный)

1. The mattress was *unwieldy* on the stairs.
2. He wrestled the *unwieldy* box into the trunk.

**2. Too large or too complicated to work well.** (неповоротливый, чересчур сложный)

3. The committee has grown *unwieldy* — twenty-two members now.
4. The tax rules are so *unwieldy* that nobody reads them.

---

### unwise

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwaɪz/ &middot; *uhn-WYZ*
**Русский:** неразумный, неблагоразумный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unwise*, *most unwise*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive (*It would be unwise to wait*)
**Form:** un- + *wise*
**Opposite:** *wise*, *sensible*

Not sensible; likely to lead to trouble.

1. It would be *unwise* to sign anything today.
2. Borrowing that much was an *unwise* move.
3. He made a few *unwise* decisions early in his career.

---

### unwitting

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-WIT-ing*
**Русский:** невольный, не подозревающий, неосознанный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *an unwitting accomplice*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *witting*, from the old verb *wit* 'know'
**Contrast:** The adverb *unwittingly* is far more common: *He unwittingly helped them*. The adjective says the person had no idea what they were part of, not that they were careless.

Not knowing what is really going on; doing something without meaning to.

1. He became an *unwitting* accomplice in the scheme.
2. The *unwitting* customers paid the fee twice.
3. She was an *unwitting* participant in the study.

---

### unworkable

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɜːrkəbəl/ &middot; *uhn-WUR-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** неосуществимый, нежизнеспособный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *workable*
**Opposite:** *workable*

Impossible to carry out or to make work in practice.

1. The plan turned out to be *unworkable*.
2. They rejected it as an *unworkable* compromise.
3. Without more staff, the schedule is *unworkable*.

---

### unworldly

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɜːrldli/ &middot; *uhn-WURLD-lee*
**Русский:** неискушённый, далёкий от житейских дел
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unworldly*, *most unworldly*
**Form:** un- + *worldly*
**Opposite:** *worldly*

Not experienced in practical life; not interested in money or status.

1. He was a gentle, *unworldly* man who never locked his door.
2. She seemed *unworldly* for someone who had traveled so much.
3. They lead a quiet, *unworldly* life on the farm.

---

### unworthy

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈwɜːrði/ &middot; *uhn-WUR-thee*
**Русский:** недостойный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb with *of*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unworthy*, *most unworthy*
**Takes:** *of* + noun or *-ing* (*unworthy of trust*)
**Form:** un- + *worthy*
**Opposite:** *worthy*

Not deserving something; not good enough for it.

1. He felt *unworthy* of such praise.
2. That kind of behavior is *unworthy* of a judge.
3. She dismissed the rumor as *unworthy* of comment.

---

### unwritten

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈrɪtən/ &middot; *uhn-RIT-uhn*
**Русский:** неписаный; ненаписанный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *an unwritten rule*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** un- + *written*

Unwritten has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not written down, but understood and followed anyway.** (неписаный)

1. There's an *unwritten* rule about taking the last slice.
2. The agreement was *unwritten*, but everyone honored it.
3. *Unwritten* office etiquette takes a while to learn.

**2. Not yet written.** (ненаписанный, ещё не написанный)

4. The last chapter of the story is still *unwritten*.
5. Their future is *unwritten*.

---

### unyielding

**Pronunciation:** /ʌnˈjiːldɪŋ/ &middot; *uhn-YEEL-ding*
**Русский:** непреклонный; жёсткий, неподатливый
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more unyielding*, *most unyielding*
**Form:** un- + *yielding*

Unyielding has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Refusing to change your mind or give in.** (непреклонный, несгибаемый)

1. The landlord was *unyielding* about the rent.
2. Their position on the deadline has been *unyielding*.

**2. Hard and not bending under weight or pressure.** (жёсткий, неподатливый)

3. The mattress was thin and *unyielding*.
4. Concrete floors are *unyielding* on your knees.

---

### up

**Pronunciation:** /ʌp/ &middot; *UHP*
**Русский:** выросший, поднявшийся; вставший; работающий; истёкший
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb (*Sales are up*); before a noun only in fixed uses: *the up escalator*, *an up year*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *down*
**Contrast:** *Up* is usually an adverb or a preposition (*stand up*, *up the stairs*). It counts as an adjective when it follows *be*, *get* or *seem*: *The market is up*, *Are you up?* Before a noun it is rare and fixed.

Up has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Higher than before in amount, level or price.** (выросший, поднявшийся)

1. Sales are *up* eight percent this quarter.
2. Gas prices are *up* again.
3. Attendance was *up* from last year.

**2. Awake and out of bed.** (вставший, проснувшийся)

4. I was *up* at five this morning.
5. Are the kids *up* yet?

**3. Working; running normally.** (работающий, в рабочем состоянии)

6. The server is back *up*.
7. The site should be *up* again by noon.

**4. Finished, of a period of time.** (истёкший, закончившийся)

8. Time's *up*!
9. Your thirty days are *up* on Friday.

---

### up in the air

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌp ɪn ði ˈɛr/ &middot; *uhp in thee AIR*
**Русский:** нерешённый, под вопросом
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after *be*, *remain* or *leave*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** idiom — *up* + *in the air*
**Contrast:** Only the idiom is an adjective; *The kite is up in the air* is literal. It means 'not decided yet', not 'in doubt about quality'.

Not decided or settled yet.

1. Our vacation plans are still *up in the air*.
2. Whether they'll move is *up in the air*.
3. The whole schedule was left *up in the air* after the storm.

---

### up to

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌp ˈtuː/ &middot; *uhp TOO*
**Русский:** на усмотрение (кого-то); замышляющий; способный справиться
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — always after *be* or *feel*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** a noun or pronoun (*up to you*, *up to the job*); *-ing* (*up to driving*)
**Contrast:** *Up to* is also a quantity word meaning 'as many as' (*up to fifty people*) and part of verbs like *keep up to date*. As an adjective it follows *be*: *It's up to you*, *He's up to something*, *I'm not up to it today*.

Up to has 3 distinct senses.

**1. up to someone: that person's decision or responsibility.** (на усмотрение, на ответственности (кого-то))

1. It's *up to* you where we eat.
2. Whether we go is *up to* the weather.
3. It's *up to* the manager to approve the refund.

**2. Doing something secret, usually something you shouldn't.** (затевающий, замышляющий что-то)

4. What are those two *up to*?
5. He's *up to* something — he's been way too quiet.

**3. Well enough or good enough for something.** (в состоянии, способный справиться)

6. I don't feel *up to* going out tonight.
7. He just isn't *up to* the job.

---

### up-and-coming

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌp ən ˈkʌmɪŋ/ &middot; *uhp-uhn-KUH-ming*
**Русский:** подающий надежды, перспективный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *an up-and-coming chef*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — always hyphenated before a noun

Becoming successful or popular, and likely to do even better soon.

1. She's an *up-and-coming* chef with two restaurants already.
2. The neighborhood is full of *up-and-coming* little places.
3. The label signs *up-and-coming* bands.

---

### up-to-the-minute

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌp tə ðə ˈmɪnɪt/ &middot; *uhp-tuh-thuh-MIN-it*
**Русский:** самый свежий, новейший
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun, often open after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — preposition phrase used as one adjective
**Contrast:** *Up-to-date* is the broader word (not out of date); *up-to-the-minute* stresses that nothing newer exists.

Including the very latest information.

1. The app gives you *up-to-the-minute* traffic.
2. We need *up-to-the-minute* numbers before the call.
3. Their storm coverage is *up-to-the-minute*.

---

### upbeat

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌpbiːt/ &middot; *UHP-beet*
**Русский:** бодрый, жизнерадостный, оптимистичный
**Type:** emotion; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more upbeat*, *most upbeat*
**Opposite:** *downbeat*, *gloomy*
**Contrast:** The noun *upbeat* is a musical term; the adjective is about mood. *Upbeat* is about how something sounds or feels; *optimistic* is about expecting good results.

Cheerful and positive in mood or tone.

1. The song has an *upbeat* tempo.
2. She stayed *upbeat* through the whole ordeal.
3. The report ends on an *upbeat* note.

---

### upcoming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌpkʌmɪŋ/ &middot; *UHP-kuh-ming*
**Русский:** предстоящий, приближающийся
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the upcoming election*; after a verb say *coming up*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Happening soon.

1. Tickets for the *upcoming* season go on sale Monday.
2. We spent the meeting on the *upcoming* election.
3. Watch your email for our *upcoming* events.

---

### upended

**Pronunciation:** /ʌpˈɛndɪd/ &middot; *up-EN-did*
**Русский:** перевёрнутый (вверх дном, набок)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *upend*

Turned over so that the wrong end or side is up.

1. An *upended* canoe lay on the bank.
2. The chairs sat *upended* on the tables.
3. We found the trash cans *upended* after the storm.

---

### upfront

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈfrʌnt/ &middot; *uhp-FRUHNT*
**Русский:** авансовый, вперёд; прямой, откровенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more upfront*, *most upfront*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing* in the 'honest' sense (*upfront about the cost*)
**Contrast:** Also written *up-front*. The money sense is often an adverb, two words: *You pay $500 up front*.

Upfront has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Paid or given before anything else happens.** (авансовый, уплаченный вперёд)

1. There's a $200 *upfront* fee.
2. The *upfront* cost is high, but you save later.

**2. Honest and direct about what you think or about the facts.** (прямой, откровенный)

3. She was *upfront* about the risks.
4. I'll be *upfront* with you: this is going to take months.

---

### uphill

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈhɪl/ &middot; *uhp-HIL*
**Русский:** идущий в гору; тяжёлый, требующий больших усилий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb *uphill* is an adverb: *The road runs uphill*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *downhill*

Uphill has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Going up a slope.** (идущий в гору, восходящий)

1. The last mile is a steep *uphill* climb.
2. We took the *uphill* route on purpose.

**2. Difficult and needing long, hard effort.** (тяжёлый, изнурительный)

3. They face an *uphill* battle in court.
4. Getting funding has been an *uphill* fight from the start.

---

### upper-class

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpər ˈklæs/ &middot; *uh-pur-KLAS*
**Русский:** принадлежащий к высшему классу, аристократический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun: *an upper-class family*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun
**Contrast:** The noun is two words with no hyphen: *the upper class*. In American English the label is about money and family, not about accent.

Belonging to the richest and highest social class.

1. He grew up in an *upper-class* suburb.
2. The novel makes fun of *upper-class* manners.
3. Her background is solidly *upper-class*.

---

### upper-level

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpər ˈlɛvəl/ &middot; *uh-pur-LEV-uhl*
**Русский:** продвинутый (о курсе); высокопоставленный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Upper-level has 2 distinct senses.

**1. More advanced, of college courses.** (продвинутый, старших курсов)

1. You need two *upper-level* electives to graduate.
2. *Upper-level* classes are much smaller.

**2. Senior; near the top of an organization.** (высокопоставленный, руководящий)

3. The memo went to *upper-level* management.
4. *Upper-level* officials refused to comment.

---

### upper-middle-class

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpər ˌmɪdəl ˈklæs/ &middot; *uh-pur-mid-uhl-KLAS*
**Русский:** принадлежащий к верхушке среднего класса
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — three words joined by hyphens

Belonging to the wealthier part of the middle class.

1. They live in a quiet *upper-middle-class* neighborhood.
2. The show is about *upper-middle-class* parents in the suburbs.
3. By any measure the family was *upper-middle-class*.

---

### uppercase

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpərˈkeɪs/ &middot; *uh-pur-KAYS*
**Русский:** прописной, заглавный (о букве)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *lowercase*
**Contrast:** Everyday speech says *capital letters*; *uppercase* belongs to typing, printing and password rules. Also written *upper-case*.

Written as a capital letter.

1. Type your last name in *uppercase* letters.
2. The first character has to be *uppercase*.
3. The sign was printed in *uppercase* type.

---

### uppermost

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌpərmoʊst/ &middot; *UH-pur-mohst*
**Русский:** самый верхний; главный (в мыслях)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the 'in someone's mind' sense only after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable — already a superlative
**Form:** *upper* + -most

Uppermost has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Highest in position.** (самый верхний)

1. A few leaves still clung to the *uppermost* branches.
2. Keep the heavy pans out of the *uppermost* cabinet.

**2. Most important in someone's thoughts.** (занимающий главное место в мыслях)

3. Safety was *uppermost* in her mind.
4. One question was *uppermost*: who paid for all this?

---

### uppity

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌpɪti/ &middot; *UH-pi-tee*
**Русский:** заносчивый, зазнавшийся, дерзкий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uppity*, *most uppity*
**Contrast:** Informal and disapproving: it says someone is acting above the place others assign them. In the United States it carries an ugly history of being used about Black people who refused to defer, so it is a word to recognize rather than to use.

Acting as if you are more important than others think you are.

1. He got *uppity* with the manager and was asked to leave.
2. They called her *uppity* for speaking her mind.
3. There's an *uppity* tone to the whole letter.

---

### upright

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌpraɪt/ &middot; *UHP-ryt*
**Русский:** вертикальный, прямой; честный, порядочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more upright*, *most upright*
**Contrast:** *Upright* is also an adverb (*sit upright*) and a noun (*an upright*, meaning a piano or a post).

Upright has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Standing straight up; vertical.** (вертикальный, прямой)

1. Please put your seat back in the *upright* position.
2. Keep the box *upright* so nothing spills.

**2. Honest and behaving by the rules.** (честный, порядочный)

3. He had a reputation as an *upright* citizen.
4. She's the most *upright* person I know.

---

### uproarious

**Pronunciation:** /ʌpˈrɔːriəs/ &middot; *up-ROR-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** уморительный; шумный, бурный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uproarious*, *most uproarious*
**Form:** -ous from *uproar*

Uproarious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely funny.** (уморительный, очень смешной)

1. The second act is *uproarious*.
2. He told an *uproarious* story about his first day on the job.

**2. Very noisy and full of loud laughter.** (шумный, бурный)

3. The joke was met with *uproarious* laughter.
4. The party got *uproarious* around midnight.

---

### upscale

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈskeɪl/ &middot; *uhp-SKAYL*
**Русский:** дорогой, элитный, для состоятельных
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more upscale*, *most upscale*
**Opposite:** *downscale*, *budget*
**Contrast:** An American word; British English says *upmarket*.

Expensive and aimed at people with money.

1. They opened an *upscale* steakhouse downtown.
2. The neighborhood has gotten much more *upscale*.
3. It's an *upscale* hotel, with prices to match.

---

### upsetting

**Pronunciation:** /ʌpˈsɛtɪŋ/ &middot; *up-SET-ing*
**Русский:** расстраивающий, огорчительный, неприятный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more upsetting*, *most upsetting*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling
**Contrast:** *Upsetting* news makes you feel bad; an *upset* person already feels bad: *The letter was upsetting* / *She was upset*.

Making someone feel unhappy, worried or angry.

1. The video is *upsetting* to watch.
2. It was an *upsetting* thing to hear from your own doctor.
3. The most *upsetting* part was that nobody called us.

---

### upside-down

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpsaɪd ˈdaʊn/ &middot; *uhp-syd-DOWN*
**Русский:** перевёрнутый вверх дном
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun (*an upside-down cake*), usually two words after a verb (*The picture is upside down*)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also an adverb: *You're holding it upside down*. The idiom *turn something upside down* means to search it or disturb it completely.

With the top part at the bottom.

1. The map was *upside-down*.
2. She made an *upside-down* pineapple cake.
3. One of the photos got scanned *upside-down*.

---

### upstairs

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈstɛrz/ &middot; *uhp-STAIRZ*
**Русский:** верхний, находящийся наверху
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the upstairs bathroom*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *downstairs*
**Contrast:** Most of the time *upstairs* is an adverb (*go upstairs*) or a noun (*the whole upstairs*). Only before a noun is it an adjective.

Located on an upper floor.

1. The *upstairs* bathroom is being redone.
2. We rented out the *upstairs* apartment.
3. Their *upstairs* neighbors play music all night.

---

### upstanding

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈstændɪŋ/ &middot; *uhp-STAN-ding*
**Русский:** порядочный, уважаемый, достойный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more upstanding*, *most upstanding*
**Contrast:** Almost always in *an upstanding citizen* or *an upstanding member of the community*; it can sound formal or lightly ironic.

Honest and respectable.

1. He's an *upstanding* member of the community.
2. The defense called her hardworking and *upstanding*.
3. They seemed like *upstanding* people.

---

### upstart

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌpstɑːrt/ &middot; *UHP-start*
**Русский:** новоиспечённый, дерзкий новичок (о фирме, кандидате)
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *an upstart airline*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Mostly a noun: *He's an upstart* (выскочка). Before another noun it means 'new and pushing in where nobody expected it', usually with a hint of disapproval.

New and ambitious, and challenging those already established.

1. An *upstart* airline is undercutting the majors.
2. The *upstart* candidate finished a close second.
3. *Upstart* studios are taking market share from the big ones.

---

### upstream

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈstriːm/ &middot; *uhp-STREEM*
**Русский:** расположенный выше по течению; начальный (в производственной цепочке)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is an adverb: *The dam is two miles upstream*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *downstream*

Upstream has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Closer to the source of a river.** (расположенный выше по течению)

1. *Upstream* towns get their water first.
2. The *upstream* side of the dam has silted up.

**2. At an early stage of a process or a supply chain.** (начальный, на раннем этапе цепочки)

3. The company sold off its *upstream* oil operations.
4. An *upstream* supplier missed a deadline and the whole line stopped.

---

### uptight

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈtaɪt/ &middot; *uhp-TYT*
**Русский:** напряжённый, нервный; зажатый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more uptight*, *most uptight*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Contrast:** Informal. It mixes anxiety with being too strict or easily shocked, so *Don't be so uptight* means 'relax, stop making rules'.

Nervous and tense, often about small things, and unable to relax.

1. He gets *uptight* about being on time.
2. Don't be so *uptight* — it's just a game.
3. She's the most *uptight* person in the office.

---

### uptown

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈtaʊn/ &middot; *uhp-TOWN*
**Русский:** относящийся к жилой (северной) части города
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *an uptown apartment*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *downtown*
**Contrast:** American usage: in New York *uptown* is the northern part of Manhattan; in many other cities it is the residential part away from the business district. It is also an adverb (*We're heading uptown*) and a noun.

In or belonging to the uptown part of a city.

1. They have an *uptown* apartment near the park.
2. The *uptown* train is running late.
3. It was an *uptown* crowd in expensive coats.

---

### upturned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈtɜːrnd/ &middot; *uhp-TURND*
**Русский:** вздёрнутый, обращённый вверх; перевёрнутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *upturn*

Upturned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Turned or pointing upward.** (вздёрнутый, обращённый вверх)

1. She has a small *upturned* nose.
2. A row of *upturned* faces watched the balloon.

**2. Turned upside down.** (перевёрнутый)

3. He sat on an *upturned* bucket.
4. *Upturned* boats lined the beach.

---

### upward

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌpwərd/ &middot; *UHP-wurd*
**Русский:** восходящий, направленный вверх
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *an upward trend*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *downward*
**Contrast:** The adjective is always *upward*; the adverb can be *upward* or *upwards* (*prices moved upward*, *upwards*). Never write *an upwards trend*.

Moving or pointing toward a higher level.

1. Home prices are on an *upward* trend.
2. There's *upward* pressure on wages.
3. The chart shows a steady *upward* climb since March.

---

### upwind

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʌpˈwɪnd/ &middot; *uhp-WIND*
**Русский:** наветренный, расположенный против ветра
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is an adverb: *Stay upwind*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *downwind*

On the side that the wind is coming from.

1. Take the *upwind* side of the fire.
2. The *upwind* boats had the advantage.
3. Smoke never reaches the *upwind* end of the camp.

---

### urban

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɜːrbən/ &middot; *UR-buhn*
**Русский:** городской
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more urban*, *most urban*
**Opposite:** *rural*
**Contrast:** *Urban* means 'of the city'; *urbane* means 'polished and sophisticated'. Different words, different stress: UR-buhn and ur-BAYN.

Relating to a city or to city life.

1. *Urban* schools face different problems than rural ones.
2. The county gets more *urban* every year.
3. She studies *urban* planning.

---

### urbane

**Pronunciation:** /ɜːrˈbeɪn/ &middot; *ur-BAYN*
**Русский:** учтивый, изысканный, светский
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more urbane*, *most urbane*
**Contrast:** Not a synonym of *urban*: an *urbane* host is smooth and well-mannered, not 'from the city'.

Polite, confident and at ease socially, in a way that suggests wide experience.

1. He was an *urbane* host who put everyone at ease.
2. Her writing is witty and *urbane*.
3. The ambassador is charming and *urbane*.

---

### urgent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɜːrdʒənt/ &middot; *UR-juhnt*
**Русский:** срочный, неотложный; настойчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more urgent*, *most urgent*
**Takes:** *that*-clause with the base verb (*It's urgent that she call us*)
**Contrast:** Russian *срочный* can also mean 'fast': an order that has to be filled quickly is a *rush order* or *express delivery*, not an *urgent delivery*.

Urgent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Needing to be dealt with right away.** (срочный, неотложный)

1. I got an *urgent* message from the school.
2. This is more *urgent* than the budget report.
3. Nothing on the list is *urgent*.

**2. Showing that you want something done at once.** (настойчивый, требовательный)

4. There was an *urgent* note in her voice.
5. He made an *urgent* appeal for volunteers.

---

### urinary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjʊrəˌnɛri/ &middot; *YUUR-uh-ner-ee*
**Русский:** мочевой, мочевыводящий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Relating to urine and the organs that make and carry it.

1. She's being treated for a *urinary* tract infection.
2. The *urinary* system includes the kidneys and the bladder.
3. *Urinary* problems are common after this surgery.

---

### Uruguayan

**Pronunciation:** /ˌjʊrəˈɡwaɪən/ &middot; *yuur-uh-GWY-uhn*
**Русский:** уругвайский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. The same word is a noun for a person (*two Uruguayans*); the country is *Uruguay*.

From Uruguay, or relating to Uruguay.

1. We went to a *Uruguayan* steakhouse.
2. The *Uruguayan* team won on penalties.
3. Her grandparents are *Uruguayan*.

---

### usable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːzəbəl/ &middot; *YOO-zuh-buhl*
**Русский:** пригодный, годный к использованию; удобный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more usable*, *most usable*
**Form:** -able from *use*
**Opposite:** *unusable*
**Contrast:** The American spelling is *usable*; *useable* is a rarer variant. Note the z sound, YOO-zuh-buhl, though the noun *use* ends in an s sound.

Usable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In good enough condition to be used.** (пригодный, годный)

1. Only half the field is *usable* after the rain.
2. Is any of this lumber still *usable*?

**2. Easy to use, of software, tools and designs.** (удобный в использовании)

3. The new site is far more *usable* on a phone.
4. They redesigned the app to make it *usable* for beginners.

---

### used to

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːst tuː/ &middot; *YOOST too*
**Русский:** привыкший (к чему-то)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after *be*, *get* or *become*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun, pronoun or *-ing* (*used to the noise*, *used to getting up early*)
**Contrast:** Two different things share this spelling. *Be* or *get used to* + noun or *-ing* means 'be accustomed to' (привык): *I'm used to working nights*. *Used to* + a bare verb is a past habit and never has *be*: *I used to work nights* (раньше работал). The classic mistake is *I'm used to work here* — say *I'm used to working here* or *I used to work here*. Both are pronounced with an s sound: YOOST.

Familiar with something because you have done it or had it often.

1. I'm *used to* the noise now.
2. It takes about a week to get *used to* the altitude.
3. She isn't *used to* driving in snow.

---

### useful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːsfəl/ &middot; *YOOS-fuhl*
**Русский:** полезный, нужный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more useful*, *most useful*
**Takes:** *for* + noun or *-ing*; *to* + a person; *to*-infinitive (*useful to know*)
**Preposition patterns:** [useful for](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#useful-for)
**Form:** -ful from *use*
**Opposite:** *useless*

Helpful for doing or getting something.

1. That's a *useful* tip, thanks.
2. A second key would be *useful* for the neighbors.
3. It's *useful* to know a little Spanish around here.

---

### useless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːsləs/ &middot; *YOOS-luhs*
**Русский:** бесполезный, никчёмный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more useless*, *most useless*
**Takes:** *at* + noun or *-ing* in the informal 'bad at' sense (*useless at math*)
**Form:** -less from *use*
**Opposite:** *useful*

Useless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not useful; of no help at all.** (бесполезный, ненужный)

1. A phone with no signal is *useless*.
2. It's *useless* to argue with him.
3. These old batteries are *useless*.

**2. Very bad at doing something — informal.** (никудышный, неспособный (в чём-то))

4. I'm *useless* at remembering names.
5. He's *useless* in the kitchen.

---

### user-friendly

**Pronunciation:** /ˌjuːzər ˈfrɛndli/ &middot; *yoo-zur-FREND-lee*
**Русский:** удобный для пользователя, простой в обращении
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more user-friendly*, *most user-friendly*
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective, hyphenated

Easy to use or understand, especially for people who are not experts.

1. The new dashboard is much more *user-friendly*.
2. We need a *user-friendly* version of the manual.
3. Their checkout process isn't very *user-friendly*.

---

### usual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈjuːʒuəl/ &middot; *YOO-zhoo-uhl*
**Русский:** обычный, привычный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun; after a verb usually with *it*: *It's usual to tip*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more usual*, *most usual*
**Takes:** *for* + noun with a *to*-infinitive (*It's usual for guests to bring something*)
**Opposite:** *unusual*
**Contrast:** *As usual* is fixed (*He was late, as usual*), and *the usual* is a noun (*I'll have the usual*). Don't mix the adjective *usual* with the adverb *usually*: *my usual seat* / *I usually sit here*.

Happening or done most of the time; normal for this person or situation.

1. We met at our *usual* table.
2. It's *usual* to tip about twenty percent.
3. She took the *usual* route home.

---

### usurious

**Pronunciation:** /juːˈʒʊriəs/ &middot; *yoo-ZHUUR-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** ростовщический, грабительский (о процентах)
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ous from *usury*
**Contrast:** Formal, mostly legal and financial. The noun is *usury*, YOO-zhuh-ree: lending money at illegally high interest.

Charging interest at an unfairly or illegally high rate.

1. The lawsuit calls the fees *usurious*.
2. Payday lenders charge *usurious* rates.
3. The contract was thrown out as *usurious*.

---

### utilitarian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌjuːtɪlɪˈtɛriən/ &middot; *yoo-til-i-TAIR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** утилитарный, практичный; утилитаристский
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more utilitarian*, *most utilitarian*

Utilitarian has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made to be useful rather than attractive.** (утилитарный, чисто практичный)

1. The kitchen is plain and *utilitarian*.
2. They chose *utilitarian* furniture that could take a beating.

**2. Relating to utilitarianism, the idea that the right action brings the most good to the most people.** (утилитаристский)

3. He made a *utilitarian* argument for the policy.
4. The debate turned into a cold *utilitarian* calculation.

---

### utility

**Pronunciation:** /juːˈtɪləti/ &middot; *yoo-TIL-uh-tee*
**Русский:** универсальный, запасной (об игроке); хозяйственный, рабочий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In *utility bill*, *utility company* and *utility room* this is the noun *utility* used as a modifier. The adjective proper means 'able to fill several roles' (*a utility infielder*) or 'plain and practical' (*utility shelving*).

Utility has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to play several positions or do several different jobs.** (универсальный, играющий на разных позициях)

1. He's a *utility* infielder who can catch, too.
2. Every crew needs one *utility* player who can cover anything.

**2. Made for plain practical use rather than for looks.** (хозяйственный, рабочий)

3. We put *utility* shelving in the garage.
4. It's a *utility* knife, not a fancy one.

---

### utmost

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʌtmoʊst/ &middot; *UHT-mohst*
**Русский:** величайший, крайний, предельный
**Type:** emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only — *of the utmost importance*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *She did her utmost*. Don't confuse it with *uppermost*, which is about position. The variant *uttermost* is literary.

The greatest possible — used to stress how much.

1. This is a matter of the *utmost* importance.
2. Handle the samples with the *utmost* care.
3. He has the *utmost* respect for her.

---

### utopian

**Pronunciation:** /juːˈtoʊpiən/ &middot; *yoo-TOH-pee-uhn*
**Русский:** утопический
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more utopian*, *most utopian*
**Opposite:** *dystopian*
**Contrast:** Capitalized when it points to Thomas More's *Utopia*, lowercase in ordinary use. It usually hints that the plan is too perfect to work.

Imagining or aiming at a perfect society, often unrealistically.

1. It's a *utopian* vision with no plan to pay for it.
2. The commune started out with *utopian* ideals.
3. Critics called the proposal *utopian*.
